# The 72-hour run, 2026-08-03 → 2026-08-06

**This file is the SPINE and the ledger `scripts/goal_guard.py` reads.** One of
its checks is `! grep -q '▢' docs/planning/queue-72h-2026-08-03.md`, so an open
row blocks the stop. That is the whole mechanism: **if every row here is `✔`, the
next item has not been WRITTEN DOWN yet** — go read `docs/planning`, find it, add
it, and get back to work.

Rows carry their reasoning and their evidence. A row is a CLAIM ABOUT THE CODE
and goes stale like any other: **re-measure before working it.** Four rows were
found stale that way in the last run, each because it was re-copied rather than
re-checked.

⛔ **AND THE COMMONEST STALENESS IS A ▢ ON WORK THAT ALREADY LANDED — three found
on 2026-08-04 in about ten checks.** B11's session owner (a sibling row said "only
safe once B11 landed" while B11 sat open), C5's binary carve (`2f9fd6085` did it
and measured it), and F6's archiving (an earlier session had already moved every
closed section to `docs/archive/`). **A row that reads DONE and is marked OPEN is
another red nobody can see past** — and unlike a wrong ✔, it costs a whole session
re-deriving something finished. ⭐ **the cheap tell: a row whose SIBLING claims it
landed.** Before working any `▢`, grep for the thing it says is missing.

⚠ the same shape one level up: `check_agent_kb.py` and the workspace job are
STANDING reds, so their output stops being read — which is how the policy suite
hid a fresh failure for two days and how a new inline-test debt hid inside an old
one. **A job's verdict is not its first test target's verdict.**

The previous ledger is [`queue-72h-2026-07-31.md`](queue-72h-2026-07-31.md).
It is **not superseded** — its open rows are carried in Lane F by POINTER, never
by copy.

---

## ⇥ START HERE

```bash
python3 scripts/goal_guard.py --status          # confirm the run is armed
git status --short                              # row zero, below
sed -n '/## Lane A/,/## Lane B/p' docs/planning/queue-72h-2026-08-03.md
```

**Row zero — ✔ closed by another session, and the way it closed is the warning.**
Two files sat modified at 22:38 on 08-02 (24 NPC `dialogue_id` fields in
`hall_of_characters_area.ron` and the level generated from them). While this
spine was being written they were committed by **another agent session** as
`153eee0c0`, which is now the parent of this file's own commit.

⚠ **THIS TREE HAS CONCURRENT SESSIONS.** Three `claude` processes were running at
02:51 UTC on 2026-08-03, two of them older than this one, and `main` moved
underneath a turn. That is not hypothetical damage: the 63-minute suite run on
08-02 outlived its own tree when a second session regenerated sprite assets 23
minutes in, and two jobs failed with `include_str!` errors on files that existed
before and after but not during. So during this run:

* **re-read `git log` before assuming HEAD is where you left it**, and never
  interpret a file you did not change as your own edit;
* a long build or suite run may be reading a tree that is being rewritten —
  prefer the narrow command, which is also Lane C's whole argument;
* ⛔ **never `git checkout --` a file to undo a probe.** It deletes the
  uncommitted fix you were probing, and here it may delete another session's
  work as well.

**Lane order, and why:** A is first because Jon asked for a visible win at the
top of the run. B is second because a review's findings decay — the tree moves
under them, and six of its thirteen are already confirmed against source below,
so the cheap ones should be taken while that verification is still true. C is the
run's ARCHITECTURE headline and everything after it is cheaper once it lands. D
and E are the product work, and three of D's rows are architecture wearing
gameplay clothes. Lanes are a priority ORDER, not a schedule: a blocked lane is
abandoned for the next row, never written up and waited on.

## Two standing instructions Jon added mid-run (2026-08-03), verbatim

> *"Make sure you sweep up old 'I did this and its done' docs every once in
> awhile. It also might be a good idea to rnn the full set of tests and then fix
> any errors that have accumulated when you get to the nexte new task point."*

- ▢ **Sweep stale "done" prose periodically.** A row that finished is evidence
  for about a day and clutter afterwards. This spine is written to carry
  reasoning, so the sweep is not "delete history" — it is: a ✔ row whose argument
  no longer teaches anything gets cut to its one durable sentence, and a ✔ row
  whose CLAIM has gone stale gets corrected or deleted, because a wrong ✔ costs
  more than a missing one. ⚠ the counts in this file have gone stale twice
  already (F7 said `287 passed / 0 failed` while `app_it` was 284/3).
  ▢ **next target: `docs/planning/status.md`** — a "HEAD status" audited
  2026-07-18/19/27, so a week and two 72-hour runs stale, whose "Current hard
  work" table is mostly rows that say **DONE** in a paragraph. They belong in a
  one-line closed list so the table shows what is OPEN. ⚠ **constraint found
  before editing**: `check_roadmap_evidence.py` flags a MET row citing NOTHING
  and treats a backticked name as a claim that it exists — so each compressed row
  must keep one live backticked citation, and a dead name must be plain prose.
- ▢ **Interleave architecture and feature work, and WRITE AHEAD IN THE WORKTREE
  while tests run** (Jon, 2026-08-04, for the overnight stretch): *"as you do the
  Overnight work interleave architecture and feature work. use a worktree to work
  ahead while tests run."*
  ⚠ **this names a real waste, not a preference.** A single agent that starts a
  build and then waits for it spends most of a long run blocked: an
  `ambition_platformer2d_actor_monolith` test build is ~3 minutes and `app_it` is
  ~4, and this session serialised on dozens of them. The worktree
  (`/home/agent/code/ambition-workahead`, branch `workahead`) exists so the NEXT
  edit is being written while the current one is being proven.
  ⛔ **never BUILD in the worktree** — Jon's standing call; a second target dir is
  a cold duplicate on a volume that has hit 100% three times. Write there, merge,
  build on `main`.

- ◐ **At each new-task point, run the full suite and fix what has accumulated.**
  ✔✔ **2026-08-04: 4/4 JOBS PASSED — the first fully green sweep in this run's
  records** (640s, 36% executing). The previous sweep was 3/4 with the workspace
  job red, and the four causes were: two `BlockStruck` registrations, a fighter
  fixture that could not stage its own question, and the workspace-policy suite —
  which had been red since 08-02 behind a job verdict nobody read past.
  ⚠ **the rule that came out of it, and it cost me a false all-clear**: a job's
  verdict is not its first test target's verdict. I reported three reds fixed
  after reading `app_it`'s result line; the job was still failing on
  `engine_policies`, in the same output, further down.
  ✔ **first pass done 2026-08-03: 3/4 jobs green, and all THREE accumulated reds
  are now fixed.** `BlockStruck` was two of them — the message-channel oracle
  (stale reader CURSOR) and the resource sweep (stale BUFFER), two instruments
  asking different questions of one subject, each now carrying its own argument.
  ⭐⭐ **the third was not a defect in its subject at all.**
  `the_shadow_model_agrees_with_the_real_sim_about_what_lands` had been red for
  days saying *"the imagination is lying hard enough that rollout arg-max is
  noise"* — and the tell was in its own printout: two of four cases ARRIVED

  ⚠ **third instance of this class in three days** — the Sanic backdrop, the

  ▢ **the original claim, kept**:

  (20 passed / 1 failed), *"she must take the wand off the ?-block through the

  The test now prints the room's collectibles beside her position, and the answer

  ```
  after bonk: pos (277.8, 356.2) airborne     items: []
  mount chunk 0: her 232.0  items: []
  mount chunk 3: her 560.0  items: []   ← parked on FIRST_PIT_SAFE_X
  mount chunk 5: her 560.0  items: []
  ```
  Not "the wand launched right into a pit at y=670", not "an authored wall at
  x≈207 she cannot pass", not "a patrol that never jumps" — **the ?-block never
  put a collectible in the room at all.** Three recorded diagnoses, all of them
  about where the wand went, and the wand was never there.
  ⭐ **the reason nobody caught it is that the failure message describes HER**
  (*"worn form is `Some("mary_o")`, wears star_wand = false"*), so every reader

  no assertion had ever asked for it. Same shape as the two fixture rows above:
  **the thing that had to be true for the question to mean anything was never

  ⛔⛔ **ANSWERED, and it is upstream of the pickup path AND of the wand:
  `block0 SPENT = false` — SHE NEVER STRIKES THE BLOCK.** So the reward spawn
  never runs, which is why the room holds no collectible, which is why she never

  ⭐ **and the bonk beat cannot report this**: it is time-boxed and asserts only

  ⚠ **D3's `SpawnedThisAttempt` is ruled out** — nothing was spawned to despawn.
  ✔ **ANSWERED — she jumps EARLY and sails clean over the block.** Instrumented
  her position while horizontally overlapping `power_block_0`: **under it for 35

  `ContactKind::Head` is impossible — there is nothing wrong with the strike
  detection, the box, or the reward path.
  ⚠ **two of my own measurements on the way were the wrong measurement**, which is
  worth more than the answer: (1) `apex_y` over the whole 400-frame drive is not a
  jump height — she can jump from elevated ground, so it reads 108.5 and means

  writing it — her live `MotionModel` carries Mary-O Classic exactly
  (`run_accel 393.75`, her own PhasedGravity thresholds), so that was wrong too.
  Only "her head, while under the block" answers the question that was asked.
  ⭐ **and her authored jump clears that row by design**: `jump_speed 450`,
  `gravity 2250`, `held_rise_gravity_scale 0.2` gives roughly a 145 px rise from a

  HELD jump goes straight past it. The beat holds jump every frame. **A held
  classic jump overshoots a row-4 block; hitting one is a SHORT hop.**
  ✔ **both repairs landed**: the beat now holds jump only until her HEAD reaches
  the underside (steering off the measurement, not a frame count — a bare tap
  undershoots by 15 px, so neither "hold" nor "tap" was right), and it asserts

  `super_mary_o_star_wand` spawns at `x = 483`.
  ⛔⛔ **and the FIRST recorded diagnosis was right all along — it just could never
  be reached.** The wand travels RIGHT and falls into the pit
  (`y 400 → 488 → 1132 → 4294`), exactly as the original note said. It is

  300. Probed by lifting the fixture's safe clamp to 620 — she arrives at 622 by
  frame 200 and the wand is already at `y = 1132`.
  ▢ **BLOCKED ON JON as decision #15** (level authoring: move the block, give the
  wand a wall to turn on, or accept it). ⚠ the test stays red and that is now a
  FAITHFUL red — everything upstream of collection is proven working in the same

  [[feedback_ask_if_a_red_test_is_new_first]] of the STRIKE specifically before

  ⭐ **the reusable lesson, and it is the fifth instance today**: the failure

## The rules of this run

- **Never stop early, never ask a question instead of working.** Pick the safest
  interpretation, execute, record the assumption in the commit message.
- **Never write a completion-shaped summary while time remains.** That prose is
  the artifact that ended a 24-hour run at 84 minutes. Report when Jon asks.
- **Never invent a budget.** No "I've spent enough on this."
- **Commit completed work unprompted** — the stall counter reads COMMITS, not
  turns. Uncommitted work looks like no work.
- **`cargo check -p ambition_app` is the compile gate**, never a per-crate check.
- **`./run_tests.sh` is the backbone and is no longer the hour-long sweep.** The
  exhaustive plan is `--run-everything-you-probably-dont-need-this` and the name
  is the instruction. Narrower is better when a focused test covers the touched
  concept. See `AGENTS.md`.
- **⛔ An LLM review will ask for compliance machinery. The answer is no**, and
  `AGENTS.md` says it without hedging. Lane B applies that rule to a live review
  rather than restating it.

## Lane A — the programmatic vanity card (FIRST)

**Jon, 2026-08-03:** *"I would like cherry picking and integrating changes from
that into the main branch to be the first task in the long run, which is a nice
visual change that helps me feel like we are making progress."*

The payload is `ambition_programmatic_vanity_card_overlay_20260726.zip` at the
repo root — six files, dated 07-26, so it **will not apply cleanly** and is not
meant to. The point of the change: **a vanity card drawn programmatically, with
no dependency on the ipfs-located rendered frames.**

Today's card is nine generated images plus a committed manifest
(`game/ambition_content/src/vanity_card.rs` → `vanity_card_frames()`), composed
at startup by `game/ambition_app/src/app/shell_host.rs:203`, with the payload
under `assets/vanity_card/` behind `assets/vanity_card.ipfs`.

- ✔ **A1. Ported (2026-08-03).** The drift was smaller than the zip's six files
  suggested: every shell API it needs — `ShellSegmentSpec::registered`,
  `ActiveShellSequence::registered_segment`, `ShellSegmentScopedEntity`,
  `ShellSequenceCommand::ProgrammaticSegmentCompleted`, `ShellSegmentRole::Vanity`
  — still exists unchanged. What HAD drifted:
  * the facade was renamed (`ambition::` → `ambition_platformer2d::`), so its
    Cargo.toml and shell_host.rs are pure rename noise plus the loss of Smash,
    the causal feature, the Android target block and the `test = false` bins.
    **Applying either file would have reverted four landed changes.**
  * `BorderRadius` is **not a Component** in Bevy 0.18 — it is a `Node` field.
    14 spawn sites, moved mechanically.
- ⛔ **A1b. The zip's own test could not pass, and the reason is the interesting
  part.** It asserted the drawn card's length equals the authored card's, with
  the drawn card's five beat constants summing to **3900 ms against an authored
  3902** — false the day it was written, by an amount no reviewer would spot, and
  the manifest is REGENERATED so any absolute copy drifts again on the next
  render. The beats are now relative WEIGHTS scaled to the authored total, with
  the last beat taking the remainder, so the equality holds by construction. The
  test that replaced it asserts the property integer division can actually break:
  the five beats reconstitute the whole card with nothing left over.
- ✔ **A2. Placed beside its siblings** in `game/ambition_content/src/presentation/`
  with `deep_dream` and `dialog`, reached through the facade path the crate
  already depends on, so **no new dependency edge and no engine crate pays for
  it** (`engine.host-names-no-content` keeps them off this crate anyway).
  `game/ambition_content/src/presentation/` already holds `deep_dream.rs` and
  `dialog.rs`; its `mod.rs` says content-owned looks layered onto the renderer's
  public seams belong there, and `engine.host-names-no-content` keeps engine
  crates off this crate, so nothing under `crates/` pays for it.
  ⚠ **Jon's condition, 2026-08-03:** *"as long as we don't tangle the modules too
  badly we can figure out how to untangle later"* — so no new crate, and the card
  must not reach into anything it would be painful to lift back out. Honoured:
  the module's only engine contact is the shell segment contract.
- ✔ **A3. LOOKED AT — and looking found two things a compile could not.**
  There is no display in this environment, so the preview binary could not run
  and `capture_scene --route ambition_startup` was the instrument. It reported
  **`unknown route 'ambition_startup'`**: the startup run-in is composed by the
  game binary, not by `build_visible_app`, so **the first surface a player sees
  was the one frontend surface this tool could not photograph.** Composed now
  when — and only when — that is the route being captured, since composing it
  also makes startup the INITIAL route.
  Then, photographed at four beats, the card drew a **stray black rectangle**:
  a "shadow" pinned 18px from the ROOT's top-left while the panel it shadows is
  CENTRED, so at 1280x720 it sat 137px left and 65px above its panel. Deleted
  rather than realigned — drop shadows are not this project's look.
  ⚠ also deleted: the panel's caption read *"A programmatic shell-startup card
  preview"*, a note about the implementation that would have shipped in front of
  every boot.
  ⚠ **noticed, not chased:** the touch overlay's Menu/Back buttons and a jump
  button draw OVER the startup card in this capture. Whether that reaches a
  desktop player is unchecked — it is a capture-composition question, not a card
  one. Written down rather than folded into this change.
- ⛔ **A3b. AND THE FIRST PORT WAS WRONG, which the pictures are what caught.**
  Jon: *"it looks like the agent that built it flaked out and didn't use the real
  art. I don't want what just landed as a fallback, I don't like it."* He was
  right about the mechanism: the zip reproduced the CHOREOGRAPHY exactly — its
  five beat constants are the config's 0.7/1.0/0.5/0.5/1.2 — and drew rounded
  rectangles with two eyes each instead of the characters. **It ported the pacing
  and dropped the art**, and a compile could never have said so.
  ⚠ **and I was looking at the wrong pipeline.** `tools/vanity_card_prep` composes
  six keyed PANEL photographs; the real card is
  `tools/ambition_sprite2d_renderer/scripts/render_author_vanity_dialog.py` — the
  game's own `player_robot_v3` and an author paper-doll rig, IK, 80 frames.
  Jon: *"you might be looking at the wrong gif path."*
- ✔ **A3c. Python owns the motion, the engine owns the drawing (2026-08-03).**
  Jon's steer: *"you can use python to generate hard coded placements if Python
  uses ik. the rust code for this can be fairly dumb and even generated as long
  as it's fast."* `export_author_vanity_card.py` runs the real timeline through
  the real solver and bakes where every part landed; the engine reads the table
  and places quads. No skeleton, no solver, no animation system in the engine for
  one screen, and re-timing is a Python re-run.
  * `render_frame`'s actor construction was extracted to `compose_actors` so the
    export and the GIF share ONE definition of where everybody is — proven
    pixel-identical before anything was built on it.
  * ⭐ **`--verify` re-composites every baked frame and diffs it against the
    renderer, and it earned itself immediately**: the first run said 85% of the
    silhouette was wrong, which was the VERIFIER — `render_frame` paints the card
    backdrop, so the reference silhouette was the whole canvas while the bake was
    correct. **A check that fails on everything teaches nothing.** With the
    backdrop lifted, all 80 frames agree to 2–3%, which is edge resampling.
  * costs, because the card is the first thing the game shows: **one** texture
    load (41 parts in a single 288 KB sheet, fewer than the nine frames it
    replaced), **4.5 ms measured** to parse 3200 placements once, ~40 node
    transforms per frame.
  ⚠ **stated plainly**: the sheet is a `*.png` and therefore git-ignored like
  every binary payload, so this does NOT make the card play in a fresh clone.
  What it removes is the ipfs dependency and the second authority.
- ✔ **A5. Named after its joke** (`vanity_card_made_this_meme`, Jon 2026-08-03) —
  "vanity card" is the CATEGORY and more are expected from other groups. The
  segment KIND string moved with it, which is what lets a second card be
  scheduled as an ordinary segment.
- ✔ **A4. One card is composed, so there is no bifurcation.** The host no longer
  builds an image sequence; the authored manifest survives as the card's TIMING
  authority and as the artefact the drawing stands in for. Deleting the payload
  (`assets/vanity_card/`, `assets/vanity_card.ipfs`, `tools/vanity_card_prep`) is
  a maintainer call — distribution is Jon's — and is not required for the card to
  work in a fresh clone, which was the point.

## Lane B — the GPT review of `5cc4337..47d7de3`, triaged against the tree

Every claim below was checked against source on 2026-08-03 before being accepted
or refused. **Do not re-derive the verification; do re-check any row you are
about to work, because the tree moves.**

| # | claim | verdict, with the evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 2 | `--combat-overlay` parser hangs | ✅ **CONFIRMED** `capture_scene.rs:410-413` sets two flags, never advances `i`; the sibling arm at `:414` does |
| 3 | overlay wired only into room capture | ✅ **CONFIRMED** `force_combat_overlay` at `:304`, absent from the route builder at `:744-745` |
| 4 | press completion only after a final tap | ✅ **CONFIRMED** the reset lives inside the `press_held.take()` branch `:1108-1133`; `Hold`/`Release`/`Wait` fall to `press_cursor += 1` and return |
| 8 | preserved arrival leaves the sweep at zero | ✅ **CONFIRMED** `arrive_body_in_room` → `reset_body_clusters` → `transit_body(TransitVelocity::Zero)`, then only `kinematics.vel` restored |
| 9 | emergence overshoots the authored rise | ✅ **CONFIRMED** `item_motion.rs:241-245` applies a full `dt/seconds` fraction on the last tick |
| 1 | moving items outside rollback authority | ◐ **registrations CONFIRMED absent**; severity ASSERTED, not shown |
| 6 | `Empowered` lifecycle is half-wired | ◐ **half CONFIRMED** — Sanic registers `SuperFormLatch` and not `Empowered`; removal needs a second call |
| 5 | the flag keeps unrelated dev chrome | ◐ real, one line of intent |
| 10 | the set-pin guard is schedule-blind | ◐ hazard real, remedy refused |
| 11 | decorative `CoreSimulation` edges | ◐ direction right, method refused |
| 12 | the archive cannot run its own validation | ◐ half accepted |
| 7 | `Empowered` cannot compose two sources | ⛔ **NO** — pre-generalization |
| 13 | `git diff --check` in the validation path | ⛔ **NO** |

### The rows

- ✔ **B1. The four one-liners are in (2026-08-03).** #2, #3, #5 and #4 landed
  together in the capture tool (`1418b0381`) and #9 separately (`6cd93427c`).
  ⭐ **the finding is not the missing `i += 1`** — it is that the command the
  recent work DOCUMENTED could not have been run even once. The fix is
  structural rather than the missing line: the match now RETURNS how many
  arguments each arm ate and the loop advances, so an arm that forgets is a type
  error instead of a hang.
  ⭐ **and the review missed half of #4**: `press_wait` was not in the loop's
  active condition either, so a trailing `wait:N` was never counted down at all.
  A step type that silently did nothing when it happened to be last.
  ⚠ #9's test uses 0.13s against a 1/60s step ON PURPOSE — the existing rise test
  uses 0.4s, which is exactly 24 ticks and green either way.
  ⚠ honest about the probe: the parser hang is proven by the loop's structure and
  by the fixed version running, not by executing the broken binary to watch it
  spin.
- ✔ **B2. #8 is fixed (`40962b94b`), and it was the best find in the review.** `arrive_body_in_room`'s own
  docstring exists to kill *"an authority that requires a follow-up call"* — and
  the ritual regrew INSIDE it, one level down: the sweep is rebuilt at zero and
  only `kinematics.vel` is put back. Assert the whole arrival — `pos`, `vel`,
  `sweep.prev`, `sweep.curr`, `sweep.vel` — at the wrapper, because
  `transit_body` alone proves nothing about `Preserve`.

  `_clone_checksum` looked strictly better: a one-field bool whose only probe is

  must NOT be CHECKSUMMED** — `_clone` plus a named entry in the exit oracle's

  ⭐⭐ **SECOND ONE TRIAGED AND IT IS A REAL DEFECT — the sweep's THIRD.**
  `ambition_platformer2d_shared_tangle::lifecycle::round::ActiveRoundScope` holds
  `current: Option<RoundScopeId>` and **`next_raw: u64`, a deterministic id
  allocator**. `settle_versus_round` takes `ResMut` of it and calls `begin()`, and

  shipped host, **inside the rollback window**.
  ⛔ so a rewind across a round boundary re-ran the mint against a `next_raw` that
  had never rewound: the resimulated timeline allocates a DIFFERENT
  `RoundScopeId` from the one it is reproducing, and `RoundScopedEntity` culls
  entity lifetime by exactly that id. Entities spared on one timeline and
  despawned on the other.
  ⭐ **the comment beside that registration already knew the shape**: *"the
  restored score depended on presentation-frame history that resimulation does not
  replay. Calling a system 'the presentation half' does not make the resource it
  writes presentational."* Somebody fixed the score by moving the system INTO the
  sim schedule — and the round scope that system writes never followed.
  ✔ registered as `resource.active_round_scope`; ceiling 25 → 24, and the
  staleness assert fired a THIRD time in one day.
  ⚠ **and registering it took THREE tries, each caught by a different seam — the
  rollback vocabulary is not a menu you pick from.**
  1. `rollback_resource_clone` → the exit oracle refused it: a clone registration
     yields a PRESENCE-ONLY probe, and this resource is always present once a
     match exists, so a presence probe would report "still there" across exactly
     the rewind that allocated a different id. *"A presence probe satisfies the
     coverage test above while seeing nothing of the value."*
  2. `rollback_resource_canonical` → **eight oracle tests went red**, because it
     installs a checksum system taking `Res<T>` that panics on any frame the
     resource is ABSENT — and `RoundScopePlugin` is installed by whatever composes
     a MATCH, so the calibration lab has no round scope at all. ⛔ the method's own
     doc says this in the paragraph above it and I picked it anyway.
  3. `rollback_resource_optional_canonical` → correct: checksums `Option<T>`, so
     "no match yet" and "round 0 is live" are different values rather than one
     being unrepresentable.
  ✔ and the schema baseline moved by exactly ONE additive row, nothing removed —
  which is what a new registration should look like and what that test exists to
  confirm.
  ✔ two more cleared by reading, both legitimately unaccounted:
  `SimulationReplayState` is HOST-owned (*"a rollback host raises it after loading
  historical state"*) — the flag that tells systems they are being rewound, and
  the one thing a rewind must not rewind; `SessionGatedSimulation` is a
  zero-sized marker inserted once by the host composition.
  ⭐⭐⭐ **AND ITS TWIN IS INNOCENT, which is the sharpest thing this whole sweep
  has produced.** `lifecycle::session::ActiveSessionScope` is *structurally
  identical* to `ActiveRoundScope`: `current: Option<SessionScopeId>`,
  `next_raw: u64`, a `begin()` that mints and increments, and an id that entity
  lifetime is culled by. **One is a rollback defect and the other is not.**
  The only difference is where its mutators are registered:
  | | mutator | schedule | verdict |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `ActiveRoundScope` | `settle_versus_round` | `app.sim_schedule()` | INSIDE the window — real defect |
  | `ActiveSessionScope` | `translate_shell_session_lifecycle`, `despawn_retired_session_entities` | literal `Update` | outside — legitimately unaccounted |
  ⛔ **so no type-shaped lint could have found this.** Both types look the same,
  both names read the same, and a checker over shape or naming would either flag
  both (crying wolf on the innocent one) or neither. What decides it is a fact
  about SCHEDULE REGISTRATION, one indirection away from the type. That is why the
  remaining 22 want reading rather than a rule.
  ⚠ **and this is the THIRD instance of one recurring conditional** — after
  `InventoryRestored` and B7's `.before(CoreSimulation)` pins. *"Registered in
  literal `Update`, therefore outside the rollback window"* was recorded here as
  true only because the shipped host is `Ggrs`, with the warning that under
  `RenderFrame` *"`Update` IS the sim schedule and every one of these judgements
  inverts"*.
  ✔✔ **THAT WARNING IS RETIRED — the class is UNCONDITIONAL (2026-08-03).** A
  `RenderFrame` host does not have a rollback window for `Update` to be inside:
  `PlatformerEnginePlugins::build` adds `AmbitionRollbackPlugin` **only** when
  `host.is_ggrs()`, and `register_app_descriptor` downgrades every registration to
  `RecordedOnly` off that host. No GGRS, no snapshots, no session — nothing to
  rewind. So a resource written only by systems in literal `Update` is never
  rollback state **in any composition**, rather than in the shipped one.
  ⭐ **and the conditional is now ENFORCED rather than observed**, which is what
  makes it safe to lean on: `AmbitionRollbackPlugin::build` asserts its host is
  `Ggrs` and refuses to install otherwise, probed in both directions. Before that
  assert, a composition COULD have paired `Update`-as-sim-schedule with a live
  GGRS session, and this whole class of judgement would have been quietly wrong
  in it. `tests/sim_phase_pins.rs` still measures where `CoreSimulation` actually
  is, which is the separate question of whether a `.before` pin bites.
  ⭐ **A REPEATABLE TRIAGE emerged from the pair, and it cut the remaining work
  down fast.** The discriminator is not the type — it is *is this resource taken
  as `ResMut` by a system registered in `sim_schedule()`, and if so, is it
  OVERWRITTEN or ACCUMULATED?*
  * **12 of the 18 remaining names are never taken as `ResMut` in production at
    all** — art manifests, hotkey bindings, the shell-hosted marker, the camera
    entity handle, the quasar shader pair, `FlagPole`, `BallDashTuning`, the two
    room-transition loading flags, the provider catalog registry. Nothing writes
    them after setup, so there is nothing for a rewind to disagree about.
  * of the 6 that ARE mutated, `FallingSandProjectionReport` is the one whose
    mutator runs in the SIM schedule — the same shape that made `ActiveRoundScope`
    a defect.
  ✔ **and it is safe, for a reason worth stating precisely**: it is `*report = …`
  on both paths, wholly overwritten every tick and never accumulated, with no
  reader between the write and the next rewrite. That is DERIVED state, so it is
  now `declare_rollback_derived_resource`d rather than waived — a stronger and
  more checkable claim than "it's a diagnostic". ⚠ a memo that GATED behaviour
  would not have qualified, which is the line this repo already draws.
  ✔ ceiling 24 → 23, and the staleness assert fired a fourth time.
  ✔ **THE TRIAGE IS COMPLETE. All 23 remaining are clear, and every one for the
  SAME structural reason.** The last five mutated names, with their schedules:
  | resource | mutator | schedule |
  |---|---|---|
  | `SmashSelect` | `present_the_select_screen` | `Update` |
  | `ScreenOccupancy` | `collect_screen_occupancy` | `PostUpdate` |
  | `PreparedPlatformerSessions` | `cleanup_prepared_platformer_sessions` | `Update` |
  | `PlatformerStreamingReadiness` | `update_streamable_packed_sfx` | `Update` |
  | `AudioEnvironment` | `detect_audio_environment` | `Update` |
  None is in `sim_schedule()`, and the other 12 are never taken as `ResMut` in
  production at all.
  ⭐⭐ **so the whole remaining ceiling rests on ONE fact**: the shipped sim runs
  in `GgrsSchedule`, therefore anything mutated only from `Update`/`PostUpdate` is
  outside the rewind window. Not 23 independent judgements — one, applied 23
  times.
  ⛔ **AND THAT IS EXACTLY WHY THE CEILING STAYS AT 23 RATHER THAN BECOMING 23
  WAIVERS.** The safety property is about SCHEDULES, and the waiver list matches
  on NAMES. A name-based waiver for these modules would swallow the next resource
  added to any of them — including one whose mutator is registered in the sim
  schedule, which is the exact defect `ActiveRoundScope` was. A COUNT cannot do
  that: a 24th appears immediately, whatever it is called.
  ⭐ **the ceiling is the right instrument for this population precisely because
  a waiver list is the wrong one.** That is worth knowing before someone
  "finishes the job" by driving it to zero.
  ⚠ and the one fact it rests on is measured, not assumed: `tests/sim_phase_pins.rs`
  asserts `Update` holds ZERO `CoreSimulation` systems against 242 in
  `GgrsSchedule`, and fails the day that inverts.
  ✔ **the sweep's final scorecard: THREE real defects** — `BrokenBricks`,
  `SpentMonitors`, `ActiveRoundScope` — every one found by reading a single name,
  and one derived declaration (`FallingSandProjectionReport`).

- ✔ **B7. #11 — per-edge evidence, and the answer is KEEP ALL NINE.**
  `CoreSimulation` is configured in `app.sim_schedule()`, which is a HOST CHOICE:
  `Update` for `RenderFrame` (the **default**), `FixedUpdate` for `Fixed60Hz`,
  `GgrsSchedule` for `Ggrs`. So the pin's meaning depends entirely on which
  schedule the pinning system was added to, and the census splits clean:
  * **six** are added to `sim` itself — the two gravity pins, the two runtime
    pins, the portal carve set, and a test fixture — so they are real in every
    host by construction;
  * **one** (`publish_ggrs_input`) is in `GgrsSchedule`, which only runs under the
    host where that IS the sim schedule, so it is real by exclusivity;
  * **three** are added to a literal `Update`: `platformer2d_host/lib.rs:371`
    (which already documented itself), `ambition_app/app/plugins.rs:423`, and
    `menu/grid_backend.rs:1141`. These are #11's entire true content.
  ⭐ **and they are still keepers**, because `RenderFrame` is the DEFAULT host —
  deleting them breaks the ordering in every fixture and demo shell that does not
  opt into rollback. Both undocumented ones now carry the measurement inline.
  ⚠ **measured, not modelled** (`tests/sim_phase_pins.rs`, on the shipped app):
  `Update`'s CoreSimulation node holds **0** systems against **242** in
  `GgrsSchedule` — an empty husk that exists only because those three pins name
  it. The test asserts both halves, so if the sim ever moves into `Update` the
  three pins become load-bearing and it says so that day.
  ⛔ **the probe nearly produced a spectacular false finding**: Bevy builds a
  schedule's graph LAZILY, on first run, so `GgrsSchedule` — which had not run in
  a shell-hosted no-window app — reported 415 systems and *no `CoreSimulation` at
  all*. Read literally that says the sim is unconfigured under rollback. The fix
  is one `initialize` call, and the lesson is the standing one: a first reading
  that indicts something huge is measuring the instrument.
- ✔ **B8. Hermetic (`88f8ceed6`), probed with `.goal/` moved away entirely.** A TRACKED test that reads an untracked
  `.goal/active.json` is a defect — build the fixture in the test. ⛔ no "archive
  validation mode".

### Refused, with the reason (so the next round is short)

- ⛔ **#7, source-keyed empowerment grants.** Nothing stacks two sources today:
  Mary-O's star is timed, Sanic's form is held, no body holds both. Write the
  policy down — insertion replaces, one grant slot — with a test that says so,
  and build the aggregate when a second concurrent source exists. Building it now
  is proving a hypothetical, which the engine direction forbids.
- ⛔ **#13, `git diff --check` in the validation path.** The whitespace is in
  `JONS_OBSERVATIONS_BUGS_AND_ISSUES.md`, a file Jon types prose into, and
  `AGENTS.md` makes formatting advisory rather than a gate.
- ⛔ **the reflexive "add a test" on one-line fixes** whose behaviour is already
  covered. Take the three that would have caught the actual bug.

- ✔ **A6. The title music no longer restarts on the way to the menu**
  (2026-08-03, Jon's report). Two things were wrong and the second is why the
  first took three attempts.
  ⭐ **The state could not answer the question.** `MusicPlaybackState` was one
  field — `active_track: String` — so a transition that stops the theme and
  starts the identical file leaves exactly the state it found. It now carries
  `play_generation`, bumped on every start and stop, with private fields so the
  counter cannot be forgotten. Asked directly, the handoff reported **3 → 6**:
  silence, silence, play, twice over, from two different systems.
  ⛔ **two rejected rule shapes, both measured false rather than argued away:**
  "previous owner and next owner are both frontends" is false at exactly the
  moment it is asked, because the audio owner passes through `None` between
  activations; and a per-route "continue what the last screen played" flag makes
  continuity a property of the PAIR of screens. What survives the gap is the
  PROFILE, so `title_theme_keeps_playing` asks whether the declared title track
  is already playing and whether the incoming owner is gameplay.
  ⚠ **the review question Jon asked, answered:** Wwise and FMOD ship capture
  logs and Unreal has trace channels, but that is a debugging surface for a whole
  audio department. *Did this song restart* is a fact about the STATE, and the
  state now answers it. If audio ever earns a timeline it belongs in
  `ambition_causal` as a domain — **not a second observability authority**.

## Lane C — test iteration cost (Jon's headline, carried from S40)

The plan is [`test-iteration-cost-2026-08-02.md`](test-iteration-cost-2026-08-02.md)
and it owns the detail. **Measured once, 2026-08-02: 3776s, ~7% of it executing
tests, 1858 compile events, the actor monolith compiled 16 times, +77 GB.**

✔ **Landed 08-02, do not redo:** the default is the backbone, the exhaustive plan
is behind `--run-everything-you-probably-dont-need-this`, `AGENTS.md` rewritten,
six never-caught-anything guards deleted. That is half of Front 3.

- ✔ **C0. Front 0 landed (`0cede5766`).** `dev/run_tests_cost.jsonl` is appended
  every run; the status file carries `current_job`, `current_started` and the

  reports the compile/run split, totalled from the "finished in Xs" line every
  libtest binary already prints — no extra build, no `--timings` HTML, live
  output preserved.
  ⚠ the regex was checked against REAL libtest output, because the crates handy
  for a smoke test execute in hundredths of a second and "0.0" is
  indistinguishable from "no match" there.
  ⭐ its first numbers make the campaign's case in miniature:
  `ambition_platformer2d_core` is **2.7s of wall clock and 0.01s of tests**.
  ⚠ **and running the python guards afterwards found two of them RED, both mine**
  — the rollback schema ratchet (two new central names) and a stale MODULES.md.
  Same class as the 08-02 lesson: run the documented suite before committing a
  change that touches a registration or a module shape. Per-job timings
  by default; the status file carries the CURRENT job, its start time, and the

- ◐ **C1. Front 1 — compile the graph once, then run everything in it.**
  ⭐ **the premise is MEASURED, not assumed: the union resolves.**
  `cargo check --workspace --features <55 entries>` — the union of every feature
  job's extra features, package-qualified — **exits 0**. That was the open
  question: 23 feature jobs today, each its own build graph, and whether cargo
  can express all of them at once was never checked.
  ⭐ **AND THE DIFFERENCE IS SHOWN, which is what the plan insisted on.** The
  union run (`cargo test --workspace -F <union>`, **5m54s wall / 31m of CPU**)
  produced 63 failures. Classified, every one of them:
  * **56** — my own duplicate `Empowered` registration, fixed in `528d761ae`. A
    defect that exists only where two content crates MEET, so `-p demo_sanic`,
    `-p demo_mary_o` and `cargo check -p ambition_app` all missed it.
  * **4** — the baseline and value-probe consequences of the new registrations
    (`8764ea198`).
  * **3** — PRE-EXISTING and not mine: `duel_arena` ×2 and the exit oracle's

  * ⭐ **2 — UNION-ONLY**, and these are the evidence:
    `rollback_exit_oracle::every_gameplay_message_channel_is_rewound_on_rollback_or_named`
    and `rollback_coverage::every_mutable_ambition_resource_is_registered_derived_or_waived`
    **pass in the default resolution and fail in the union**, because extra
    features add message channels and resources the default graph never
    compiles. The union does not merely cost less — it SEES more, and adopting
    it means accounting for those channels first rather than discovering them
    later.
  ⚠ **and the union job cannot simply replace the per-crate jobs as-is**: cargo
  stopped at the first failing target, so one red crate hides every later one.
  `--no-fail-fast` is required, and that is a property of the plan, not a detail.
  ◐ **the restructure is IN and running its first measured pass.** `build_jobs`
  now emits a `cargo check -p <crate> --all-targets --features <extra>` per
  combination (the compile proof, metadata-only) plus ONE
  `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast --features <union>` that runs every
  gated test in one graph.
  ⭐ **MEASURED, from C0's ledger rather than by hand:**
  | lane | cost |
  |---|---|
  | 23 compile proofs (`cargo check`) | **468s total, 20.3s mean** |
  | the union test job — every gated test, one graph | **339.5s wall, 126.8s executing (37%)** |
  | (for scale) `workspace (default features)` | 349.0s wall, 110.1s executing |
  ⭐ **808s for what used to be 23 separate feature TEST jobs**, each of which

  integer is required, not dict`. A runner whose `--help` crashes is a runner
  nobody asks — which is exactly how the exhaustive sweep stays the reflex, the
  precise thing this front exists to stop. Fixed, plus three stale "~1 hour"
  claims that are now ~25 min.

  ⭐ **the two most-edited crates in the repo were not among them.** By source

  `ambition_app` (276). The two that DO have overrides are `ambition_render` (131)

  |---|---|---|
  | opt-1 (baseline) | 2.57 / 2.67s | 32.4s |

- ✔ **C4. Front 2 — a parallel runner cannot help, and finding out why relocated
  the whole cost.** Cargo takes a build lock per target dir so concurrent cargo
  serializes anyway, and libtest is already parallel: swept, the curve is FLAT
  after 4 threads (1→136.1s, 4→72.6s, 8→67.3s, 16→68.6s), a 2.0× ceiling. So
  `cargo-nextest` would buy process ISOLATION (which would have caught E1's
  shared-clock flake structurally) and nicer reporting, not speed — an easier
  decision than "is it faster", and it needed no install.
  ⭐⭐ **the durable finding is why the ceiling is 2×: ONE TEST is 33% of the
  subset and four are 63%** (`a_player_death_reset_survives_the_rollback_window`
  alone is 44.3s of 134s serial). A 44.3s test inside a 67s parallel run means the
  subset cannot go below ~44s on any number of cores — the "serial half" is a
  single test on the critical path, not a lock or an I/O bottleneck. Boot is
  **370 ms**, not the 2.7s that 67s ÷ 25 suggested.
  ⛔ **three wrong theories were built against that average before the
  distribution was read**: an LDtk parse cache (built, measured, bought nothing,
  reverted — LDtk is under 1% of a boot), a boot-cost theory, and C2's *"the cost
  is what each test pays to exist"*, retracted here. **An average over a skewed
  population reads exactly like a per-item cost and is not one, and the
  distribution was two commands away the whole time** — now written into
  `AGENTS.md` as the "get the DISTRIBUTION before theorising" recipe.
  ⭐ and the heavy test is slow for an ENGINE reason: 2400 frames under GGRS
  sync-test at `check_distance = 4`, linear in the distance. Superseded by the

---

- ◐ **F7-duel. MEASURED (2026-08-03): a fighter that FLIES can never shield.**
  The test prints both fighters, and they are asymmetric with identical
  capabilities:
  ```
  PCA:   shield_frames=535  dash=0   fly=194  fly_toggles=4   blinks=3
  robot: shield_frames=0    dash=30  fly=427  fly_toggles=12  blinks=1
  ```
  ⭐ **the cause is in the decision's own preconditions.** In
  `brain/smash/mod.rs`, arming the block requires `cfg.can_shield &&
  obs.self_on_ground`, and HOLDING it requires `shield_hold_timer > 0.0 &&
  obs.self_on_ground`. The robot is airborne for 427 of ~600 frames, so the
  second layer of the layered defense (blink → shield) is unreachable for it —
  not chosen against, *unreachable*. Nothing says so at the decision site.
  ⚠ **likely fallout from the 08-02 frame-typing fixes** (`5ae87b2e8`,
  `3e7780623`): the fighter used to write raw world x into the body-local
  `locomotion`, so its movement — and therefore the closing speeds and the flight
  decision that reads them — changed when that was corrected. The test's
  expectation was calibrated against the buggy movement.
  ▢ **this is a design call, not a repair**, and it belongs to Jon: (a) let a
  fighter shield in the air, (b) make a perceived threat ground a flying fighter
  when shield is the chosen layer, or (c) accept that a flyer's defense is blink
  and change what the test asserts. ⚠ (c) is the cheapest and the one most likely
  to be wrong — "the defensive game is layered" is the design, and a flyer losing
  a layer silently is the finding.

- ✔ **F7. CLOSED 2026-08-03 — by the FEATURE lane, not this one.** The two duel
  tests had been red since before this run and were parked on decision #9 (can a
  flying fighter shield). ⭐⭐ **they were fixed by Jon's fireball complaint**:
  `dispatch_move_events` re-samples the aim at a ranged move's fire frame,
  `frame.fire` is an edge cleared every tick, and the fallback was a bare
  `(1.0, 0.0)` resolving to world RIGHT — so every ranged moveset move fired right
  whichever way its body looked, and in a duel that faces two fighters at each
  other one of them shot away from its opponent for the whole fight. **This is the
  case for interlacing**: a gameplay complaint closed tests the architecture lane
  had not moved in two days, because the lane was looking at shields and the bug
  was in aim.
  ⚠ **decision #9's measurement stands and is no longer urgent.** `shield_frames`
  535 vs 0 between two fighters with identical capabilities is still true — arming
  and holding a block both require `obs.self_on_ground` and the robot is airborne
  427 of ~600 frames, so its layered defence is UNREACHABLE rather than unchosen.
  The design question survives on merit; it blocks nothing.
  ⚠ **the count this row carried (`287 passed / 0 failed`) was STALE and is
  corrected here** — `app_it` measured 2026-08-03 at **284 passed / 3 failed**,
  none of them the duel pair: `BlockStruck` (named since), the shipped-composition
  resource sweep, and `fb6_shadow_fidelity`. A ✔ row carrying a wrong number costs
  more than an open one, which is the whole argument for sweeping these.
- ✔ **D1. Dormant actors — DESIGNED, BUILT, and the first landing's own bug found
  and fixed.** Worked out while the suite held the build
  lock (2026-08-03).** Jon: *"We probably need an engine concept that allows
  actors to be dormant… this sort of optimization will likely be generally
  important for any game using the engine, although it's not something that
  should be inherent."* The symptom: AI slop walks off the level before Mary-O
  ever arrives.
  ⚠ **`BossBrain::Dormant` is NOT this.** It exists, and it is an AUTHORED tag
  for a boss that starts asleep until triggered — content vocabulary, not a
  runtime optimization. Reusing the word without reusing the concept would be
  the confusion this row starts with.
  ⭐ **the wake rule must not name the player**, which is also what makes it
  correct for split screen and netplay — the two cases Jon flags. Not *"near the
  player"* but **"near any OBSERVER"**: a body is awake if any active viewpoint
  is within its policy's range. One rule covers one player, four on a couch, and
  a remote peer, and it never mentions a protagonist. (Core value: relativity
  over player-centrism.)
  ⭐ **dormancy is a POLICY the actor declares**, per Jon's "not inherent": a
  `Never` variant is what a character that must keep simulating asks for, and
  the engine assumes no distance at all without one.
  ⭐ **it should skip the BRAIN, not the body.** The reported defect is an actor
  ACTING off-screen; a body with no control input already stands still, while
  freezing the body would leave a falling enemy hanging in the air and make
  dormancy visible. Minimum correct scope, and the cheapest.
  ⚠ **rollback**: whether a body is awake is DERIVED from positions, so it must
  be recomputed after a restore rather than stored — or, if stored for cost,
  registered. A memo that gates behaviour is rollback state
  ([[reference_a_derive_memo_is_rollback_state]] in the agent's memory, and the
  07-31 ledger's own row).
  ✔ **BUILT (`e740f41c1`).** `DormancyPolicy::{Never, AwakeNearObservers{radius}}`
  declared per actor and absent by default; `assess_dormancy` recomputes a
  `Dormant` marker from live positions each tick, chained before
  `tick_actor_brains`, which gains `Without<Dormant>`.
  ⭐ the couch case is a TEST, and it is the one a "near the player" rule cannot
  express: seat one far away, seat two beside the actor, actor awake — because
  somebody is there, not because the protagonist is.
  ⚠ no memo by construction, so there is no derived-state-that-is-really-rollback
  -state to get wrong; the sweeps confirm it by having nothing to say.
  ⛔ **AND THE FIRST LANDING DID NOT ACTUALLY STOP THEM — found by reading, hours
  later, and fixed.** `ActorControl` is a COMPONENT, not a per-tick message:
  `update_ecs_actors` writes it only for actors it ticks (`Without<Dormant>`), and
  `integrate_sim_bodies` reads it for EVERY sim body with no dormancy filter at
  all. So a sleeping actor does not have "no control input" — it has a STALE one,
  and the body keeps integrating it. **A slop that fell asleep mid-stride walked
  that direction forever, which is precisely the reported symptom.**
  ⚠ **the module doc asserted the opposite** (*"a body with no control input
  already stands still"*) and the first test asserted only that the POLICY WAS
  ATTACHED — true, and useless: the actor was correctly marked dormant and kept
  walking. That is the "green through its own motivating case" failure in its
  purest form, and it passed a red-probe check because the probe tested the
  attachment too.
  ✔ going dormant now RETRACTS the brain's last word, on the transition; the doc
  says why; and the new test asserts what the actor DOES. Probed red.
  ✔ **content: LANDED, radius DERIVED not guessed (2026-08-03).** Mary-O's AI
  slop declares `AwakeNearObservers { radius: AI_SLOP_WAKE_RADIUS }` in the same
  tag pass that gives them their `AiSlop` marker — the engine's spawn vocabulary
  stays free of a per-character decision, and any future staging path inherits it.
  ⚠ **probed red first**: with the insert removed the new guard FAILS, so it is
  not green through its own motivating case. The number was
  the one thing this row said wanted a play test. It does not — it wants the view
  size, which is a setting with five documented values:
  | preset | world view | half-width |
  |---|---|---|
  | Tight | 640×360 | 320 |
  | Combat (default) | 800×450 | 400 |
  | Arena | 960×540 | 480 |
  | Cinematic | 1120×630 | **560** |
  | Debug | 1600×900 | 800 |
  A wake radius smaller than the half-width of the view a player picked pops an
  actor into frame already moving. So the floor is Cinematic's **560** — the widest
  view that is a play choice rather than a developer's — and **720** adds 160px
  (5 tiles at Mary-O's `T = 32`) so an actor has settled before it is visible.
  ⚠ **Debug's 1600 is deliberately NOT covered**: a developer zoomed to twice the
  gameplay view is looking for exactly this kind of thing, and sizing all content
  for it would make the policy cull nothing.
  ⭐ **and 720 does cull the motivating case.** Level 1-1's AI slop sit at columns
  13/24/53/70 (`T = 32` ⇒ 416/768/1696/2240 px) plus one per stair step near the
  flagpole, in a level spanning 104 columns / 3328 px, with the player spawning
  at `2 * T` = **x=64** (`lib.rs:642`). At 720 the near pair is awake and everything from x≈800 out —
  including every stair slop — is asleep until she approaches. That is precisely
  Jon's report: the far ones walked off the level before she arrived. *"We probably
  need an engine concept that allows actors to be dormant… this sort of
  optimization will likely be generally important for any game using the engine,
  although it's not something that should be inherent."* AI slop walks off the
  level before Mary-O arrives. ⚠ **the non-inherent half is the design**: split
  screen and network multiplayer break a camera-proximity rule, and some
  characters never sleep. Dormancy is a POLICY an actor declares, not a distance
  the engine assumes — and it is rollback state the moment it gates a brain.
- ⛔ **D2. MY CLAIM WAS WRONG TOO — the moment IS named. Traced 2026-08-03.**
  Jon: *"the enemies seem to reset before the death animation is finished. The
  level reset needs to happen all at once at a time that is easy to express."*
  I wrote that `RoomReplayRequested` is "a bare marker whose consumer drains it,
  so every subsystem resets whenever it happens to read the message". Reading the
  chain end to end says otherwise:
  * ONE consumer — `apply_room_replay_request_system` in
    `ambition_platformer2d_runtime/src/sandbox_reset.rs`;
  * `RoomReplaySchedulePlugin` ANCHORS two content slots before it, and
    `ContentRoomReplayResetSet` is where content-named per-attempt state resets,
    so the engine consumer names no content;
  * it then writes ONE `ResetRoomFeaturesEvent`, which is the only thing that
    writes it apart from the manual reset — snakes and every other feature key
    off that;
  * enemies with `RespawnPolicy::OnRoomReenter` write no death flag, so they
    return with the room reconstruction rather than on a reset of their own.
  ⭐ **and Mary-O's death already defers the request**, with a comment saying
  exactly why: `restart_level_after_death` fires *"on the frame the dwell runs
  out and never on the frame the death lands — a replay in the same frame as the
  death would cancel the beat it is supposed to follow"*, and the animation timer
  is driven from the same `sequence.remaining`.
  ✔ **PROBE RUN 2026-08-03, and it settles the row: the reset is gated, the WORLD
  IS NOT.** `death_reset_timing::the_room_resets_no_earlier_than_the_death_beat_ends`
  drops her in a pit (the door a player uses, not a hand-written
  `ActorDiedMessage`) and records per frame:
  | frame | beat `remaining` | non-player body signature |
  |---|---|---|
  | f163 | 0.517 | 37188 |
  | f180 | 0.233 | 37158 |
  | f194 | ~0 | 37134 |
  | f195 | 0.0 | 37131 |
  | **f196** | 0.0 | **36720** ← the room reset |
  ⭐ **the reset lands on f196, one frame AFTER the beat ends on f195** — so the
  gating the chain describes is real and D2's earlier reading was right. But the
  17 non-player bodies **move on essentially every frame of the ~0.55 s dwell**,
  drifting 37188 → 37131, and only then snap. **To a player that reads exactly as
  "the enemies reset before the death animation finished": they were walking the
  whole time, and then jumped.** Jon's symptom is real and its cause is the
  opposite of its description — nothing resets early, nothing HOLDS STILL.
  ⭐ **and that is what *"the level reset needs to happen all at once"* is asking
  for.** It is also what SMB1 does — the level freezes on death and only the
  death animation plays — which is the convergence target for Mary-O.
  ▢ **the fix is an ARBITRATION design, not a two-liner, which is why it is not
  in this commit.** The beat would set the world clock to zero and run its own
  dwell on the unscaled clock. But `ambition_time::ClockState` is a bare
  `time_scale: f32` — **one scalar with no claimant set** — so a death beat
  writing 0 clobbers hitstop, bullet-time and pause, and whichever writes last
  wins. ⭐ the engine already solved this shape once, next door: `Invulnerability`
  is a REASON BIT SET precisely so a scripted beat and a beacon can both hold it
  without clobbering, and the death beat already uses it
  (`Invulnerability::SCRIPTED`). **A `time_scale` with the same reason-set shape is
  the row**, and it is an engine seam rather than a Mary-O fix — every game gets
  "a scripted beat holds the world still" for free, and hitstop stops being a
  thing that can be silently overwritten.
  ⚠ **the probe asserts only the ORDERING and prints the rest.** Pinning
  "the world keeps moving" either way would be a regression test over an unmade
  design decision, which `AGENTS.md` forbids; the ordering is a real invariant the
  chain claims and now cannot lose quietly.
  ⚠ **second wrong diagnosis of the session** (see D10). Both came from reading
  one file and generalising. The pattern to break: trace the chain to its
  consumer BEFORE writing the row, not after.

- ✔ **D3. FIXED (`1f9100c3f`), reproduced first.** See the row's detail below.
- ✔ **D3-detail. Diagnosed and then fixed (2026-08-03).**
  Jon: *"When I reset the level old drops from enemies still seem to be there."*
  ⭐ **enemy drops are spawned SESSION-scoped, not room-scoped**
  (`features/ecs/damage/actor_hit.rs:536`, `spawn_session_scoped`, and the boss
  path at `damage/boss_hit.rs:311` does the same). A room reset despawns every
  `RoomScopedEntity` — the drop is not one, so it survives, exactly as reported.
  ⚠ **and the obvious fix is wrong.** Session scope is not a mistake: a weapon
  dropped in one room and still there when you walk back IS the intended
  behaviour for Ambition, and room scope would delete it on a plain transition.
  The real defect is that ONE scope has to answer two different questions — *does
  this survive leaving the room* and *does this survive REPLAYING the room* — and
  a replay is not a transition. That is the same seam D2 is about: the reset is
  not expressible as one thing, so each subsystem answers it separately.
  ✔ **done that way**: `SpawnedThisAttempt` names what a run produced and the
  reset despawns it beside the post-boss NPCs — a replay lifetime distinct from
  the transition, rather than a re-scope. The rule was already there for
  in-flight enemy volleys ("they belong to the previous attempt"); the loot was
  simply not in it.
  ⚠ reproduced BEFORE the fix: a coin spawned in the room survived
  `ResetRoomFeaturesEvent`, asserted as a failing test first.
- ✔ **D3b. The original report — the SAME item as D3/D3-detail, closed there

- ◐ **D4. Hall of characters: humanoids are dramatically out of scale.** The

  `collision_scale`, which **is** `frame_height / body_pixel_bbox.h`

  (`BodySource::SpriteAuthored { world_per_pixel }`) is reachable only through a
  `CharacterDefinition`, and **the 140-row catalog does not produce definitions**
  — `register_character` is called by the smash roster, the versus fighters and
  the player-robot lineage, and by nothing that walks the catalog. A definition
  folds the catalog IN; a catalog row alone never becomes one. So the recipe that
  fixed v3 works for definition-backed characters and not for the Hall.

  ⭐ **what a Hall NPC's on-screen size actually is**, via
  `sprite_body_collision_for_character_id_from_data`:
  `authored LDtk spawn box × collision_scale × (body / frame)`.
  **Two of those three are per-character guesses, and the one quantity a viewer
  actually compares — how TALL this character is — is authored nowhere.** That is
  why they are mutually out of scale: nothing in the pipeline ever states a
  height, so nothing can be consistent about one.

  ⭐ **48.0 is the PROTAGONIST, not a plausible number.** `player_robot_v3`

  ⚠ **that changes what the design IS.** At 52 of 140 the default reaches 37% of

  ⚠ **and NO row states a height of its own** — `standing_height` appears zero

- ◐ **D5. THE SEAM DID NOT EXIST FOR HER KIND OF TARGET (fixed 2026-08-03),

- ◐ **D6-Sanic. TRACED (2026-08-03): the mechanism is COMPLETE, so this is not
  a missing seam.** Jon: *"When SANIC is hit, there it seems like he is given no
  iframes. He should also have some hitstun and be knocked back a bit."*
  The chain, read end to end: a badnik's `contact_strength` → `HitEvent` with
  **`HitMode::Knockback`** (`actors/update.rs:844`) → `resolve_player_hit` →
  `WalletShielded` (which is why rings scatter at all) → and that arm explicitly
  does `knock_off_ledge` + `apply_player_knockback`, with the comment *"The
  shield prevents HP loss and death, not the ordinary physical reaction… without
  making positive currency a no-recoil invincibility exploit."* The default feel
  is substantial too: knockback **360/260**, hitstun **0.24s**, knockback
  invulnerability **0.75s**.
  ⭐ so the question is not "add hitstun" but **"where does the applied reaction
  go?"**, and the ranked candidates are:
  1. **Sanic's locomotion model eats the impulse.** He runs `SurfaceMomentum`,
     which owns velocity from its own speed state; an impulse written into
     `kinematics.vel` is lost the next tick if the model recomputes rather than
     integrates. This is the same class as the ladder thread's answer — *a body
     under knockback obeying physics with an input that is losing to momentum* —
     and it would explain "no knockback" while every value is non-zero.
  2. **Presentation**: the reaction happens and nothing SHOWS it (no stun pose,
     no flash), so it reads as nothing at 60fps.
  3. values overridden for this demo — cheapest to rule out, so do it first.
  ⭐⭐ **(1) IS THE ANSWER, and the kernel's own doc comment says so
  (2026-08-03).** `SurfaceBody::vel` is documented as *"World velocity.
  Authoritative while `Airborne`; **DERIVED** (published for observers) while
  `Riding`."* While Sanic is riding a surface — which is every moment a badnik
  can touch him on the ground — the authoritative state is the scalar `v_t` along
  the tangent, and `vel` is an output. `apply_body_reaction`
  (`damage_apply.rs:847`) does `*vel = knockback_velocity(...)`. It writes the
  OUTPUT. Next tick the model republishes `vel` from `v_t` and the impulse is
  gone, having never existed as far as the body was concerned.
  ⭐ **verified by a third, independent route**, which is the rule this repo keeps
  learning: `ball_dash.rs` — Sanic's OWN demo code — writes `v_t` when it wants to
  change his speed, and its module doc says so in the first line. The demo already
  knows which channel is authoritative; the shared knockback does not.
  ⚠ **so this is an AUTHORITY defect, not a feel one**, and the numbers were never
  the problem: knockback 360/260 and hitstun 0.24s are applied faithfully to a
  field nothing reads. Same family as B2's arrival momentum and as
  "an authority that needs a follow-up call" — the write goes to the published
  mirror instead of the state that owns it.
  ⭐⭐ **THE SEAM ALREADY EXISTS, and reading the kernel bridge is what found it.**
  `movement/kernel.rs:233`, immediately AFTER `step_surface_body` returns:
  ```rust
  if let Some(impulse) = touching_rebound_aabb(ctx.world, clusters.kinematics.aabb()) {
      surface_momentum::apply_pad_impulse(ctx.world, &mut body, impulse, ctx.dt);
  }
  clusters.kinematics.vel = body.vel;
  ```
  A rebound pad is an external impulse that must survive a ride, and it is applied
  in exactly the right place with exactly the right operation — `apply_pad_impulse`
  decomposes into the ride frame and, when the impulse leaves the surface, sets
  `body.vel` AND `motion = Airborne`, "keeping the momentum perpendicular to the
  impulse axis". A spring launching a running body is the same problem as a hit
  launching one. **Knockback is the only external impulse that does not go through
  it.**
  ⚠ **but it is not a drop-in, and the difference is a rule not a tuning value.**
  A pad's tangent-dominant case only replaces `v_t` *"when that makes the body
  faster"* — a booster declines to slow you. A hit must not decline: knockback
  along the run overrides it. So the operation wanted is a sibling,
  `apply_external_launch`, that shares the frame decomposition and drops the
  faster-only guard.
  ✔ **BUILT (2026-08-03): a launch CHANNEL, drained by the one movement gateway.**
  * `BodyFlightState::pending_launch` — a world-space launch a reaction imparted,
    not yet handed to the model. ⭐ **it rides on that cluster because that
    cluster is ALREADY the world-imparted momentum channel** (`carried_run`'s own
    doc says "written by the portal adapter and by knockback"), and it is already
    rollback-registered as `body.flight`, so a pending launch rewinds with
    everything else and needed NO new registration. The snapshot macro refused to
    compile until the new field declared how it encodes, which is the schema seam
    working exactly as designed.
  * `surface_momentum::apply_external_launch` — the sibling of
    `apply_pad_impulse`, differing by a RULE: a pad replaces `v_t` only when that
    makes the body faster (a booster declines to slow you); a hit must not
    decline, or being shoved while sprinting does nothing.
  * `step_motion` drains it for every model, BEFORE the step so the launch lands
    this tick. ⛔ **one drain, on purpose** — the alternative is every reaction
    calling the model after writing the velocity, which is the
    authority-that-needs-a-follow-up-call shape, and whose failure mode is a
    launch that silently does nothing. Exactly this bug, again.
  ⭐ **the old behaviour is PINNED as a test**, not just fixed:
  `writing_velocity_on_a_riding_body_does_not_launch_it` asserts that a velocity
  write is republished away while riding. If someone later "simplifies" the
  channel back into a velocity write, that test is what says no.
  ⚠ **the crawler keeps its attachment** — whether a hit peels an adhesive body
  off its surface is Jon's open puppy-slug question, and answering it as a side
  effect of routing knockback would have been the wrong way to decide it. Its
  velocity still takes the launch.
  ✔ **BOTH ENDS of the seam are now tested**, which is the part a channel can get
  wrong that a single function cannot — each half passing while the two never meet.
  * writer (`damage_apply`): a hit PUBLISHES its launch and it equals the velocity
    written, so the two channels cannot disagree about what the hit did; a hit
    with no knockback publishes nothing, so a damage-only hit never clears a ride.
  * reader (`kernel`, through `step_motion` itself, where production enters): a
    pending launch takes a rider OFF the floor, and the channel is EMPTIED by the
    step that consumes it — with the tick after asserting gravity is pulling the
    climb back, so a one-shot written into persistent state cannot re-fire forever.
  ▢ **still owed: Jon seeing it.** The mechanism is proven end to end in tests; the
  SYMPTOM is not signed off until a badnik actually knocks Sanic about on screen.
  ⚠ do not close the report on the strength of the tests — the diagnosis was
  triangulated three ways and was still a claim about mechanism, not about feel.
  ⚠ **do not add a second hitstun mechanism to the demo.** That is the
  bifurcation this repository names first, and the reaction is already applied by
  the shared kernel.

- ◐ **D6-MaryO. The rest of the feel cluster, and ONE of its five items is now
  explained by D6 rather than by feel.** Sanic gets no i-frames, hitstun or
  knockback on hit and his rings barely splash; super-Sanic is not invincible to
  spikes; Mary-O is hit during her death animation; her downgrade is instant where
  it should be a beat. These are one seam (hit response →
  stun/i-frames/knockback, already the authoritative kernel
  `ambition_platformer2d_core::hit_response`), not four bugs. Ring recollection
  after hitstun is a real design constraint — they must outlive the stun.
  ✔ **"Sanic gets no knockback" is CAUSED and REPAIRED** — see D6: the launch was
  written to `BodyKinematics::vel`, which is a derived mirror for a riding
  surface-momentum body, so it was republished away before anything read it.
  ⚠ **and that reframes two of its neighbours rather than fixing them.**
  `hitstun_timer` and the i-frame timers are written to `BodyCombat`, which no
  motion model derives — so they were almost certainly ALWAYS applied, and "no
  i-frames, no hitstun" is more likely *nothing visibly happened, so all three
  read as absent* than three independent failures. ⭐ **that is worth knowing
  before anyone tunes them**: a timer that is running and invisible does not want
  a bigger number, it wants a reaction the player can see.
  ⛔ **STALE, corrected 2026-08-05: three of those four have LANDED.** This row
  went on listing them as untouched while the commits sat in the log, which is
  the staleness this ledger's own header names.
  * super-Sanic vs spikes — `4a60273ed` *"The same spikes behaved differently
    depending on how they were drawn"*
  * Mary-O hit during death — `658fad962` *"Her death beat makes her
    untouchable, and gives the body back"*
  * the instant downgrade — `6297dad7d` *"An armor-absorbed hit gets its beat"*,
    plus `transform_beat_policy`
  All three are struck through in `JONS_OBSERVATIONS_BUGS_AND_ISSUES.md` already,
  so this row disagreed with the file it was tracking.
  ▢ **what actually remains is the RING SPLASH alone**, and it is Jon's line 6
  there, un-struck: *"The rings don't splash out nearly large enough. He needs an
  opportunity to recollect some of them after his hitstun wears off and before

  recollection WINDOW — and the second is the one a magnitude tweak will not

- ✔ **D7. FOUND, and FIXED (`692a3d027`) — an engine DEFAULT became a policy.**
  Jon: *"Maryo coins and sanic rings should not be magnetic to the player."*
  `features/ecs/pickups.rs::magnetize_pickups` pulls **every** uncollected pickup
  toward the body at `PICKUP_MAGNET_RANGE = 130.0` px at `PICKUP_MAGNET_SPEED =
  340.0` px/s. Two hardcoded constants, applied to all loot in every game.
  ⛔ **and its query is `With<PrimaryPlayer>`** — the engine reaching for THE
  player, which is the first core value inverted ("avoid player-centrism, value
  the principle of relativity"). On a couch it means loot flies at seat one.
  ⭐ the fix is the same shape as D1: **magnetism is a policy a pickup DECLARES**
  — a `PickupMagnet { range, speed }` component, absent by default, so Mary-O's
  coins and Sanic's rings simply stop being magnetic without a per-game opt-out
  — and the attractor is *a body that can collect*, not the primary player.
  ⚠ `PickupCollectLock` already exists for the related reason (a tossed ring
  must not be "reeled straight back… the instant it spawns on top of the
  player"), which is the same complaint one step earlier and connects to D6's
  *"he needs an opportunity to recollect some of them"*.
  ✔ **BUILT (`692a3d027`).** `PickupMagnet { range, speed }`, absent by default;
  Ambition's own coin/heart drops declare `PickupMagnet::classic()` so their feel
  is unchanged and now stated; the attractor is the NEAREST body in the same
  population `collect_ecs_pickups` claims with.
  ⭐ **the couch defect was worse than "loot flies at seat one"**: the old query
  was `With<PrimaryPlayer>` + `.single()`, so with two players present it matched
  nothing and the magnet would have stopped entirely. Both cases are now tests. *"Maryo coins and sanic rings
  should not be magnetic to the player."* One attraction rule, two games.
- ✔ **D8. BOTH halves resolved (size 2026-08-03, direction 2026-08-04).**
  ⭐ **the size is one number**: `spark_shot()` builds
  `ProjectileFlight::arcing(...).with_half_extent(Vec2::new(7.0, 7.0))` — a 14px
  square, which is Jon's *"very tiny"*. Its neighbours are all named constants
  (`SPARK_SPEED`, `SPARK_GRAVITY`, `SPARK_BOUNCES`, `SPARK_LIFETIME_S`); the
  extent is the one written inline, which is why it reads as an oversight.
  ✔ **the direction WAS located — and this marker sat `▢` two lines above the
  answer.** ⚠ **a row that records its own conclusion and leaves the question
  open reads as open**, which is the same staleness as a `▢` on landed work, at
  paragraph scale rather than row scale. The finding, kept: `spawn_player_projectile` takes `direction: Vec2` as a PARAMETER, so
  the fault is in whichever caller launches a `RangedActionSpec` bolt — and if
  that caller hardcodes `+x` rather than reading the body's facing, then *every*
  game's bolt fires right and this is an engine bug wearing a Mary-O costume.
  ⚠ that is the reference-frame family this repo already knows: a body-local
  vector treated as a world one.
  ⭐ **TRACED, and the engine-wide worry is RULED OUT.** The shared ranged path
  is correctly frame-typed at all three points:
  * the human brain sets `fire` from `local_aim`, falling back to
    `Vec2::new(snapshot.actor_facing, 0.0)` — her facing — and wraps it as
    `ActorFireRequest::controlled_body_local` (`brain/player.rs:148`);
  * the moveset's `MoveEventKind::Ranged` handler passes the request's dir AND
    its policy through, with a body-local `+x` fallback when nothing aimed;
  * the spawn seam calls `request.dir_to_world(frame)`.
  So a bolt fired through the ranged verb follows the shooter. **No engine bug.**
  ⭐ **and her OWN press handler reads correct too (2026-08-03), which closes the
  "does her spark even travel this path" half.** `fire_spark_on_run_press`
  (`movement.rs:198`) raises the ordinary `fire` intent as
  `ActorFireRequest::controlled_body_local(Vec2::new(kin.facing.signum(), 0.0))`
  — so it is the shared ranged path, and it does supply her facing.
  ⚠ **the tempting reading of that line is WRONG and worth writing down**: it
  looks like a double-application of facing — facing baked into a vector that is
  then declared body-local. It is not. `ControlledBodyLocal` resolves through
  `AccelerationFrame`, whose basis is built from GRAVITY (`side` is `down` rotated
  −90°), so the frame carries no facing at all and the caller supplying it is
  exactly right. Under normal gravity the transform is the identity.
  ✔ **ANSWERED 2026-08-04, and it did not need the runtime probe** — the two
  consumers cannot disagree, because they read the same field.
  `fire_spark_on_run_press` takes `kin.facing.signum()`. The sprite takes
  `BodyPoseView.facing`, which `publish_player_pose_views` assigns from
  `kinematics.facing` (the only real facing assignment in `pose_view.rs`; the
  other three are `Default`s), and `animation.rs:66` sets
  `sprite.flip_x = gravity_aware_flip_x(facing, dir)`, which is `facing < 0.0`
  with the gravity argument deliberately unused. So `sprite.flip_x` is
  `kin.facing < 0`, on the same value the fire handler reads.
  ⭐ **a static trace is STRONGER than the print here**, because the question was
  "do these two agree" and the answer is that they are one value with two
  readers — a print could only ever sample that agreement. What it does not prove
  is that `kin.facing` is itself right; but if it were wrong she would MOONWALK,
  and Jon reported a tiny bolt, not a body facing the wrong way.
  ⛔ **and the SIZE half was already fixed while this row still said inline** —
  the fourth stale `▢` found in two days. `SPARK_HALF_EXTENT = 10.0` is a named
  constant beside its neighbours (20 px across a 32 px tile, up from 7.0/14 px),
  carrying the note that `ProjectileRenderSize::Body` makes the drawn quad follow
  it so the sprite cannot disagree with the hitbox. **Both halves of D8 were
  done; only the bookkeeping was open.**
- ◐ **D9. The obvious writers are all INNOCENT, so the flash comes from
  somewhere else (2026-08-03).** Jon: *"maryo flashes when her fireball hits an
  enemy. that should not happen."* Every `hit_flash` writer was read:
  * `damage/actor_hit.rs:145` and `boss_hit.rs:165/202` write the **victim's**
    `combat`, not the attacker's;
  * `movement_fx.rs:323` is guarded to hazardous resets, with a comment saying
    exactly why an action must not flash;
  * `input_systems.rs:214` only decays it.
  ⭐ so the likely culprit is not a hit_flash writer at all: `ambition_sim_view`
  projects `hit_flash_secs` into the pose view, and the renderer's flash pass
  draws whatever the view says. A flash on the striker would then be a VIEW
  binding reading the wrong body — the same shape as the presentation-binding
  failures this repo has hit before, and NOT a damage-rule bug.
  ⚠ **and the view binding reads CORRECT too (2026-08-03), so the row's own
  leading hypothesis is now the weaker one.** `hit_flash_secs_for_source`
  (`ambition_render/rendering/hit_flash.rs:369`) takes the PLAYER path first and
  reads `poses.get(source_entity)` — the same entity that carries the sprite,
  per-entity so clones flash independently. There is no wrong-body lookup on the
  path Mary-O travels.
  ⭐ **the fragile lookup is the OTHER branch, and it is worth writing down**: the
  feature path is keyed by a STRING id (`feature_views.get(feature.id.as_str())`),
  so two features sharing an id would read each other's flash. Mary-O short-
  circuits past it as a player — but that is the shape to check first if the probe
  ever shows her routing through the feature branch.
  ▢ probe, unchanged in form and now sharper in meaning: fire her spark at a snake
  and print `BodyCombat::hit_flash` for BOTH bodies on the frame it connects. With
  both the damage writers and the view binding read clean, a non-zero value on HER
  body means a writer nobody has found yet; a zero one means the white Jon sees is
  not `hit_flash` at all, and the search moves to the spark's own impact visual and
  to her downgrade blink. The striker is
  reading the victim's feedback; the same class as the shared-reason invulnerability
  work.
- ⛔ **D10. MY FIRST DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG, and the error is the useful part.**
  Jon: *"maryo can stand on a broken brick."*
  I recorded that `BrokenBricks` had exactly one consumer (its own dedup) and

  requires, and has its own test.
  ⚠ **I grepped the TYPE NAME across files and concluded absence.** The consumer
  is in the same file as the type, so `grep BrokenBricks | grep -v bricks.rs`
  hid it. That is the standing lesson — *a name search finds absence reliably and
  presence never* — made again, in a ledger row, an hour after using the right
  technique on the same problem.
  ✔ **what IS verified by reading, link by link**: break marks the bit; the bit
  becomes `removed_block_names`; `world_with_sandbox_solids` applies
  `apply_overlay_subtractions` before anything reads `solids()`; the level
  authors `brick_<i>` with the matching `GeoId` and a test pins that. Sanic's
  monitors use the identical seam.
  ✔ **THE PROBE IS WRITTEN AND IT PASSES** (`a_broken_brick_leaves_the_collision_world_the_body_reads`):
  against the REAL level, brick 0 starts solid in
  `world_with_sandbox_solids(...)`, and after the break + the contribution the
  composed world no longer contains it while its neighbour is untouched. The
  composition — the one link that had no test — is correct.
  ✔ and the re-arm is ruled out too: `RoomLoaded` is written once per successful
  room construction (`world/rooms/transaction.rs:229`), not per frame, so
  `refill_bricks_on_room_loaded` only fires on an actual (re)load, which is the
  intended behaviour its own test pins.
  ⭐ **so the leading hypothesis is now that the report is about a SPENT ?-BLOCK,
  not a broken brick** — and a spent ?-block staying solid is CORRECT (it is what
  the genre does). `SpentPowerBlocks` contributes nothing to
  `removed_block_names`, deliberately.
  ⚠ **which makes D11 the actual fix.** Jon's other row asks for used blocks to
  get *"a new texture so they are visually distinguishable"* — if a spent
  ?-block looks like a broken brick, then "I can stand on a broken brick" is
  exactly what a player would report. Two rows, one cause, and the visual is the

  ▢ **needs Jon**: which block was it — one that shattered into shards, or one
