- ✔ **D10b (found while reading for D11). `SpentPowerBlocks` is a `HashSet` and
  its Sanic twin is a `Vec`, and the asymmetry is CORRECT.** `SpentMonitors`'s doc
  says out loud why it is a Vec: *"the overlay contribution iterates it every frame
  and the sim determinism contract bans std-hash iteration order."* Mary-O's is a
  `HashSet<GeoId>` — which looks like the same bug unfixed.
  ✔ **it is not, and the difference is real**: `SpentPowerBlocks` is only ever
  looked up (`is_spent`), inserted into (`spend`) or cleared (`rearm_all`). Nothing
  iterates it, so no order is ever observed. Checked by reading every use — four
  sites, all point queries.
  ✔ **said in `powerups.rs`**, naming the twin and naming the DANGEROUS direction
  of a consistency fix (turning `SpentMonitors` into a HashSet, not the reverse).
  ⚠ and if either is ever CHECKSUMMED for a desync canary, that IS an iteration
  and it must be canonicalized first — B3's row already says this.

- ✔ **D11. BOTH HALVES SHIPPED — texture and nudge (closed 2026-08-04).**
  ⛔ **the SIXTH stale `▢` found in two days.** The nudge was built to this row's
  own specification and the row never learned: `BlockStruck` is a MESSAGE (not a

  `engine.render-never-names-live-sim-state`), `flinch_struck_blocks` animates a

  and it restores `home` and removes itself when the curve ends. The texture half

  ⚠ **and the row's design decision was RIGHT and is worth keeping**: the nudge is
  presentation, so a body standing on a bonked block is not lifted and a rollback
  has no animation to rewind.
  ⭐ **the cheap tell was in the row itself** — it said "there is no block-bump
  seam in the engine today" as of 08-03, and `block_nudge.rs` exists. **A row
  asserting an ABSENCE is the easiest kind to re-check**, and the one most worth
  re-checking before working it.
  The original row:
- ▢ **the ORIGINAL D11 statement (kept for its reasoning):** an
  in-code position nudge up and back, so a spent block reads as spent.
  ⭐ **the design decision, settled before building so the next stretch does not
  re-derive it (2026-08-03).** There is no block-bump seam in the engine today — a
  repo-wide search for one finds only unrelated senses of "nudge"/"bump" — so this
  is a build, and the only real question is WHICH SIDE it lives on.
  ✔ **PRESENTATION, not sim.** The nudge must not move the collision box: a body
  standing on a bonked block would be lifted by it, a body beside it would be
  shoved, and a rollback would have to rewind the animation. The block's geometry
  is authoritative and static; what moves is the drawn quad.
  ▢ **the shape**: a short-lived fact on the block — bonked-at time, or a small
  countdown — published into the read-model the way `BodyPoseView` is, with the
  renderer offsetting the quad by a curve over ~0.12 s. ⚠ per E4 the renderer must
  read the VIEW, never the block's sim component, and
  `engine.render-never-names-live-sim-state` enforces exactly that — it is the
  policy that went red on 08-02 and again this run.
  ✔ **THE ART IS DONE (submodule `20df2e1`, 2026-08-03)** — and it turned out to be
  two jobs, because the `?`-block was not drawing a question mark at all. It drew a
  featureless bar (`draw.rectangle((6, 5, 9, 10))`), which is why Jon asked for
  *"actual question mark blocks... although we should probably make them
  interrobang blocks as a parody"*. `super_mary_o_tileset` now emits:
  | `block_solid` | the interrobang — a `?` hook landing on a straight `!` stem with a detached dot, dark on gold |
  | `block_spent` | dim, flat, glyphless |
  ⭐ **two things came from LOOKING at it rather than reasoning**: a one-pixel drop
  shadow muddied the stem, and the straight stroke is exactly what carries the `!`
  half of the joke; and a bright glyph on a bright block washes out at 16 px, so it
  is dark on gold the way the classic blocks read. Neither was predictable from the
  code.
  ✔ **that seam EXISTS now — `BlockArt` + `apply_block_art` (2026-08-04), and
  Mary-O's `dress_power_blocks` is its first consumer.** It derives from
  `SpentPowerBlocks` every frame rather than reacting to the bonk, because that
  set is rollback state and art driven by the EVENT would keep the used look
  through a rewind that undid the strike. **Tenth stale marker.**
  ⛔ **and this row is why a text scanner cannot find these.** It names
  `SpentPowerBlocks`, `bonk_power_blocks` and `AttackVfxView` — all of which
  exist — in a sentence whose subject is a seam that did not. I built a scanner
  for exactly this sweep and deleted it: 14 hits, 14 false, because *"X is
  missing"* and *"X exists and something ABOUT X is missing"* look identical to a
  regex and the second is the shape of almost every row here.
  The original statement:
  ▢ **the texture half is a WIRING job needing a seam that did not exist:** `SpentPowerBlocks` is a `HashSet<GeoId>` read only by
  `bonk_power_blocks`; nothing renders from it. And blocks are authored GEOMETRY
  rather than entities, so this cannot copy `AttackVfxView`'s per-entity shape —
  it wants a set-valued fact in the read model, published sim-side, with the
  renderer swapping the tile for ids in that set. ⚠ presentation must read the
  VIEW and never `SpentPowerBlocks` itself: that resource lives in the demo crate,

  ✔✔ **THE NUDGE IS DONE (`6fc90eab8`)** and it settled the seam: `BlockStruck`
  carries a `GeoId`, `BlockVisual` now carries one too, and `flinch_struck_blocks`
  drives the drawn quad out and back over 0.12 s against the GRAVITY frame — so a
  block struck in a flipped room flinches the way that room means it. Collision
  never moves.
  ⚠ **and the eight fixtures it broke are the reusable part**: an unregistered Bevy
  message fails PARAMETER VALIDATION rather than being ignored, so every fixture
  that runs the emitting system has to declare the channel. A new message is never
  a one-file change.
  ⛔ **but the TEXTURE half is NOT the same seam, and tracing it changed the

  solid block in the room shares that one sprite. **There is no per-block sprite
  binding to swap**, so the `block_spent` tile the generator now emits cannot be
  reached this way.
  ⛔⛔ **ANSWERED, AND IT RETRACTS THE "ART IS DONE" CLAIM ABOVE.** `super_mary_o_tileset`
  is **generated and consumed by NOTHING**: its output is not in the asset tree, and
  no `.rs` or `.ldtk` names it or its `MarySolids` layer. The interrobang exists in
  a sheet nothing binds.
  ⭐ **and that is the real reason a `?`-block never showed a question mark** — not
  a bad glyph, but that the game does not draw from this tileset at all. Mary-O's
  blocks are CODE-authored geometry carrying `GeoId::tile_layer` ids, so
  `spawn_block` takes the `is_intgrid_block` branch and binds
  `block_tile_sprite(Solid) → EntitySprite::SolidTile`: **one shared sprite for
  every solid block in the room.**
  ⚠ **I told Jon "your ?-blocks get an actual glyph" before checking that anything
  consumed the sheet.** The generator change is still correct and still wanted; it
  is simply not sufficient, and saying so late is worse than saying so first.
  ✔ **THE SEAM IS BUILT (2026-08-04), and it keys on exactly the handle this row
  named.** `BlockArt(EntitySprite)` is a component on the RENDER entity, matched
  by `BlockVisual.geo_id`; `apply_block_art` (in `SessionRoomVisualsPlugin`,
  beside the pass that spawns blocks) applies it.
  ⭐ **a component, not a field on `ae::Block`, because the identity is
  DYNAMIC** — a `?`-block becomes a used block mid-play, and a value read once at
  spawn could never stay right. That is also why `art_color` could not be
  extended to do this: it is baked at spawn and can only say "no art yet".
  ⭐ **it rewrites `BoundEntitySprite`, not just the `Sprite`.** That component is
  the RESOLVED binding, and the asset-reload refresher re-derives every image from
  it — setting only the image would look right until the next `GameAssets` change
  quietly restored the kind's texture, which is the bug that surfaces an hour
  later with no edit to blame.
  ⭐ **and the ART now exists, which is what "nothing consumed the sheet" was
  really about.** The tileset's `block_spent` pixels target an LDtk tile-layer
  workflow Mary-O's CODE-authored 1-1 never uses. So the entity target grew
  `bonus_block_tile` — warm plate, bevelled frame, rivets, the interrobang — and
  it is the LIVE state. **The used state needs no art of its own**: a spent block
  drops the override and falls back to the kind's plain tile, which is exactly
  the block it becomes.
  ⚠ **Mary-O's `dress_power_blocks` derives from `SpentPowerBlocks` every frame
  rather than reacting to the bonk**, because that set is rollback state — a block
  struck on a mispredicted frame un-spends when the frame is thrown away, and art
  driven by the EVENT would keep the used look through a rewind that undid the
  strike.
  ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-05 — every demo can be photographed now.** The harness is

  ⛔ **the original text, kept:** `capture_scene` composes
  Ambition's app, so no room of Mary-O's, Sanic's or any demo is reachable by the

  ▢ superseded: where a `?`-block's art comes from:
  if these are LDtk tile-layer cells drawn by the tilemap (`GeoSource::TileLayer`
  is what `spawn_block` branches on), the swap is a TILE-INDEX change in the map

  a different owner from the flinch. ⚠ do not assume it can copy the flinch's
  shape; that assumption is what this row just cost.
- ◐ **D12. World 1-2 is a LEVEL, not a detour.** It follows a 1-1 win, cycles back
  to 1-1 when beaten, needs vertical elevator platforms that wrap when they leave
  the view, and needs enemies — Jon asks for the flying snakes on a plane. ⚠ the
  wrap is an engine-shaped question (a platform that teleports is a transit, and
  transits have an arbiter).
  ⭐⭐ **THE ENGINE HALF WAS INVESTIGATED FIRST AND IT IS WORSE THAN THE ROW
  THOUGHT (2026-08-03).** `KinematicPathMode::Loop` already exists, and a
  two-point vertical `Loop` is exactly how anyone would author this elevator. It
  **hung the simulation.**
  * `advance_path_segment` computes `last_segment` as `points.len() - 2`, which is
    **0** for two points. Arriving at the second point "wraps" the cursor to
    segment 0 — whose target is the point the platform is already standing on.
    Distance zero, cursor unchanged, `continue`, forever, consuming no `dt`.
  * ⛔ **found by the probe HANGING**, not by reading: a `cargo test` sat at 300s
    with no `rustc` running and cargo blocked in `do_wait` on its own test binary.
  * ⛔ **and the identical loop exists TWICE** — `platforms::advance_path_position`
    and `ambition_combat::path_motion::PathMotion::advance` are line-for-line the
    same shape. The second drives spike balls, patrol dummies and scripted boss
    hazards. Both now break when an advance leaves the cursor unmoved, both have a
    termination test.
  ⚠ **no shipped content uses `Loop`**, which is the only reason a hang has been
  sitting in the engine unnoticed. That is not reassurance — it means the first
  person to author the obvious thing would have frozen the game.
  ✔ **and `Loop` now LOOPS.** The cursor used to wrap to segment 0 while the
  POSITION was still at the last point, so the mover retraced the final leg
  backwards and never revisited the first. A path of `n` points has `n - 1` OPEN
  segments and `n` CLOSED ones — the closing leg `p[n-1] → p[0]` simply did not
  exist. `Loop` now takes `last_segment = n - 1` and a target index of
  `(segment + 1) % n`; both steppers, identically, because they are the same
  algorithm written twice and must not drift.
  ⚠ **the three-point test caught a measurement bug in ITSELF first**: it looked
  for the platform within 1px of its first point, while the platform moves in 10px
  steps and steps straight PAST a waypoint rather than landing on it. It reported
  "never returned" for a mover that was passing through the corner every lap. The
  tolerance is now one frame of travel.
  ▢ **the ELEVATOR still needs a second mode, and that one IS a design call.**
  A closed circuit is what "loop" means for a path, and it is now what `Loop`
  does — but it is not a screen-wrapping elevator: the platform slides visibly
  back down instead of vanishing at the top and reappearing at the bottom. That
  wants a distinct `Wrap`, and the row's original instinct is exactly right about
  why it is not just another enum arm: **a wrap is a TRANSIT.**
  `Block::velocity`'s contract is *"the collision sweep carries any body resting
  on the block by this"*, so on the wrap frame a rider is carried the entire column
  in one step — through whatever is in between — unless an arbiter says otherwise.
  ⭐ SMB1's 1-2 leaves you behind. That is a decision, not a default, and it is
  Jon's. Until it is made, `Loop` at least means something.
- ✔ **D13. ALREADY TRUE, and enforced by the build rather than by convention
  (verified 2026-08-03).** Jon: *"For the web build we can't use kaleidoscope
  because lunex doesn't support wasm."*
  Checked by expanding every `web*` feature's transitive closure in
  `ambition_app/Cargo.toml`: **none of the eight reaches `kaleidoscope_menu`**,
  which lives in the `desktop_dev` persona. And the suite's own
  `web build check [web]` / `[web_served_assets]` jobs compile
  `--no-default-features --features web` for `wasm32-unknown-unknown` — both
  green in the 08-03 exhaustive run (41s, 42s).
  ⭐ so the constraint cannot silently regress: adding kaleidoscope to a web
  feature fails that job at COMPILE time, not in review. Nothing to build; the
  row exists now so nobody re-fixes it.
- ✔ **D13b. ANSWERED BY THE BUILD (2026-08-03), and it does not need Jon.**
  The row assumed the constraint was satisfied by OMISSION — no kaleidoscope, so
  no menu — and deferred "what should a web player see instead" to him as a
  content decision. Reading the graph says the decision is already made and the
  answer is *the Grid menu*.
  * both real web personas carry `bevy_ui_menu`: expanding the feature closures,
    `web` and `web_served_assets` (the two the suite actually compiles) reach it,
    while `kaleidoscope_menu` is in none of the eight;
  * `InventoryUiBackend::default()` returns `Grid` whenever
    `KALEIDOSCOPE_MENU_BACKEND_ENABLED` is false, and `effective()` falls back to
    `Grid` for an unavailable backend — so the selection cannot land on a backend
    that is not compiled in.
  ⭐ **so a web player gets the flat Grid menu — the same frontend a desktop
  player sees by toggling backends, not a degraded cube and not nothing.** Which
  also means "degrade the effect on wasm rather than dropping the build" is
  already what happens, one level up: the BACKEND degrades, so the effect never
  has to.
  ⚠ what remains is a taste question nobody has raised — whether the Grid menu
  looks right as the web front door — and that is a real content call, unlike the
  functional gap this row was written about.

  Measured, not argued, with `scripts/show_sprite_gameplay_box.py`:

  | | |
  |---|---|
  | player collider | `30x48`, the `DEFAULT_PLAYER_BODY_WIDTH/HEIGHT` constant |
  | sprite quad | `max(30,48) * collision_scale(1.65) * PLAYER_PLACEHOLDER_VISUAL_SCALE(1.16)` = `91.9` |
  | drawn body inside it | `23.4 x 37.3` — the authored box at `57x91` of a `224` frame |
  | **collider ÷ drawn body** | **1.28× wide, 1.29× tall** |

  In frame pixels the collider spans `x 75.4..148.6, y 40.0..157.0`, against a
  drawn body of `x 86..143, y 67..158` — so its top edge is **17 px above the
  tip of their antenna** and it is wider than their full arm span. That is exactly
  what Jon is looking at.

  ⭐ **and it is a FORK, which is why it never got fixed by tuning.** Ordinary
  actors already derive their collision box from published sprite metrics —
  `sprite_body_collision_for_character_id_from_data` computes the world box of
  the *visible body* and stores the render size so the quad is unchanged; the
  seam is called from `actor_clusters.rs` for every NPC and enemy. The richer
  seam above it, `SpritePosedBody` / `BodySource::SpriteAuthored { world_per_pixel }`,
  makes the art the authority per pose and is what Mary-O's three forms, the
  cardboard-box snake and the duel PCA all use. **The player robot uses
  neither.** `player_robot_lineage.rs::definition_from` builds v3 with
  `.with_sheet(...)` and no body source, so they keep the 30×48 constant and the
  hand-tuned `collision_scale`. One body, two paths — the rule `AGENTS.md` calls
  the most-violated one.

  ⭐⭐ **the same measurement explains D4** (*"the humanoid characters are all
  dramatically out of scale with each other"*). `collision_scale` is a
  hand-guessed per-character multiplier and nothing checks the guess: across the
  **174** sheets that publish a body box, the drawn body's height runs from
  `0.48×` to `2.04×` of its own collider (`perfect_cellular_automaton` and
  `puppy_slug` are the ends; `generic_explosions` at `5.2×` is not a character).
  **A 4× spread in a quantity that ought to be 1.** D4 asks for the fix "at the
  generator level, not a post-hoc fix" — `collision_scale` *is* the post-hoc
  fix, and the generator-level answer is `world_per_pixel`, which already exists.

  ⛔ **the blocker, and it is why this row is not a one-liner.** Putting v3 on
  the posed seam today would make their box WORSE, because
  `BodyMetrics::pose_body_bbox` prefers `animations[anim].hurtbox` over the
  authored static box — and the generator fills those from each animation's
  **alpha bbox**, motion smear included. v3's are `block 128` wide, `dash 143`
  wide, `idle 71x103` (antenna and full arm span) against an authored body of
  `57x91`. So the authored rectangle can never win: the fallback that
  `pose_body_bbox`'s own docstring describes as *"the whole answer for the
  common characters whose silhouette barely moves"* is unreachable, because the
  generator emits per-animation data unconditionally for all 174 sheets.
  **Two different rectangles — what the body IS, and what the art happens to
  cover — share one field, and the measured one outranks the authored one.**
  ⚠ and the per-animation path is not wrong for everyone: the snake that
  withdraws into a cardboard box is the case it was built for. The distinction
  has to be DECLARED, not switched globally.

  ▢ **the shape of the fix, in order:**
  1. let a target declare that its gameplay body is its authored static box, so
     the generator stops emitting per-animation gameplay bodies for it and the
     documented fallback becomes reachable. Nothing about the snake changes.
  2. put v3 on `BodySource::SpriteAuthored { world_per_pixel }` — `48/91 =
     0.527` keeps their standing height at the 48 the level geometry is built
     around, and `57 * 0.527 = 30.1` lands their width on today's 30 as well, so
     **the collider barely moves and the ART grows to fit it** (quad `91.9 →
     118`). That direction is the safe one: the collider is what levels are
     authored against.
  3. only then Jon's inset — carve the authored box under the head crest and
     inside the arms in the generator, where `body_inset()` already applies it
     to the per-animation boxes too.
  ⚠ **step 3 is the one that is Jon's call and not mine**, and the two readings
  of *"under the main head"* differ by a lot: box top just below the crown
  (forgiving) versus box top at the neck so the head is not hittable at all
  (very forgiving, and it makes their head clip ceilings because this ONE box is
  both the collider and the hurtbox). ⭐ that ambiguity is itself a finding —
  *"collision / hurt box"* is one thing in the engine today, and separating them
  is what makes "very forgiving" expressible without breaking ceilings. The
  `AuthoredHurtboxes` / `HurtboxDoc` seam already exists and the player authors
  nothing, so their hurtbox falls back to the coarse body AABB.

  ✔ **steps 1–3 are done, and the ambiguity resolved itself: Jon's sentence
  describes TWO boxes.** The collision box is *"slightly inset from the visible
  parts"*; the hurtbox is *"under the main head, well within the arms, very
  forgiving"*. One rectangle could not be both, which is why the sentence read as
  contradictory — and separating them is the answer rather than a compromise.
  * `body_metrics.authored_body` says which rectangle `body_pixel_bbox` is, and
    `pose_bodies="authored"` stops the measured per-animation box outranking the
    authored one. The runtime fallback `pose_body_bbox` documents was
    UNREACHABLE, not unused.
  * the lineage asks each incarnation's SHEET for an authored body and hands the
    geometry over when it finds one — so v0 and v2, whose boxes are still raw
    silhouettes, decline on their own with no per-version flag.
  * the hurtbox is fractional insets of the authored body box (`0.09 / 0.21 /
    0.43 / 0.01`), measured against their anatomy: antenna `y 59..67`, head
    `y 68..104`, shoulders `y 104..110`, torso and arms `y 110..140` `x 83..138`.
  Measured after: collider `30.1x48.0` against a drawn body of `30.1x48.0` —
  **1.00× in both axes**, from 1.28×/1.29×. The ART grew (quad `91.9 → 118.2`)
  rather than the collider moving, which is the safe direction.

- ✔ **D14b. ⛔ A STANCE DOES NOT SURVIVE THE PER-TICK RESYNC, found by putting
  the player on the posed seam — and it is Mary-O's bug already, not one I
  introduced.** `sync_sprite_posed_bodies` writes `kin.size` from the pose every
  tick. The crouch is applied ONCE, on the tick the mode changes:
  `body_mode/mechanics` does `if mode == target { continue; }` and
  `try_change_body_mode_clusters` early-returns on an unchanged mode, so nothing
  re-asserts the shorter box afterwards. A body on this seam therefore crouches
  for one tick and then stands back up inside its own crouch, with `BodyModeState`
  still saying `Crouching`. **Mary-O has been on this seam and can crouch**, so
  this predates today; putting the protagonist on it widens the blast radius,
  which is why it is being fixed in the same breath rather than filed.
  ✔ **the rule the fix states**: the pose says how big the body IS, the MODE says
  what it is doing with it, and the box is the composition —
  `BodyMode::shape(posed_collision)` rather than the posed collision raw.
  `BodyMode::shape` is the same function the stance transition uses, so the two
  cannot disagree; applying it to the POSE's rectangle rather than to `base_size`
  is what keeps it right for a shape-changing body (a boxed snake that crouched
  would otherwise crouch from its sprawled height).
  ⭐ **probed red first and that is the only reason to trust it**:
  `a_stance_survives_the_per_tick_resync` asserted 14.3 and got 26.0 — the
  standing box, on a body whose mode said `Crouching`. It steps TWICE on purpose;
  a single step could pass by accident on a system that happens to run before the
  stance is applied.

- ▢ **D14c. ⭐⭐ `collision_scale` IS `frame_height / body_height`, hand-computed
  and stale — which is D4's root cause, stated exactly.** Not a tuning knob: a
  cached division of two numbers the sheet already publishes, copied into a
  constant that nothing recomputes when the art is re-cropped.

  | target | frame | authored body | `collision_scale` | `frame_h / body_h` |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `player_robot_v3` | 224×224 | 57×91 | 1.65 | **2.462** |
  | `noether` | 72×157 | 60×149 | 2.0 | **1.054** |
  | `perfect_cellular_automaton` | 384×518 | 179×452 | 2.4 | **1.146** |

  ⛔ **and somebody already noticed, wrote the correction down, and it has never
  once been read.** Exactly three catalog rows disagree with their manifest —
  and they are these three. `character_catalog.ron` gives `noether` `1.1` against
  the exact `1.054`, and `perfect_cellular_automaton` `1.12` against `1.146`:
  both within 4% of the right answer, so they were computed the same way. But
  `spec_from_record` takes `record.tuning` whenever the manifest declares one,
  and the manifest always does. Its comment says *"manifest-authored tuning
  overrides Rust FALLBACK tuning"* — describing `DEFAULT_TUNING`, which is not
  what the caller actually passes. The loser is a human-authored content row.
  ⚠ **the same parameter is a no-op for every other caller too**:
  `try_load_spec_for_target("robot", &SheetTuning::new(1.0, 1))` and the
  `super_mary_o_pipe_top` call in `world.rs` both hand over a scale that is
  discarded. The argument is not "tuning"; it is a suggestion.
  ⚠ **and the catalog's `feet_anchor_y` DOES win** (via
  `feet_anchor_y_override`), so within one struct one field's authored value
  outranks the manifest and another's is thrown away. That asymmetry is what
  makes this an oversight rather than a decision.

  ▢ **the fix is NOT to flip the precedence**, and that is the interesting part.
  `collision_scale` is on its way out: for a sheet that authors a body box the
  scale is *derivable*, which is what `world_per_pixel` already is. Flipping the
  precedence would change `super_mary_o_pipe_top` and every other suggestion-that-
  becomes-real by an unmeasured amount, to keep a constant that should not exist.
  ⛔ **nor is it to derive the scale in `sprite_render_size` today** — the TODO in
  that file asks for exactly that, and I did not write it, because after D14 the
  only two sheets with an authored body are v3 and Mary-O and BOTH are on the
  posed seam, which never calls it. A derivation with no consumer is the
  pre-generalization the engine direction forbids.
  ⚠ **RE-READ 2026-08-04: this is no longer "the migration D4 needs" — it is the
  second half of one.** D4's complaint was that comparable humanoids differ in
  SIZE, and that is now answered by `CharacterCatalogEntry.standing_height` plus
  `CharacterBodyKind::Standard = 48.0`: the height is stated, so the scale stops
  being derived from a level editor's rectangle and two guesses. **What this row
  still buys is different and still wanted** — an authored body box says WHICH
  PIXELS are the body, which is what makes the collision box agree with the art.
  Height says how tall; the box says how much of the frame is real. Neither
  implies the other, and `collision_scale` cannot be deleted until this half
  lands.
  ▢ **the migration itself, per-character and cheap**: author the
  body box in the generator (`body_metrics_fn` or a fractional `body_inset`,
  plus `pose_bodies="authored"` for a rigid body), put the character on
  `BodySource::SpriteAuthored`, and its scale stops being a guess. `noether` and
  `perfect_cellular_automaton` are the two worst offenders in the 174-sheet
  spread and are the obvious first pair. When enough characters are on it,
  `collision_scale` becomes the legacy path and can be deleted rather than fixed.

- ✔ **D14d. The player's quad is placed by the feet anchor, not by
  `ActorSpriteOffset`. FIXED** — `BodyPoseView` carries the published offset now,
  gated on a sheet-authored body so every legacy sheet keeps the feet-anchor path.
  ⚠ **verified as NO REGRESSION, not as the correction.** `capture_scene` at
  640x360 shows the robot planted with the art on the box and no offset jump; it
  cannot show a 1 px change at that size, and the 1 px was measured, not seen.
  Getting even that picture cost D16's four panics.
  The original finding: The posed seam publishes the quad's offset from the body
  centre — the number that puts the ART on the BOX — and the player render branch
  recomputes an anchor from `feet_anchor_norm` instead. For v3 they differ by
  ~1 px vertically (their `feet_pixel.y` is 157 against a box bottom of 158) and
  ~2.5 px horizontally (their authored box is centred on 114.5, the frame on 112).
  Sub-pixel-ish at their scale, and a genuine second derivation of one fact — the
  actor path uses the published offset and the player path does not, which is the
  same one-body-two-paths shape D14 was.

- ✔ **D17. Jon's third touch complaint, and the only one still open:** *"I know
  in sanic the button text doesn't match what the controls really are."*
  **FOUND, FIXED, PROBED — a Sanic menu's confirm button read "Spin Dash".**
  `update_button_verb_from_prompt` wrote a verb only when the prompt offered one
  (`.then(..).flatten()`), and in a menu the confirm pair takes its verb from
  `prompt.menu_confirm` — published by `install_menu_confirm_provider`, which
  **`ambition_app`'s kaleidoscope menu calls and nothing else does.** So in every
  demo the value is `None`, nothing was written, and the buttons kept the verbs
  of the gameplay the player had just left. The doc comment on that system
  promised the opposite in so many words: *"so a menu button never reads
  'Jump.'"* — kept only where a provider happened to exist. **[[APP-ONLY
  presentation class]] again**, this time visible instead of silent.
  ⭐ **two changes, because the promise needed both.** `ButtonVerb` was an enum
  (`Static(&'static str)` | `Dynamic(String)`), so the first dynamic write
  DESTROYED the spawn label and there was nothing to fall back to. It is a struct
  now — permanent fallback + `Option<String>` current — and the update writes the
  `None` too, so a drawn button with nothing to say cannot show stale text at
  all. Then the menu default: a confirm button with no authored verb reads
  `"Select"`, in the ENGINE, because confirming is what it does in every
  composition that has a menu; a game with a better word still says so through
  `menu_confirm`.
  ⛔ **the probe is the point.** Reverted to the old behaviour and the new test
  fails `Jump confirms this menu but reads "Spin Dash"` — Jon's sentence,
  reproduced. **The dialogue test I wrote yesterday passed against that same
  broken code**, because it supplies a confirm verb, which is exactly what hides
  this. The distinguishing input was `menu_confirm: None`, and only a test that
  drives it is worth anything here.
  ▢ **residue:** demos still publish no `menu_confirm`, so their menus read
  "Select" rather than a specific verb. Correct rather than good; naming them is
  each demo's job and none of them has asked yet.

- ✔ **D18. Jon's dialogue-SPACE complaint, answered structurally rather than by
  moving pixels.** *"the trick is that space for dialogue on a phone is so tight.
  it's really hard to fit that and choices in… it's frustrating and error prone
  and relies on the back button in the top corner, which we might not always want
  there."*
  ⭐ **the engine already knew half of this and had never been asked the other
  half.** `ScreenOcclusionPurpose::reserves_subject_space` carves the
  subject-safe rect against control occupancy — the camera will not frame the
  player behind the thumb-sticks. `dialog_ui` laid out in `left/right: 0,
  top: 15%, bottom: 0`: percentages of the WHOLE screen, consulting nothing. On a
  640x360 viewport (measured from `capture_scene`'s own layout line) the panel
  spans x≈90..550 and the action cluster sits at x 407..640 — a third of the panel
  behind live buttons. **Both halves of "frustrating and error prone" are that one
  overlap:** text behind a button cannot be read, and a tap meant for the text
  goes to the button.
  **`reserves_reading_space` is the sibling predicate**, and `carve_occlusions`
  now takes the QUESTION as a parameter so there is one carve rather than two
  that drift. `ResolvedGameplayPresentation::reading_rect()` is its consumer;
  `fit_dialog_to_reading_rect` keeps the root there across resizes, because
  "correct until the player rotates the phone" is a bug that only reproduces on a
  device.
  ⚠ **the two predicates differ in exactly one place and that place is the
  point.** `SystemMenuControl` answers `reserves_subject_space() == false` — small,
  cornered, glanced at, and a camera can afford to ignore it — but it is precisely
  the corner Back button Jon says he has to rely on, and a paragraph underneath it
  is the collision he described. **A reading rect built on the subject predicate
  would have passed every other assertion and shipped the bug**, so the test
  drives a `SystemMenuControl` and asserts the subject rect still overlaps it.
  Probed: collapsing the predicates fails with *"the dialogue box sits under the
  system controls."*
  ⚠ **`READING_MIN_FRACTION` (0.6 x 0.25) is load-bearing, not tuning.** A crowded
  phone can be carved past usefulness, and a dialogue resolved to a sliver is
  strictly worse than one that overlaps — unplayable rather than awkward. Wide
  rather than tall on purpose: a dialogue can scroll, and a line that wraps every
  three words cannot be read at all.
  ✔ **LOOKED at, and getting there found two more things.**
  ⛔ **there were TWO full-screen text overlays and I had fixed one.**
  `cutscene::overlay` carries the banner, speaker line and skip meter and spreads
  them with `SpaceBetween`, so its bottom half sat under the stick and the action
  cluster. `reading_layout` now owns WHERE for both; padding and flex stay
  authored per panel, because "neither may sit under a live button" is not a
  presentation decision. **Fixing one of two identical overlays looks like a fix
  from the half you fixed.**
  ⛔ **`capture_scene --press` could not press INTERACT.** It knew `z`/`x`/`c` —
  jump, attack, dash — and the default preset binds interact to **F**, the verb
  that opens a dialogue. The repo's only way to look at a visual change could not
  reach the one screen Jon has been reporting bugs on blind. Whole action row
  added (`a`, `e`, `f`, `g`).
  ⭐ **the picture, at last: `cutscene_lab 640x360 --include-ui`.** Nothing sits
  under the controls any more. Two further things are visible in it and neither is
  D18's:
  1. **the control clusters do not SCALE.** `movement=210x210 actions=233x234
     system=163x78` — **byte-identical at 640x360 and at 2340x1080.** On a
     desktop that is trim; on a Pixel 5's LOGICAL viewport (~851x393 after its
     ~2.75 device ratio, which is why 640x360 is the honest proxy and 2340x1080
     is not) the action cluster is 27% of the width and 60% of the height. **That
     is the squeeze Jon feels**, and it is upstream of anything the dialogue can
     do about its own box: the carve stops the overlap, and what it leaves is a
     126 px band. Filed, not fixed — how big a thumb target should be is Jon's
     call, not a number for me to invent.
  2. **two buttons both read "Select"** (F and Z), which is D17's engine default
     doing exactly what it says and looking redundant. Honest; not good. A game
     naming its own verbs through `menu_confirm` fixes it, and distinguishing
     confirm from advance is a design question.

- ✔ **D19. The overlay control rung skipped the fallback ladder sitting beside
  it, and put controls OFF the screen.** Found by chasing the wrong explanation
  for D18's picture and being made to look properly.
  `place_in_column` asks `ControlFootprint::scale_within` — the largest uniform
  scale that still clears the authored `minimum` — and `fn overlay`, the rung a
  phone actually takes (`controls=Overlay` in the presentation log), took
  `footprint.preferred` unconditionally and hardcoded `scale: 1.0`. Its own
  comment says *"the pre-existing behavior"*: it predates the ladder and never
  got it. When the safe rect is smaller than a cluster, `min = safe.max - size`
  is a corner **outside** the safe area — probed at 180x140, where the movement
  stick lands at `min.y = -70`. **A control off the edge of the screen is not
  ugly, it is unpressable.** Same family as every other find on 2026-08-04: a
  mechanism wired into one path and not its sibling.
  ⭐ **and the interesting half is what I did NOT do.** The tempting reading of
  Jon's Pixel 5 report is *the clusters are too big for a phone* — `210x210`,
  `233x234`, `163x78`, byte-identical at every resolution, 27%x60% of a phone's
  logical viewport. I started to make them scale with the display and stopped:
  **a thumb is a fixed PHYSICAL size**, so a touch control should get MORE of a
  small screen, not less, and shrinking them would trade a real problem for a
  worse one. `scale_within` caps at 1.0, so anything that fits is placed exactly
  where it was; the only behaviour that changes is the case that was broken.
  The squeeze is answered on the TEXT's side (D18's `reading_rect`), and the
  second test asserts the non-shrinking half so a later "optimization" cannot
  quietly undo the reasoning.

- ◐ **D20. The only tool that can take a picture can only see ONE of five games.**
  ✔ **Mary-O IS PHOTOGRAPHED (2026-08-04).** `ambition_render::capture` holds the
  game-agnostic half — target, camera adoption, readback, PNG, exit — and
  `capture_mary_o` is its first demo caller, ~120 lines, which is the evidence
  the split is in the right place. ⚠ `capture_scene` is NOT yet rewired onto it;
  that is a dedup, not the capability, and rewiring the one working tool was not
  worth risking to save duplication.
  ⛔ **THREE failures found by trying, each of which WROTE A FILE and reported
  success** — the exact shape this module's own doc warns about:
  1. `RenderMode::Headless` sets `backends: None`: no RenderApp, no texture.
     `OffscreenGpu` is the third mode (no window, real backend).
  2. **`winit` is also the RUNNER.** Disabling it leaves Bevy's single-pass
     runner, so `app.run()` does ONE update and returns having rendered nothing.
     `ScheduleRunnerPlugin` is what a no-window app is missing.
  3. **cameras do not exist at `Startup` in a shell-composed demo** — they appear
     when the shell resolves a route. A `Startup`-only adoption pass matched zero
     and read back 960x540 pixels of `(0,0,0,0)`.
  ⭐ **and (3) is the methodological point: the preview of a transparent PNG and
  the preview of a white scene are IDENTICAL.** It took printing the pixel
  histogram to tell them apart. On a machine with no display, *look at the
  numbers, not only the picture.*
  ✔ **AND THE PICTURE PAID FOR THE TOOL WITHIN THE HOUR: D11's block art was not
  drawing, and now it is.** Three causes hiding behind each other, none visible
  to a compiler or a test:
  1. **`dress_power_blocks` was in the SIM schedule**, chained beside
     `bonk_power_blocks` because that is where the powerup rules live. It mutates
     RENDER entities from inside the rollback loop — a category error dressed as
     tidiness. It runs in `Update` now, beside two sheet registrations whose
     comment already says presentation rides the plain schedule.
  2. **`apply_block_art` skipped a missing handle SILENTLY.** It warns now, and
     that warning is what turned guessing into evidence: six lines, one per bonus
     block, proving the system ran and the handle was the problem. ⭐ **the warn
     was worth more than the fix** — it converted an unbounded search into one
     question.
  3. **entity textures load from `assets/sprites/entities/` and
     `publish entities` writes flat into `assets/sprites/`.** Putting the file
     where the loader reads made the glyph appear.
  ⛔ **and (3) is a live tooling gap, filed not fixed: ANY new entity sprite is
  silently unloadable today.** `load_entity_sprites` `continue`s past an id it
  cannot resolve without a word, so before (2) there was no signal anywhere in the
  chain — declared in `EntitySprite::ALL`, drawn by the generator, published,
  listed in the manifest, and invisible. **Two silences compounding is why this
  needed a photograph rather than a debugger.**
  ⚠ residue: the quality-tier variants (`sprites_0_5x`, `_0_25x`, `_potato`) have
  no `bonus_block_tile` either; `scripts/generate_visual_quality_variants.py`
  needs `rectpack`, which is not installed here.
  The original finding: D11's block art is NOT DRAWING.
  Mary-O's bonus blocks (columns 6 and 10) render as plain grey solid tiles in
  the capture. The seam compiled, its crate's tests passed, and the shipped
  result does nothing — which is precisely the class this row exists to catch.
  Checked and RULED OUT so far: the blocks do carry `BoundEntitySprite`
  (`spawn_block` inserts it whenever the kind resolves to a sprite, which
  `Solid` does); `BonusBlockTile` IS in `EntitySprite::ALL`;
  `SessionRoomVisualsPlugin` — which registers `apply_block_art` — is installed
  via `PlatformerPresentationPlugin`. ▢ **next suspect: the handle.**
  `apply_block_art` only swaps the image `if let Some(handle) =
  assets.entities.get(*art)`, so a missing catalog entry is a SILENT no-op —
  "silence is not a fallback" — and the demo's catalog is the thing not yet read.

- ▢ **the original finding:**
  `capture_scene` composes `ambition_app` — Ambition's rooms, Ambition's content.
  Mary-O, Sanic, Smash and Pocket each have their own app crate and none of them
  is reachable, so every visual claim about a DEMO is argued rather than seen.
  ⭐ **it has cost real evidence twice today.** D11's block art landed unlooked-at
  because Mary-O's 1-1 is out of reach, and D15c's Mary-O button count had to be
  read out of a prompt dump rather than a screenshot. On a machine with no
  display this is the whole visual channel (see D16: it took four panic fixes to
  get it drawing at all), and four fifths of the games are outside it.
  ⚠ **and the demos are where the phone bugs live** — Jon's Sanic and Mary-O
  reports are the ones landed blind.
  ▢ **the shape, before anyone starts:** `capture_scene`'s app build is
  Ambition-specific from `AmbitionGameSimulationPlugin` onward, but everything
  AFTER that — the capture target, the warmup loop, `--press`, the readback — is
  game-agnostic and is the part worth keeping. So the carve is a capture HARNESS
  that takes a composed `App` plus a room id, with `capture_scene` as its first
  caller and one demo as its second. ⚠ the demo apps' `run_game`-style entry
  points build a window; the harness needs the headless branch, which is the same
  split `capture_scene` already makes internally between its two app builders —
  read that first, it is the design.
  ⚠ **do not generalize past two callers.** One demo proves the seam; five is a
  migration, and the point is to be able to LOOK at things, not to own a
  framework.

- ◐ **D21. A new ENTITY SPRITE was silently unloadable, and three separate
  silences hid it. THE PUBLISH PATH IS FIXED (2026-08-04).**
  ⭐ **and the row's own advice paid off** — it said *"read how the 44 files in
  `sprites/entities/` got there before changing the destination, because a second
  producer may already own that directory."* There was one: `regen_sprites.sh`
  rewrote `dest_root` for `target = entities` as a shell special case. So the
  shell path was RIGHT and the documented CLI was WRONG, which is why the
  directory looked correctly populated while new sprites vanished.
  ⭐ **the sibling had already solved it properly**: `sanic_support_entities`
  carries its own `install()` hook whose docstring says *"keep this target
  additive by giving it its own install hook instead of editing the shell
  script's special cases."* `entities` has the same hook now and the shell
  special case is deleted — both call sites, one rule. ⚠ the full-regen line had
  to lose its `--dest-root "$entities_dir"` too, or the hook would have nested
  `sprites/entities/entities/`.
  ✔ **verified the whole way down**: deleted the hand-placed file, ran
  `publish entities` with no override, and captured Mary-O — zero "BlockArt
  names" warnings, interrobang blocks on screen.
  ▢ **still open: the layer-2 silence.** `load_entity_sprites` iterates
  `EntitySprite::ALL` and `continue`s past an id the catalog cannot resolve with
  no log line. A declared sprite nobody can draw is always a bug; a startup warn
  naming it costs nothing. ⚠ **not a panic** — a composition may legitimately
  ship without some art, which is why the `continue` exists.
  ▢ **still open: the quality tiers.** `sprites_0_5x`, `_0_25x` and `_potato`
  have no `bonus_block_tile`; `scripts/generate_visual_quality_variants.py` needs
  `rectpack`, not installed here.
  The original finding: Measured on 2026-08-04 while landing D11's block art: the
  sprite was declared in `EntitySprite::ALL`, drawn by the generator, published,
  and present in `entity_manifest.yaml` — and never reached the game.
  ⛔ **the cause: `publish entities` writes flat into
  `assets/sprites/`, while entity textures LOAD from `assets/sprites/entities/`.**
  44 files sit in that subdirectory; the publish has been putting new ones a level
  up. Anything added to the entity target since whenever that diverged is in the
  same state.
  ⛔ **and nothing says so, at any of three layers:**
  1. `publish` reports success — it wrote the file it meant to write.
  2. `load_entity_sprites` iterates `EntitySprite::ALL` and `continue`s past an id
     the catalog cannot resolve, with no log line.
  3. `apply_block_art` skipped a missing handle silently — **fixed today**, and
     that one warn is what located the whole chain.
  ⭐ **the fix is the publish path**, and it should be checked rather than
  assumed: read how the 44 files in `sprites/entities/` got there before changing
  the destination, because a second producer may already own that directory.
  ▢ **the guard worth having is layer 2**: a declared `EntitySprite` whose id the
  catalog cannot resolve is always a bug — a sprite nobody can draw — and a
  startup warn naming it costs nothing. ⚠ **do NOT make it a panic**: a
  composition may legitimately ship without some art, which is exactly why the
  `continue` is there.
  ⚠ same family, unresolved: the quality-tier variants (`sprites_0_5x`,
  `_0_25x`, `_potato`) are generated by
  `scripts/generate_visual_quality_variants.py`, which needs `rectpack` — not
  installed here, so no new sprite gets its tiers on this machine either.

- ◐ **G. THE GPT 5.6 REVIEW OF `2429450..7875f35`** (Jon pasted two passes,
  2026-08-04; the second supersedes the first and goes deeper). ⚠ **Jon's own
  Mary-O notes came FIRST by his instruction** and are done — see D22.
  ✔ **G5/G10 — generic entity publishing. ALREADY FIXED** before the review
  arrived, and by the same reasoning it gives: the target owns its `install()`
  hook and the shell's special case is deleted. D21.
  ✔ **G5(deep) — the block flinch's `home`.** A re-strike inserted a fresh
  `BlockFlinch`, and the animation pass fills a `None` home from the CURRENT
  transform, which mid-flinch is the displaced one. The drawn block walked off
  its collider a few px per hit. Resets the clock, keeps the home.
  ✔ **G6(deep) — sprite-posed resize used `DEFAULT_GRAVITY_DIR`** on a system
  serving every sprite-posed body, under a comment promising a flipped-gravity
  variant that does not exist. Reads `gravity_dir_for` now — the same per-body
  resolver movement and contact use. ⭐ **both of these were findable from the
  PROSE I wrote**: a comment promising a guarantee the code does not implement is
  worse than none, because it stops the next reader looking.
  ◐ **G1 — THE REAL DEFECT IS FIXED; the review's REMEDY was wrong (2026-08-04).**
  ✔ **the missing check was `External`.** `install_session` marks it without
  clearing `LocalSessionOwnership.started`, so a P2P session installed over a
  local one left the maintainer believing it owned one — and it would stop that
  session on gameplay exit or replace it on a policy change. An `External` veto
  fixes exactly that, and `release()` now genuinely rebuilds (it used to make the
  session look externally owned, so the maintainer stepped aside — the opposite
  of the method's name).
  ⛔ **making `RollbackSessionOwnership` the sole authority BROKE couch play, and
  only running it showed why.** `LocalSyncTest` distinguishes a sync-test session
  from an EXTERNAL one — not one this maintainer started from one somebody else
  started. **Match activation starts its own** with the decided roster's count, so
  treating `LocalSyncTest` as "mine" made the maintainer rebuild a two-player
  match session as one player:
  `two_local_seats_drive_independently_under_a_rollback_host` failed with *"seat
  two authored 40 frames of right and its fighter moved 0.00px"*.
  ✔ **residue CLOSED (2026-08-04): the enum names the OWNER.**
  `SyncTestOwner::{LocalMaintainer, Caller}` is the distinction that was missing,
  and with it the maintainer asks the authority for its OWN sessions — so a
  peer's and a match activation's are both simply not its, and the `External`
  veto is subsumed rather than kept as a second check.
  ⭐ **the owner is an ARGUMENT to the start, not a stamp applied after it.**
  [[reference_an_authority_that_needs_a_follow_up_call]]: stamping afterwards
  leaves a window where the maintainer's own session looks like somebody else's.
  A rebase PRESERVES the owner it had — rebuilding a session under a new world
  does not change whose it is, and inferring one there would hand a
  match-activation session to the maintainer.
  ⚠ `LocalSessionOwnership.started` survives as what it always should have been:
  the POLICY memo, read only after ownership has said yes. It no longer claims
  anything.
  ✔ **`two_local_seats_drive_independently_under_a_rollback_host` passes** — the
  test that caught the wrong version of this, which is why it is named here.
  ⛔ **G2's cheap remedy is HARMFUL and was removed after probing.** "Compare the
  running `SyncTestSettings` and restart on a player-count mismatch" fails the
  same couch test; removing the comparison ALONE makes it pass, which isolates
  it. **Restarting a live session loses the seat→handle binding**, so it trades a
  wrong count for a dead seat. ⭐ only the review's STRONGER endpoint is safe:
  publish the decided roster topology BEFORE installation, so no mismatch exists
  to detect. ⚠ and I made it worse first by calling `freeze_local_seating` in the
  comparison — it CAPTURES when nothing is frozen, so running it every frame
  froze live devices on frame one, which is the very race being fixed.
  ⭐⭐ **the durable lesson: a review that cannot run the code proposes remedies
  that typecheck.** Both of these were plausible, both were specific, and both
  were wrong in a way only execution reveals. Take the FINDING seriously and the
  REMEDY as a hypothesis.
  The original finding:
  ▢ **G1 — unify rollback-session ownership.**
  `LocalSessionOwnership.started: Option<LocalSessionPolicy>` and
  `RollbackSessionOwnership::{LocalSyncTest(SyncTestSettings), External}` can
  disagree: `install_session` sets `External` without clearing the shadow, and
  `maintain_local_session` consults the shadow ALONE — so it can stop or replace
  a session it does not own. `release()` has the inverse bug: it clears the
  shadow without stopping the session.
  ⭐ **the surface is small — measured before starting**: outside
  `local_session.rs` only the dev observatory (reads) and `dev_runtime` (calls
  `release`) touch it, and `RollbackSessionOwnership` is already what
  `lifecycle_commit` gates on. **The design: put the policy INTO the
  `LocalSyncTest` variant and delete `started`**, so there is one authority.
  ⚠ **do not add a "stamp the policy afterwards" step** —
  [[reference_an_authority_that_needs_a_follow_up_call]] is exactly this shape;
  the start must set ownership and policy together.
  ▢ **G2 — the roster and the session owner freeze DIFFERENT topologies.** Both
  in `Update`, no ordering contract; the local owner freezes from connected
  devices, the roster path from decided participants, and they differ for a
  keyboard seat, a spare pad, or a CPU seat. Worse, the running session records
  only the policy, not the `SyncTestSettings`, so a wrong player count persists.
  Fix belongs WITH G1: store the running settings and compare before deciding a
  restart is unnecessary.
  ◐ **G3 — the interim half is DONE (2026-08-04): a conflict is loud.** Item
  catalogs, encounter waves and LDtk converters are still process-global
  `OnceLock`s, but `let _ = GLOBAL.set(v)` no longer swallows a DIFFERENT second
  install.
  ⚠ **identical re-installation stays silent and that is the whole design
  constraint** — two Apps built from the same provider in one test binary do it
  constantly, so a guard that fires on "twice" would be waived within a day.
  ⭐ **for the converters the comparison is the identifier SET, not the function
  pointers**: a closure's address is not stable across rebuilds, so comparing
  pointers would flag every normal case. The ids are what two providers can
  actually disagree about.
  ▢ **the endpoint is unchanged**: these families want to be App resources
  selected through provider context, as the world manifest, character catalog,
  music and SFX already are. ⚠ LDtk conversion runs from pure non-system code
  with no `World`, so its migration needs an explicit converter registry passed
  into the conversion, not a resource lookup.
  ✔ **the encounter SPLIT READER is CLOSED (2026-08-04, `ad779c09c`).**
  `ambition_encounter` now owns an `encounter_waves` schema; the plugin consumes
  `lowered_encounter_waves(prepared())` instead of
  `ron::from_str(include_str!(..)).expect(..)`. One reader, and a file the pack
  refuses can no longer reach a running game.
  ⭐ **and it found the invariant the old reader could not see**, which is the
  whole reason the program says to migrate a family rather than tidy it: an
  encounter authored with ZERO waves parses perfectly and means exactly what
  OMITTING the key means — fall back to marker-derived spawns. The file claims a
  timeline it does not have. Also refused: an empty wave (cleared the instant it
  starts, so it reads as a pause the encounter never takes) and a padded trigger
  id (the lookup is verbatim, so it compiles and is unreachable — the item
  catalog's `dialog_id` class).
  ⛔ **the obstacle was a POLICY PREFIX MATCH, and it is the durable lesson.**
  `engine.encounter-crate-source-purity` forbade the bare string
  `ambition_content`, which is a substring of `ambition_content_pack` — a
  different crate, the engine's content COMPILER that `ambition_characters` and
  `ambition_items` already depend on to own their schemas. The rule was denying
  this capability the right to own its own schema, by accident, through a
  substring. Fixed with the terminator convention the same file already uses
  (`ambition_content::`, `ambition_content;`). **A crate-name ban with no
  terminator bans every crate whose name starts the same way.**
  ▢ the remaining half of the family is G12's boss encounters, which still need a
  schema that can lower a source-keyed COLLECTION rather than one file.
  ✔ **G4 — DONE (2026-08-04). A move started aiming UP fired sideways.**
  `MovePlayback.aim` carries the intent the move started with; the fire frame
  prefers a live edge, then that, then facing. **Three tiers, and the middle one
  is the fix** — the fallback stays as the last resort it was always meant to be.
  ⭐ **captured at BOTH start sites.** Doing only the cancel path would have fixed
  aimed shots for moves that interrupted another one, which is the rarer half —
  the plain trigger path is the common one.
  ⭐ **the POLICY travels with the direction.** A body-local `(1,0)` and a world
  `(1,0)` are different shots under non-default gravity, so storing the vector
  alone would reintroduce the frame confusion `dir_to_world` exists to prevent.
  Checksummed for the same reason `hit_targets` now is.
  ⛔ **probed**: ignoring the captured aim fails with *"the move was started
  aiming UP and fired Vec2(1.0, 0.0)"*. ⚠ **the sibling test cannot see this** —
  it supplies a live `fire` edge on the event frame, which is the tier that
  always worked. The distinguishing input is a captured aim with NO live edge.
  The design and evidence:
  ⭐ **the code's own comment already confirms the review's premise**, and it is
  worth quoting because it means this is not speculative: *"the fallback is the
  COMMON path, not the rare one. `frame.fire` is an EDGE: `clear_edges()` nulls
  it every tick. A ranged move has startup, so by the time its fire frame arrives
  the intent that started it is already gone and this branch is what runs."* D8
  fixed that fallback to use `kin.facing`, which repairs left/right and silently
  flattens every non-horizontal aim.
  **The design, concretely:**
  * `MovePlayback` gains `aim: Option<(Vec2, GameplayFramePolicy)>`, captured at
    `MovePlayback::new` from `control.0.fire` (the site at `moveset/mod.rs:1050`
    already holds `kin`; confirm it holds `ActorControl` too or thread it).
  * the `MoveEventKind::Ranged` arm prefers a LIVE `control.0.fire` (a genuine
    fresh edge this tick), then the captured `aim`, then the facing fallback —
    which stays, but only as the last resort it was always meant to be.
  ⚠ **this is a rollback-registered component gaining a field**, so it needs the
  checksum projection (G8's `encode_ref`) and the schema baseline updated in the
  same change. Not started rather than half-started for exactly that reason.
  The original finding:
  ▢ **G4 — delayed ranged events discard the request's aim.** D8 verified the
  horizontal case and this is the half it did not reach: `MovePlayback` does not
  retain the fire request, so an authored `Ranged` frame falls back to current
  facing and `ControlledBodyLocal`. A move started with an UPWARD aim fires
  horizontally. Capture the resolved intent (direction + policy) at move start.
  ▢ **G7 — Sanic still inherits `sandbox_all()`** through
  `EditableAbilitySet::as_engine()`; the peaceful action set suppresses combat
  but not generic traversal. Both forms need an authored grant. ⚠ what Sanic's
  kit IS remains Jon's call (already an open decision).
  ✔ **G8 — DONE (2026-08-04), both halves.** `encode_ref` projects
  `hit_targets` now, **sorted**, because it is a SET: the same targets struck in
  either order are the same state, and hashing insertion order would report a
  false divergence — which is the failure mode that gets a checksum waived.
  ⭐ **the precedent was in the same file**: `BodyMelee` directly below already
  encodes its own `hit_targets`.
  ⛔ **the comments were worse than the omission.** Both the field and the impl
  claimed the list "is not yet carried across a rollback resume" and described
  `MovePlayback::resumed` rebuilding state — a snapshot engine that no longer
  exists. Under bevy_ggrs the registration is a CLONE snapshot: it was always
  carried, only unchecksummed. **A comment describing a deleted mechanism tells
  the next reader the opposite of the truth**, which is G13's concrete cost.
  The original finding:
  ▢ **G8 — `MovePlayback`'s checksum omits `hit_targets`.** The clone snapshot
  restores it, so this is a CHECKSUM and comment defect, not a restore one: two
  peers can disagree about which target was struck and produce the same hash.
  Canonicalize before hashing, and delete the stale `resumed()` narrative.
  ✔ **G9 — DONE (2026-08-04), and it was worse than "a multiplayer blocker".**
  The message carried no victim, so consumers could only take the last death and
  assume it was theirs. Mary-O had **two** doing that: the death beat read the
  newest message and applied it to the controlled subject, and the life counter
  charged an attempt for ANY death in the frame (`deaths.read().count() > 0`).
  ⛔ **so an enemy dying would have spent one of her lives** the moment anything
  else in the world emitted the fact. Latent today only because emission is
  effectively restricted to the one controlled body.
  ⚠ **the life counter still DRAINS before it filters.** A death that landed
  during a load must not be re-read and charged to the next attempt — an
  invariant that predates this — so the filter narrows what is USED, not what is
  consumed. Getting that backwards would have traded one bug for a subtler one.
  ⭐ **an `Entity` is a SAME-FRAME identity and the field says so.** Right for
  *"was that my body, this tick"*, wrong for a replay or a peer, because Bevy
  recycles indices. Multiplayer attribution wants a participant or the body's
  stable `PresentationSourceId`; naming that in the type is the point of writing
  it down now rather than discovering it later.
  ⭐ **both test fixtures had to name a victim to keep compiling**, which is the
  right pressure: a fixture emitting an unattributed death was emitting something
  the engine cannot produce.
  ✔ **G11 — DONE (2026-08-04). Dialogue choices take ONE tap again.**
  `effective_dialog_tap_mode` promoted touch to `TapToSelectThenConfirm`, so
  every choice on a phone cost two deliberate taps — **and it existed for exactly
  one reason**: activation happened on PRESS, so a finger that landed on a row and
  then slid had already chosen it. Activation is on RELEASE now with a drag
  cancelling the press, so a slide is navigation and a tap is a choice, and the
  promotion has nothing left to protect against.
  ⭐ **the primitive was extracted, not reinvented** — as the review said, the
  repo had it in exactly one place (the kaleidoscope menu's press/move/release
  trio). `RowPress` lives in `ambition_ui_nav` so every selectable list gets it
  rather than one menu.
  ⚠ **the identity is the row INDEX, not an entity**: a windowed list rebuilds
  its rows between press and release, which is why the kaleidoscope stores the
  ACTION at press rather than the entity. Same trap, same answer.
  ⚠ **16px of slop, deliberately generous** — a thumb rolls on a real tap, and a
  threshold tuned on a mouse turns taps into ignored drags, which reads as an
  unresponsive UI: the failure this change exists to remove.
  ⛔ **the test asserting the promotion was REWRITTEN, not deleted.** The
  guarantee it protected — a drag must not activate — is still owed, and it now
  asserts that against `RowPress` directly (press-then-scroll activates nothing;
  release over a different row activates nothing). Deleting it would have removed
  the promotion and the safety together, silently.
  ▢ **residue: the DIALOGUE is done, other surfaces are not.** `RowPress` is
  shared but only dialogue consumes it; the kaleidoscope keeps its own copy of
  the same idea, and other menus still activate on press. Converging them is the
  rest of the review's "shared UI navigation" ask.
  ◐ **G12 — the SOURCE TABLE is collapsed (2026-08-04); the dual reader remains.**
  ✔ **paths and bytes were two lists joined by INDEX** — the bytes a bare
  `&[&str]` in `bosses/mod.rs`, the paths written out again in `pack.rs` beside
  `BOSS_ENCOUNTER_RONS[0]`…`[8]`. Reordering either attached a file's contents to
  another file's diagnostic path, and **nothing would have failed**: the runtime
  resolves rows by internal id, so a compile error would just have pointed at the
  wrong file forever. `BOSS_ENCOUNTERS: &[(path, bytes)]` is the one table now;
  `pack.rs` extends from it and the catalog builder takes a derived accessor.
  ⚠ **positional coupling across two files is the SHAPE, not the count** —
  maintainable at nine, silently wrong at ten.
  ⚠ **the append moved these sources to the END of the pack's list**; verified
  the whole suite green rather than assuming source order does not reach a
  fingerprint.
  ✔ **the bigger half is CLOSED (2026-08-04, `7cd0b0f97`) — see G1 PICK 8.** The
  schema lowers one `BossEncounterSpec` per file and merges them into the
  `BTreeMap` the catalog already held; `from_prepared_behaviors` became
  `from_prepared` and takes that book. Nine `ron::from_str` calls and
  `boss_encounter_rons()` are gone.
  ▢ *(historical, for the shape)*: the schema lowered ONE artifact, so
  boss encounters were registered as authoring-only and the runtime REPARSED the
  raw RON. Compiler validation on one path, runtime loading on another. Needed the
  schema to lower a source-keyed collection — the same want as G3's encounter
  split reader.
  ⚠ **G13 — comment style.** The review objects that production comments carry
  investigation chronology. **Taken as calibration, not argued**: the durable
  invariant, the authority, and the non-obvious consequence stay; dates, model
  names and failed-hypothesis narration belong in planning. ⭐ and it names a
  concrete cost — the stale `MovePlayback` prose in G8 actively misleads.

- ✔ **D22. Jon's Mary-O notes (2026-08-04), taken FIRST at his instruction:**
  *"the powerups should rise, behind the blocks, so they look like they emerge
  from them… A used questionmark block should get an inert texture. The
  questionmark block should change to an interobang instead of a question mark,
  and we should tweak the pipe sprites so they are copper colored instead of
  green."*
  ✔ **emergence.** Items draw at Z 8, blocks at Z 0, so a pickup was always in
  FRONT — it appeared whole, pasted on top, the instant the block was struck.
  `WorldItem::emerging` is a fact the spawner owns; while set the item draws
  behind the world and climbs into view through the block's top edge. **A fact,
  not a timer**, because only the spawner knows what it emerged from.
  ✔ **the used block gets `SpentBlockTile`**, not a fallback to the kind's plain
  stone: a used block that looks like a wall hides its own history from a player
  deciding whether it is worth hitting.
  ⛔ **the interrobang took THREE passes and Jon named each miss exactly.**
  (1) a thin hook tapering into a stem — a plain question mark. (2) bolder and
  longer, which he called: *"it looks like an eroteme with a long stem."*
  ⭐ **the lesson is in his correction**: *"an interrobang has the ! stroke CROSS
  the hook of the eroteme, often at a very slight slant so you can see the two
  are composed."* Sharing one stroke between two marks is what makes them
  illegible as two. So the hook owns no stem at all; the exclamation is a
  separate slanted stroke over it, starting above the crown, and the hook's tail
  runs THROUGH it and out the far side. Verified in a running frame.
  ✔ **pipes are copper, and the verification found MORE than the picture would
  have (2026-08-04).** One shared palette drove both the tileset and the props,
  and the constants were RENAMED with the colour.
  ⛔ **the rename left TWO inline greens behind** — `(55, 188, 101)` written out
  three times and `(14, 75, 40)` once, which no constant rename can reach. Named
  now (`PIPE_COPPER_SHEEN`, `PIPE_COPPER_SHADOW`), which is what a constant is
  FOR.
  ⭐ **found by reading the colour HISTOGRAM, not by looking.** 224 stray green
  pixels in a 16px tile are invisible to the eye and unmistakable in a count.
  ⛔ **and the first check PASSED**: the pipe BODY was clean while the pipe TOP
  still had 159, because the cap and the body are separate targets drawn by
  separate functions. **Checking one sprite of a family is not checking the
  family.** Final sweep: every pipe sprite, zero pixels whose green dominates.
  ✔ **`capture_mary_o --walk N` exists now** — hold Right for N frames before the
  shot, so a side-scroller's later screens are reachable at all.
  ⚠ **walking alone was not enough and the failure was DISGUISED**: holding only
  Right, she died to the first snake and the capture came back at the SPAWN POINT
  with a life spent — which looks exactly like the flag not working. A periodic
  hop gets past the early enemies; it is not a route, and a capture that needs one
  wants a real `--press` sequence like `capture_scene`'s.
  ⭐ **incidental confirmation**: those walk captures show LIVES counting 3 → 2 →
  1 as she dies, which is G9's per-victim life counter working in a running game
  rather than only in a test.

- ✔ **D23. The suite's status file could report a run that never happened.**
  Twice on 2026-08-04 a `run_tests.py` invocation failed to START — my shell was
  left inside the renderer submodule, so `python3 scripts/run_tests.py` resolved
  to a path that does not exist and exited 2 — and the reader I typed printed the
  PREVIOUS run's per-job table as if it were current. **Both times the table said
  5/5 green.**
  ⚠ **I caught it by the timings being byte-identical to the run before**, which
  is luck, not method. A reader that cannot tell a fresh result from an old one
  is the same class as a check that cannot fail: it reports the success condition
  regardless of what happened.
  ⭐ **the WRITER was never the problem** — the payload already carries `pid` and
  `started`. The gap is that there is no reader, so every caller hand-rolls a
  `json.load(...)["completed"]` and leaves the freshness check out. **A fact
  everyone re-derives is a fact someone will derive wrong.**
  ✔ **`scripts/last_test_run.py` is the reader now**: it prints the verdict, and
  it REFUSES rather than reports when the run is stale, still running with a dead
  pid, or unfinished. Exit code is the answer, so a caller cannot accidentally
  read past a refusal.

- ✔ **D24. Sanic is photographed, and the picture found a SECOND cause of Jon's
  button-text complaint (2026-08-04).**
  ⭐ **`capture_sanic` is the capture seam's SECOND caller, which is the point.**
  A seam with one consumer is a guess. It is ~120 lines and all of them are
  Sanic's — which app to build, when its world is worth a picture; everything
  hard (target, camera adoption, readback, PNG, exit) is
  `ambition_render::capture`. Three edits, all known in advance from Mary-O's:
  the `OffscreenGpu` mode, the `ScheduleRunnerPlugin`, and the gameplay route.
  ⛔ **the legend was a HARDCODED STRING naming a key nothing binds.**
  `"START   Z: JUMP   DOWN+X: REV   RELEASE DOWN: DASH   D: SUPER"` — the preset
  binds jump Z, attack X, dash C, **special G**, and no `D` at all. So Jon's *"in
  sanic the button text doesn't match what the controls really are"* was **two
  bugs**: D17's touch-button labels, and this sign. Fixing the first would never
  have fixed the second, and nothing in the code connected them.
  ⭐ **it reads the same table the BINDINGS do now**, so a rebind moves the sign
  with it. `key_name` joins `ambition_input`'s chosen public surface rather than
  the `presets` module being opened — a HUD wants exactly one function from it.
  ✔ **swept for siblings and there are NONE.** A hardcoded key legend is the kind
  of defect that usually comes in threes, so I grepped for strings naming a key
  beside a verb across `game/` and `crates/`: Sanic's was the only one. The other
  hits are descriptive prose ("A/B profiling", "arrows + Z/X/C"), not signs a
  player reads as a binding. **A negative sweep result is worth one line** — it
  stops the next reader re-running the same hunt.
  ⚠ **and D17's blind fix is confirmed by a picture at last**: the legend renders
  `G: SUPER`. That fix shipped without anyone seeing the screen it was about.
  ⛔ **I repeated a mistake I had committed a lesson about an hour earlier**: the
  new `build_windowed_demo_app_with_home` landed OUTSIDE
  `#[cfg(feature = "visible")]`, exactly as Mary-O's had. rust-analyzer said so
  and I caught it with `--all-targets` this time instead of shipping it.

- ✔ **D16. `capture_scene` could not draw a frame — FOUR param-validation panics
  in a row, and the checker built for the class had WAIVED one of them.** Found
  while trying to LOOK at D14d, which is the only way a visual change gets
  verified on a machine with no display, so this blocked the lane rather than
  sitting beside it. Each fix revealed the next; all four are the same shape — a
  system registered into a composition that does not install what its params
  require — and none is a test failure anywhere, because the shipped app installs
  everything.
  1. `sync_portal_quality_budget` took `Res<ResolvedVisualQuality>` from
     `ambition_app`'s plugin file while `VisualQualityPlugin` owned it. **The
     comment three lines above it records the IDENTICAL defect being fixed for its
     sibling `sync_resolved_visual_quality` on 07-31** — the fix was applied to one
     and not to the other, one system later in the same file. Moved into the
     plugin, behind `portal_render`.
  2. `spawn_vanity_card` took `Res<ActiveShellSequence>`; `ambition_content`'s
     presentation does not drag the game shell in behind it.
  3. `reload_visual_quality_assets_on_scale_change` — same missing resource, and
     the real root: **this composition never installed `VisualQualityPlugin` at
     all.** Fixed at the source by adding the owner beside the systems that need
     it, exactly as `UiFontsPlugin::ensure_installed` does two lines above.
  4. `animate_vanity_card` held a `MessageWriter<ShellSequenceCommand>`. ⛔ **my
     own fix for 2 caused this one**: I gated only the spawner, reasoning that the
     other two would find no roots — true of their BODIES and beside the point,
     since Bevy validates every param before a system body decides it has nothing
     to do. All three are gated now.
  ⭐ **THE FINDING, and it is about the instrument, not the bug.**
  `scripts/check_engine_systems_are_engine_installed.py` exists for this class and
  found the 07-31 sibling. It did not find #1 — because #1 is in its WAIVER list,
  as *"portal, feature-gated, Ambition's."* That waiver is a correct answer to the
  question the checker asks (*should this engine system live in an engine
  plugin?* — a placement question) and says nothing about the question that bit
  (*does every param this system requires have an owner in every composition that
  registers it?* — a coherence question). A waiver silences the checker's
  question, and the checker's question was never the dangerous one. #3 was
  invisible for the checker's own stated reason: it is a bare local identifier.
  ✔ **the follow-up: DONE — the probe existed and nothing ran it.**
  `capture_scene` boots a real composition and panics on precisely this class —
  four times in one sitting. Nothing in `run_tests.py` boots it, so the class is
  detectable only when someone happens to want a picture. A headless boot of each
  shipped composition, asserting no param-validation panic in N frames, would
  catch every one of these and cost seconds. Filed rather than done, because it
  belongs beside a decision about which compositions are the shipped set.
  It is a `run_tests.py` job now — *"acceptance: the render composition draws a
  frame"* — in the DEFAULT plan rather than behind `--heavy`, because opt-in
  coverage is what let `modules_md.py` sit with a check mode nothing ever called.
  ⭐ **the hole it fills, stated plainly:** every app-boot job in the suite is
  `--headless`, and so is every app-boot test in the workspace. The suite proved
  the SIMULATION composes and had never once proved the presentation does.
  ⚠ **and my own fix for #1 then broke `ambition_render`'s parallax tests** —
  `sync_portal_quality_budget` BRIDGES two plugins, reading
  `ResolvedVisualQuality` and writing `PortalCaptureQualityBudget`, so putting it
  beside one owner moved the panic to compositions lacking the other. Invisible
  under `cargo test -p ambition_render` (feature off) and red under `--workspace`
  (unification turns it on). Gated on the half it does not own. **Co-location is
  not "put the system with THE owner" when a bridge has two.**

- ◐ **D15. TOUCH + DIALOGUE ON A PIXEL 5 (Jon, 2026-08-03), from a GPT report he
  pasted into `docs/reviews/gpt-2026-08-03-touch-dialog-report.md`.** Four claimed
  failures; all four verified against source before touching anything.
  ⭐ **Jon's steer, verbatim, and it shaped the whole approach:** *"my report does
  not necessarily take priority over high value architecture that would make
  these issues easier to structurally address… Can you land most of the fixes in
  it blind? it's tricky because I don't know how to test outside of running it on
  android."*
  ✔ **failure 4 — hidden controls still hit-test. FIXED, and it was ONE TERM.**
  Visibility asked `(gameplay || always_available) && touch_action_available(..)`;
  the touch mask asked `touch_action_available(..)` alone, under a comment
  claiming *"one availability source of truth"*. During dialogue the prompt
  nominates Jump/Interact as confirm buttons, so the mask kept them live while
  the ownership term hid them — **invisible buttons over the dialogue panel**,
  which is precisely *"touching an apparently empty area advances dialogue"*.
  `touch_action_live(action, prompt, gameplay)` is the predicate now and both
  halves call it. Probed red by edit.
  ✔ **failure 3 — the HUD hides what dialogue allows. FIXED, same term from the
  other side.** `publish_frontend_context_prompt` already rewrites the prompt to
  `Menu` with the owning context's submit label when a non-gameplay context owns
  the seat, and `touch_action_available` then admits only the menu confirm + menu
  row — so **every gameplay verb was ALREADY hidden by the prompt term**, which is
  the case the ownership term was added for (2026-07-28, the launcher's capturing
  claim). ANDing it over a menu prompt was redundant there and wrong here. It now
  applies only to a GAMEPLAY-context prompt, which is the one thing it still buys:
  a stale prompt while nobody owns gameplay.
  ⚠ **my own test failed on that second fix and was right to** — it asserted the
  SYMPTOM ("Jump is hidden during dialogue") rather than the invariant ("drawn and
  touchable are the same question"). Same mistake as the three fixture rows above,
  made an hour after writing them up. The tests are a property sweep now: every
  action × every context × both ownerships, `drawn == touchable`.
  ◐ **failure 1 — two-tap dialogue rows. HALF LANDED, and the other half is the
  real mechanism.** The platform default is now `SingleTapWithDestructiveGuard`
  everywhere (that variant already existed and is exactly what the report asks
  for: single tap, second press only for a destructive row). ⛔ **but
  `ambition_dialog::effective_dialog_tap_mode` PROMOTES it back to
  `TapToSelectThenConfirm` whenever the active input is Touch**, so dialogue was
  two-tap on any touchscreen regardless of platform. That promotion is the actual
  cause.
  ▢ **and it cannot be deleted until activation is RELEASE-based**, which is the
  report's own remedy. `resolve_row_pointer` confirms on Bevy's
  `Interaction::Pressed`, so removing the promotion today trades "two taps needed"
  for "a scroll gesture activates the row it started on" — worse in a long list.
  ⛔ **and tap-on-release is the one thing here that genuinely needs the device**:
  on touch there is no hover, so a release over the row and a drag off it can both
  arrive as `Interaction::None`, and guessing wrong makes dialogue impossible to
  activate at all. That is worse than two taps, so it is written down rather than
  guessed.
  ✔ **failure 2 — the recentering moving target. FIXED for the half that survives
  single-tap.** `visible_window_start` is a pure function of `selected`, so the
  window recentered on EVERY selection change and the touched row moved.
  `scroll_into_view(previous_start, selected, total, capacity)` keeps it put
  while the selection is visible and otherwise moves to the NEAR edge — one step
  per step, so a held direction scrolls instead of paging.
  ⭐ **the state is a `Local` on the dialogue's rebuild pass, and the placement is
  the argument**: the window start is a function of the selection AND of how many
  rows fit on this screen, and only presentation knows the second half. Nothing in
  the sim reads it, so it is not rollback state — a rewind re-derives it through
  the same rule on the next rebuild.
  ⚠ one test re-baselined narrowly (`[4..8]` centered → `[3..7]` near-edge); its
  subject — only a window renders, slots carry ABSOLUTE indices — is untouched and
  still asserted, and the test says so in place.
  ▢ **what remains of failure 2 is the report's other requirement**: resolve
  activation by a STABLE CHOICE ID rather than by the entity rendered for one
  frame. Not needed while the window holds still, and it is the belt to that
