  braces — worth doing when the release-based work below opens the same files.
  ✔ **failure 3's sibling — the STICK. CHECKED, the report was right, FIXED.**
  `bind_touch_virtual_inputs` maps `TouchVirtualStick` to BOTH `A::Move` and
  `A::MenuStick`, and `read_joystick_messages` writes the axis ungated — so while
  a menu owns the seat the stick genuinely steers the selection, and hiding it on
  `gameplay_owned()` left a phone player **no way to move a dialogue selection at
  all**. Which is also why touching rows directly mattered so much: it was the
  only route left.
  ⭐ **the asymmetry is the interesting part and it is why these were two bugs and
  not one.** Same `gameplay_owned()` term, opposite failure modes: a hidden Bevy
  UI node takes no drags, so the STICK was genuinely dead while invisible; the
  BUTTONS hit-test a fixed rectangle themselves, so they stayed live while
  invisible. One term, one half drawn by the UI and one half hit-testing itself.
  The Empty context still hides the stick — neither binding routes there, and the
  standing rule holds.
  ✔ **D15b. Jon, 2026-08-03, verbatim:** *"the trick is that space for dialogue
  on a phone is so tight… I am able to navigate it on the phone, but it's
  frustrating and error prone and relies on the back button in the top corner,
  which we might not always want there. Just having the controls on screen would
  help a lot. another thing to note is that it would be nice if we had a test
  that proved the on screen buttons displayed the right text for the dialogue
  menu (i.e. accept / back). Not sure how far we got in the input refactor
  roadmap to support that. I know in sanic the button text doesn't match what the
  controls really are."*
  ✔ **the test exists now** (`every_button_a_dialogue_shows_reads_what_it_does`),
  driven through `update_button_verb_from_prompt` → `render_touch_button_text` so
  it asserts the RENDERED text. Buttons are seeded with gameplay verbs first,
  because that is the state a dialogue opens from. It asserts over the LIVE
  buttons — a stale label on a hidden one is invisible; on a shown one it is the
  complaint — which ties it to the same `touch_action_live` the overlay draws and
  hit-tests with.
  ⭐ **it also pins "just having the controls on screen"**: a dialogue showing
  fewer than three live controls fails the test rather than failing on a phone.
  Under the old ownership rule only two were live.
  ⚠ **the roadmap answer he asked for**: the vocabulary is already there — a
  Menu/Dialogue prompt carries `menu_confirm`, `Jump`/`Interact` wear it, and the
  menu row is statically "Menu"/"Back". What was missing was anything asserting
  it, not the wiring.
  ◐ **Sanic's mismatch: PROBED, and it is NOT in the published prompt.** Booting
  the standalone Sanic host and reading `ControlPrompt` after the body activates:
  | slot | label | binding |
  |---|---|---|
  | Jump | "Jump" | `Z` |
  | Attack | "Spin Dash" | `X` |
  | Dash | "Dash" | `C` |
  | Blink | "Blink" | `A` |
  | Interact | "Interact" | `F` |
  | Utility | "Fly Toggle" | `D` |
  | QuickAction | "Shield" | `E` |
  Every entry carries both halves, so the seam the prompt's own docstring is about
  (verb from the scheme, key from the live `InputMap`, never hand-written) is
  working. The complaint is not reproduced HERE.
  ⛔ **and my first run of this probe produced a FALSE finding I nearly recorded**:
  with default features it printed `NO SeatBindings resource at all` and every
  binding `None`. `ambition_demo_sanic_app` is `default = []` and the input stack
  is behind `input` (which `visible` pulls in), so the absence was an artifact of
  the TEST BUILD, not of the shipped binary. ⚠ **`cargo test -p <demo>_app`
  without `--features input` is not measuring the game anybody plays** — the same
  class as the union-vs-default finding in C1, and it would have sent someone to
  fix a resource that is installed.
  ⛔⛔ **FOUND IT, and the prompt is not lying — the ABILITY AUTHORITY is wrong.**
  `sanic_setup` passes `editable_abilities` into `simulation_world`'s
  `SimulationSetup`, so the body's abilities are
  `EditableAbilitySet` — and `EditableAbilitySet::default()` is
  **`ae::AbilitySet::sandbox_all()`**, every ability on. The prompt then honestly
  reports what the body can do, which is why a hedgehog is offered `Blink`,
  `Fly Toggle` and `Shield`. **The buttons match the body; the body is the
  sandbox's give-me-everything set instead of Sanic's kit.**
  ⭐ that is a far more useful statement than "the text is wrong", because it says
  where to fix it: Sanic should declare its own `AbilitySet` (roll, spin dash,
  jump — the kit its levels are designed around), and the prompt needs no change
  at all.
  ⚠ **`EditableAbilitySet` lives in `ambition_dev_tools`** — it is the dev menu's
  live ability-toggle mirror — and reaches the demo through a
  `runtime::demo_fixture::EditableAbilitySet` re-export, so the call site does not
  look like it is reading a dev tool. Same trap as
  [[reference_orphan_rule_adjudicates_crate_placement]]: **a re-export path lies
  about ownership.** A developer's toggle panel being the ability authority for a
  shipped demo is the product/tooling boundary this repo keeps separate
  everywhere else.
  ⛔ **and it is not Sanic-specific — NO DEMO HAS AN ABILITY AUTHORITY.** Mary-O's
  setup passes the same `editable_abilities` into the same `SimulationSetup`, and
  the only place that ever seeds the resource is `ambition_app`
  (`resources.rs:197`, `EditableAbilitySet::from(sandbox_data.abilities)`). So:
  | where | what a demo body's abilities are |
  |---|---|
  | standalone `build_demo_app` | nothing seeds it → `Default` → **`sandbox_all()`** |
  | inside `ambition_app` | **the SANDBOX's** authored abilities |
  **Sanic's abilities are never Sanic's, and Mary-O's are never Mary-O's** —
  they are the sandbox's kit or every ability in the engine.
  ⛔ **RETRACTED, same hour: Mary-O is NOT affected and my claim was wrong.**
  Probed her standalone host and her published prompt is three entries —
  `Jump` (Z), `Modifier` "Run" (X), `Interact` (F) — not `sandbox_all()`. She
  authors a kit: `catalog.ability_set(MARY_O_CHARACTER_ID)` composes to
  `AbilityGrant::RunJump` alone, and her character kit outranks the editable set.
  So the gap is real for a demo that authors NO grant list (Sanic) and absent for
  one that does. ⚠ I generalised from one demo to "no demo has an authority"
  without checking the second — the same one-file generalisation this ledger has
  now recorded three times.
  ▢ **the fix shape, and the one decision in it that is Jon's**: a demo should
  DECLARE its kit and the editable set should be seeded from that — "editable"
  means a developer can then change it live, which is the opposite of it being
  the source. The engineering is small (one field on the demo's setup, seeded
  where `ambition_app` already seeds its own). **What Sanic's kit IS — roll, spin
  dash, jump, and what else — is product scope and belongs to Jon**, which is why
  this row stops at the seam rather than inventing a kit.
  ✔ **CHECKED 2026-08-04, and it was NOT the shape this row guessed — it was
  worse.** The row expected a button wearing a verb from the wrong slot. The two
  tables (`touch_button_slot` in `bevy_plugin.rs`, `touch_bindings` in
  `virtual_device.rs`) agree on every slot they both name. What nothing checked
  is that they name the SAME SET: **`Modifier` had a slot and no binding.**
  ⛔ **so on a phone Mary-O could not RUN.** Her published prompt is exactly
  `Jump`, `Modifier` "Run", `Interact` (D15c). The Modifier button was therefore
  available, labelled "Run" from her scheme, and hit-tested — and pressing it
  sent nothing, because `touch_bindings` never bound `A::Modifier`. Keyboard
  binds it (X, the default preset) and gamepad binds it (LeftTrigger2). **The one
  verb a Mario-like is unplayable without, missing on the device Jon plays on.**
  ⭐ **the guard is the missing implication, one-way**: a button with a
  `ControlSlot` is drawn, labelled and hit-tested, therefore it must be bound.
  Not the converse — `Start` and `Reset` are bound and carry no gameplay slot,
  which is correct, they are shell verbs. Probed by removing the binding again.
  ⚠ **and the row's advice to defer was wrong in a way worth keeping**: it said
  this was *"cheaper to check once the ability set is the demo's, because most of
  the confusing buttons will be gone."* The defect was never about there being
  too many buttons to look at — it is two tables in two files with nothing tying
  them together, and a set comparison found it in one reading.

- ✔ **D15c. Jon, 2026-08-03: *"I did notice maryo has more than 2 on screen
  buttons when I played yesterday. that shouldn't be the case for her. Fire and
  run are tied to the same button."*** REPRODUCED and LOCATED in one probe.
  Her published prompt is exactly three: `Jump` (Z), `Modifier` "Run" (X), and
  **`Interact` (F)** — so the extra button is Interact, and the run/fire half is
  already right (`Modifier` is one slot whose label grows, and the slot's own
  doc gives *"Run"* → *"Run / Spark"* as the intended example).
  ⛔ **the cause is an explicit line in `derive_action_scheme`: *"Interact is
  universal — every controllable body can attempt it."*** So it is a decision,
  not a leak — and the decision is an assumption about the GAME, not about the
  body. A game with nothing to interact with shows a button that never does
  anything, which is the same rule the touch overlay already enforces for every
  other verb (*a control nobody can use must not be on screen*).
  ✔✔ **BUILT (2026-08-04). Interact is an ABILITY now, and Mary-O's HUD is two
  buttons.** Probed after: her published prompt is `Jump` + `Modifier "Run"`, and
  the third entry is gone.
  ⭐ **`AbilitySet::interact` was the right home after all, and the composition
  mechanics are what make it right rather than what ruled it out.** `compose`
  folds grants from `NONE`, so a character that authors a grant list gets exactly
  what it asked for — and `AbilityGrant::RunJump`, whose own doc calls it *"the
  minimal kit a platformer protagonist needs"*, carries no talk verb, faithfully
  to the game it is named after. `basic` / `sane_subset` / `sandbox_all` all keep
  it, so **the only bodies that lose Interact are the ones that authored a
  restricted kit — Mary-O alone today.** The Hall, every NPC conversation and
  every default body are untouched.
  ⚠ the earlier note here said AbilitySet was the wrong home because "Interact is
  an affordance of the WORLD". Half right and it led the wrong way: the world half
  (*which* interactable) was always resolved at press time by `ActionGate::Interact`,
  and what remained — does this body have the verb at all — is exactly a
  capability. **Cost: one field, four constructors, `union`/`intersect`, the
  snapshot codec, and ONE broken literal (the dev-tools mirror).** The two designs
  below were sized at 61 and 12 edit sites respectively.
  ✔ the kit test now asserts it: `!mary_o_kit.interact`, beside the double-jump /
  blink / dash / attack exclusions it already pinned. That is the assertion that
  would have caught Jon's report.
  ⚠ **`serde(default = "yes")`, not `bool::default()`** — data predating the field
  describes a body that COULD interact, so a missing field meaning "no interact"
  would silently take talking away from everything loaded from data without
  failing anywhere. The shipped `platformer_defaults.ron` caught it (14 app_it
  tests, one root cause) and now states it explicitly.
  ⭐⭐ **and it exposed a test asserting the OPPOSITE of the availability rule.**
  `the_title_screen_does_not_show_gameplay_touch_buttons` demanded that nothing
  but Start/Reset be visible on the game-select screen, on the premise that
  gameplay verbs *"do nothing there"*. Measured: that screen publishes
  `ControlPrompt { context: Menu, menu_confirm: Some("Play") }`, and
  `touch_action_available`'s Menu branch has ALWAYS admitted the confirm buttons —
  so Jump and Interact are the **Play button** there. **The overlay agreed with
  the test rather than with the rule**, which is precisely the divergence Jon hit
  from a phone: no on-screen way to confirm, so the corner back button was the
  only route. Re-baselined to the invariant that was always meant (nothing
  advertised that does nothing) plus the half it could not state: **the screen
  must OFFER a confirm.**
  `app_it` **287 / 0**.
  ▢ **the DESIGN alternatives, kept because they were sized and would otherwise be
  re-derived:**
  * ⛔ **not `AbilitySet`.** Every grant composes from a zeroed set, so a new
    `interact` flag is either cleared for everybody or defaulted on and inert.
  * ◐ **`ActionSet` is the right SHAPE** — its fields are already
    `melee: Option<…>`, `ranged: Option<…>`, `special: Option<…>`, with `None`
    meaning "no capability", so `interact: bool` sits naturally beside them and
    `derive_action_scheme` already takes it. ⚠ **but the reach is unverified**:
    Mary-O's row declares `default_action_set: "peaceful"`, and v3's row records
    that `default_action_set` describes *"its Hall pedestal / NPC face, NOT what
    the worn home box can do"* — so authoring it there may never touch the body
    the player drives.
    ✔ **ANSWERED: it DOES reach the worn body.** `character_runtime/presentation.rs`
    inserts `ActionSet` on the body from `prepared.kit.action_set()` — the same
    `prepared` pass that installs `AuthoredHurtboxes` and `SpritePosedBody`, both
    of which were verified reaching the worn player earlier today. So `ActionSet`
    is a viable home and the design is unblocked.
    ⚠ **one detail left for the implementer**: that insert is
    `if let Some(action_set) = …`, so a kit providing none leaves the body without
    one — and `derive_action_scheme` takes `Option<&ActionSet>`. The default when
    absent must therefore stay "interacts", or every body without a kit silently
    loses the verb. Mary-O then declares `interact: false` on hers.
  ▢ **the fix has a blast radius worth stating before anyone starts**: the Hall
  of Characters and every NPC conversation depend on Interact existing, so it
  cannot simply stop being universal. The shape is that a body (or its game)
  DECLARES it, defaulting to on for anything with interactables — and Mary-O
  declares no.
  ✔ **the PIPE precondition is checked and SATISFIED — she does not need it.**
  `level_1_2.rs` records Jon's own rule: *"[pipes] stay directional presses
  (Jon's rule — a pipe answers UP or DOWN, never a [button])"*, and `lib.rs`
  adds that you enter one by TOUCHING its mouth. Nothing in Mary-O routes through
  Interact, so removing it costs her nothing.
  ▢ **what is left is one engineering choice: where the declaration LIVES.**
  `derive_action_scheme` upserts Interact unconditionally between the movement
  actions and the combat ones, so the lever must be one of its four inputs.
  ⛔ **not `AbilitySet`** — every grant composes from a zeroed set, so a new
  `interact` flag would either be cleared for everybody (the Hall included) or
  defaulted on and therefore inert. Interact is an affordance of the WORLD rather
  than a capability of the body, which argues against that home regardless. The
  `ActionSet` is the per-character declaration of what a body's actions ARE and
  is already an input — the candidate to size first.
  ⚠ **deliberately not started at the end of a long session**: it is cross-cutting
  on a path where getting it wrong silently removes Interact from the Hall and
  every NPC conversation, and the symptom (a button that does nothing) is much
  milder than that failure mode.

  ◐ **and this row's own scope: Sanic's GAMEPLAY prompt is still not covered.**
  Its MENU path is, as of D17's
  `a_menu_with_no_authored_verb_still_never_reads_a_gameplay_one`, which drives
  `menu_confirm: None` — the input that distinguishes the broken code, and the
  one the earlier dialogue test never supplied. What remains uncovered is a
  GAMEPLAY-context prompt (labels come from the controlled subject's action
  scheme via `prompt.entries`), and this test is about the menu path. ⚠ do not
  read it as covering Sanic — the two contexts take different branches of the
  same match.
  ✔ **that hole is CLOSED by D17 (2026-08-04), and it turned out not to be
  harmless.** The row read it right — in a Menu/Dialogue context only
  `is_menu_confirm_button` got a label, so every other button kept a stale verb —
  and called it harmless because those buttons are hidden there. **The confirm
  buttons themselves are not hidden, and they had the same bug**: with no
  authored `menu_confirm` the `.then(..).flatten()` wrote nothing and they kept
  the gameplay verb, which is Sanic's menu reading "Spin Dash".
  ⭐ **the fix is the one this row named** — the menu branch answers for EVERY
  button now. `ButtonVerb` is a struct (permanent spawn fallback +
  `Option<String>` current) and the update writes the `None` too, so a button the
  prompt has nothing to say about resolves to its spawn label instead of stale
  text. **Making the failure unrepresentable rather than gating it** is what also
  covers the next thing that shows one.

  ⭐ **the testing route Jon asked about, answered**: the touch HUD's availability
  logic is a pure function of `(action, prompt, gameplay)` and is now covered
  headlessly by the property sweep — no device needed for failures 3 and 4. The
  raw-touch path also has a desktop mouse mirror already
  (`update_buttons_from_interactions`'s cursor hit-test), which is the "mouse as
  touch proxy" he suggested, and it is what the end-to-end tests drive.

---

## Lane E — the couch-input test that only fails in a full run

- ✔ **E1. SOLVED (`a32912480`), and the ledger's own conclusion is retired.**
  It fails under **LOAD**, not in a full binary run: three full `app_it` runs
  alone are green; **two concurrent full runs fail in BOTH processes, every
  time.** A full run merely makes contention likely.
  ⛔ **the first suspect was wrong and was probed rather than believed.** The
  per-user `~/.local/share/ambition/{settings,sandbox_save,developer}.ron` really
  are read and written by every app test in parallel — a real hazard, kept as
  E2 — but running the two concurrent suites with separate `AMBITION_DATA_DIR`s
  changed nothing.
  ⭐ the channel is `TimeUpdateStrategy::Automatic`: `app.update()` advances the
  clock by REAL time, so "40 updates" is an unknown amount of simulation, and
  this module asserts the DISTANCE two fighters moved. **Third occurrence of one
  defect** — `shell_host_startup` pins for this reason, `shell_host_rendered` was
  fixed for it, and `dev/journals/code_smells.md` already states the lesson.
  ⚠ **and the pin was checked for MASKING, because the assertion is a ratio.**
  The failure read 99.80px against 252.18px (0.40, over the 0.25 bar); pinned,
  under the same load, it reads **1.44px against 116.29px** (0.012). No
  cross-talk — the 99.80px was residual motion over an unknown window.
  ⚠ so **"a keyboard and a pad drove the same fighter" never happened.** The
  couch bug class is real and has bitten before; this instance was the harness.

- ✔ **E2. CLOSED (`b218f53a4`, `9754e90ca`).** A windowless host now writes its
  own private state directory.
  ⭐ **the rule already existed for the other side effect**: `build_visible_app`
  switches `NoWindow`/`OffscreenGpu` to `AudioOutputMode::Recording` so it does
  not play into the user's speakers. Writing the user's files is the same class
  and now takes the same branch — one change covering all 55 test files with no
  per-test ritual, and principled rather than a test hack.
  ⚠ **`developer.ron` kept moving after the other two stopped**, because
  `ambition_dev_tools` resolved its path from the environment on its own. That is
  what "an ambient global has more readers than you think" looks like when
  checked instead of assumed. Proven by timestamp afterwards: a batch of
  shell_host + smash + duel tests leaves all three files untouched.
- ✔ **E2b. FIXED and now GUARDED (`tests/isolated_persistence.rs`, 2026-08-03).**
  `PersistenceSchedulePlugin` loads settings AND the save at `Startup` and
  autosaves both, and the directory was per-USER — so `app_it` tests shared three
  mutable files with each other, with every other worktree, and with any
  concurrent session. It did not cause E1 and it was still a channel nobody
  declared.
  ✔ the fix was NOT `AMBITION_DATA_DIR` per test, which the row proposed: reading
  a path from the environment at every call is what made it ambient in the first
  place, and a test cannot opt out of a global. `PersistenceRoot` makes it APP
  STATE, defaulting to the platform dir, and a windowless host inserts
  `isolated()` beside `AudioOutputMode::Recording` — the same rule for the other
  side effect a non-session App must not have.
  ⭐ **the guard has two halves because one would rot.** "Not the player's
  directory" is compared against `PersistenceRoot::default()` rather than a
  hardcoded path, so it cannot drift from what it guards; and a second test proves
  two Apps in ONE process get different roots — a single fixed temp path would
  pass the first check while putting every App in a binary back on shared files,
  which is the original defect with a new directory name.

- ✔ **E1b. The same item as E1, closed there (`a32912480`) — and its own PREMISE
  was the thing that was wrong.** This row records the original report:
  `a_keyboard_player_and_a_pad_player_drive_different_fighters` "passes in
  isolation and fails when the whole `app_it` binary runs", diagnosed as *something
  earlier in the binary leaves seat or pad state this test then reads*.
  ⭐ **it is not the binary, it is LOAD** — three full `app_it` runs alone are
  green; two CONCURRENT full runs fail in both processes, every time. A full run
  merely makes contention likely, and "shared state left by an earlier test" was a
  reasonable story that the isolation/full-run framing made hard to leave.
  ⭐ the actual cause was an unpinned `TimeUpdateStrategy`, which makes
  `app.update()` a unit of WALL CLOCK, so a loaded machine changes what a frame
  means. Pinning it took the cross-talk from 0.40 to 0.012 of a body-width.
  ⚠ **and the row's ⛔ held**: no serial-test attribute was added. It would have
  hidden a real timing dependency behind a scheduling constraint, and the couch
  bug class this test guards would have gone back to being invisible.

- ✔ **E3. THE FOURTH LANDING IS THE LAST ONE (`fe28ceb38`).** E1 recorded that
  the unpinned `TimeUpdateStrategy` was the *third* occurrence of one defect and
  that `dev/journals/code_smells.md` already stated the lesson. It landed a
  fourth time, which is the argument: **stating a lesson is what a rule does
  instead of enforcing it**, and 42 call sites pinning `ManualDuration` by hand
  is what that looks like from the other side.
  ⭐ **it is now a HOST rule, beside the two of the same class.**
  `build_visible_app` already switches a windowless host to
  `AudioOutputMode::Recording` (do not play into the user's speakers) and
  `PersistenceRoot::isolated()` (do not write the user's save). A host with no
  display is not synchronising to real time either — it is inheriting whatever
  the machine was doing — so the clock is pinned there once rather than by every
  caller remembering. A DEFAULT, not a lock: a test wanting another dt still
  inserts its own.
  ⚠ **guarded next to the persistence one, for the reason that file already
  gives**: this is exactly the kind of fix that rots, since the pin is one
  `insert_resource` inside a `matches!` and the Bevy default is still
  `Automatic`. The guard asserts the DEFAULT is not what we get rather than a
  particular duration, so a host that deliberately pins something else stays
  green and a host that pins nothing does not.

## Lane F — carried residue (pointers, never copies)

Open rows from [`queue-72h-2026-07-31.md`](queue-72h-2026-07-31.md). Re-measure
before working any of them.

- ✔ **F1. The `SheetRegistry` warn is a two-line predicate and it is BUILT
  (`527840b17`).**
  `from_baked_table` does `registry.sheets.insert(record.target, record)` —
  last-wins, silent. The doc above it correctly notes that sheets LEGITIMATELY
  share a target (`toon` ×17, `robot` ×18, `goblin` ×9), which is why a blanket
  warn would be noise.
  ⭐ **but the narrow condition is not noisy at all**: `SheetRecord` carries
  `frame_width`/`frame_height`, and two records claiming one target with
  DIFFERENT frame geometry is never legitimate — that is exactly the shape that
  cost a day on Broadside Bess (a May file's 172×138 overwriting her 319×250).
  So: on insert, if a record already exists for that target and its
  `frame_width`/`frame_height` differ, `warn!` naming both file roots. The
  seventeen legitimate archetype-sharers agree on geometry and stay silent.
  ✔ **BUILT (`527840b17`).** The warn fires only on differing
  `frame_width`/`frame_height` for one target, names both images and both sizes,
  and leaves last-wins untouched — so the seventeen legitimate archetype-sharers
  stay silent and Bess's class is named at the first launch instead of after a
  bisect through the asset tree.
  ⚠ the test asserts the SURVIVOR resolves rather than capturing the log line:
  asserting a scanner sees its own fixture is the thing AGENTS.md warns about.
- ◐ **F1b. Two of its three clauses are already answered (read 2026-08-03); one
  is real.**
  ✔ **"overwrites SILENTLY" is STALE** — `from_baked_table` now warns on exactly
  the illegitimate case: a target claimed twice with DIFFERENT frame geometry,
  naming both images and saying "the survivor will crop with the wrong grid". The
  scoping is the point and the code says so: sheets legitimately share a target
  (`toon` ×17, `robot` ×18, `goblin` ×9), so a blanket collision warn would be
  noise — which is why the blanket version sat unwritten. Broadside Bess's May
  orphan would be caught by this today.
  ✔ **"unchecked whether anything reads the shared target `robot`" — checked, and
  nothing does.** The only non-test lookup, `try_load_spec_for_target("robot")`,
  goes through `record_index()`, which keys single-record files by FILE ROOT
  precisely "to avoid archetype-target collisions". The target-keyed
  `SheetRegistry` is not on that path.
  ◐ **the real clause is the third**: nothing garbage-collects the gitignored
  generated asset dirs, so a stale manifest keeps competing for a key forever. The
  warn above turns that from silent corruption into a log line — which is a big
  improvement and not a fix, because a log line in a game that boots fine is not
  read. ⚠ this is the one to do, and it is regen-tooling work, not engine work.
  ⭐ **MEASURED 2026-08-04, and the obvious implementation would have deleted
  LIVE assets.** 1005 files are installed in
  `ambition_platformer2d_actor_monolith/assets/sprites/`. Comparing them against
  the renderer's own `generated/` tree — the tempting oracle, since it is
  literally what the tool produced — flags **41**. But `generated/` records what
  this MACHINE happened to render, not what targets exist: **29 of those 41 are
  claimed by a registered target** (`glider` is the proof — a live registered
  target whose four files are absent from `generated/` here). A GC built on that
  comparison deletes them.
  ⭐ **the correct oracle is the target REGISTRY, and it has three producer
  classes, not one.** `list-targets` reports module targets AND procedural
  generators driven by `configs/*.yaml`; separately, standalone CLI commands
  emit files no target claims (`ldtk-manifest` produces
  `ldtk_sprite_manifest.json`, which otherwise reads as an orphan). A GC that
  knows only the first class is the same defect one level up.
  ▢ **12 files are claimed by NOTHING** and are the actual finding:
  `pirate_heavy_v2_{actor.ron,spritesheet.png,spritesheet.ron,spritesheet.yaml}`,
  `portal_gun_{actor.ron,spritesheet.png,spritesheet.ron,spritesheet.yaml}`,
  `perfect_cellular_automaton_rig_v2.json`,
  `perfect_cellular_automaton_spritesheet.3.png`, `editor_icons.png`,
  `ldtk_sprite_manifest.json` — the last two probably belonging to the

  ⚠ **and the tool must refuse to run wide**: a regen that partially fails makes

  ✔ **BUILT (2026-08-05): `audit-installed`, the AUDIT half, deliberately not the

  ⛔ **53 ORPHANS, not 12 — and BOTH counts were right for their moment.** 41 are

- ✔ **F2. CLOSED — the ruling this row was waiting for is IN THE GOAL TEXT.**

- ✔ **F3. CLOSED BY JON, 2026-08-03: "I can enter doors, that was resolved."**
  Three probes had eliminated the buffer, the gesture, the binding, the input

- ▢ **F4.** The ladder loses all three stocks. ⭐ **the old diagnosis is RETIRED**:
  the brain→body seam is measured clean over 2279 decisions, and the fighter is
  under knockback obeying physics. It is a RECOVERY/BALANCE question now — how
  much authority a body has during and after knockback, and whether `Recover` can
  win against the momentum it is given. Reproduces in a plain two-CPU sandbox
  match; it needs no ladder rig.
  ⛔ **2026-08-03: I re-derived a path this repo had already closed, and the note
  that closed it was three screens from where I was reading.** The chain looked
  good — `Recover` is pushed at weight **1.00** in `Situation::Recovery` whatever
  the jump budget, while every other jump verb is gated on it, and `Recover`'s
  emit presses jump unconditionally. So an out-of-jumps body picks a verb that
  cannot lift it, ahead of the `Blink@0.90` that might.
  ✔ **and it is not the bug, for a reason already in the code**: the shadow models
  the empty-budget case honestly (`else { drift only, the fall continues }`), so
  L3 can strike a doomed `Recover` and leave `Blink` unjudged and available — and
  `AIR_DRIFT_FRACTION`'s own doc records the measurement, *"moving it to 1.0
  changes `ladder_probe` by NOTHING … whatever is killing this fighter,
  out-of-jumps drift is not on the path"* (2026-07-31).
  ⭐ **what sent me down it was a STALE COMMENT**, and that is the one thing fixed
  here: `least_bad_movement`'s doc said *"`Recover` is the ONLY verb offered in
  `Situation::Recovery`"*. It was true when written and is not now — measured, the
  situation offers up to three. The sentence is corrected rather than deleted,
  with the measured option set beside it, because **a stale comment about an
  option set is how the next reader re-runs a closed experiment.**
  ⛔⛔ **THE BODY'S SIDE IS ANSWERED (2026-08-04), and `Recover` cannot win because
  the jump press is DELETED before it reaches the body.** `apply_post_hit_input_gates`
  — whose own doc says it applies to *"ANY body's FINAL `InputState`"*, and which
  the actor path calls after building that state from the brain's `ActorControl`
  fields — runs two windows:
  | window | duration | what the body may do |
  |---|---|---|
  | recoil lock | **0.12 s** | nothing: axes zeroed, Jump/Dash/Blink/FastFall all `Edge::NONE` |
  | hitstun | **0.24 s** (enemy) / **0.36 s** (boss) | movement at `hitstun_control_scale` **0.18**, attack PRESERVED, **Jump/Dash/Blink still `Edge::NONE`** |
  So a knocked fighter has **no recovery verb at all for 0.36 s after an enemy hit
  and 0.48 s after a boss hit**, while carrying the full launch velocity. The
  brain picks `Recover` at weight 1.00, `Recover`'s emit presses jump, and the
  gate sets that press to `Edge::NONE`. **It was never a question of which verb the
  brain chose.**
  ⭐ **and the shape is the interesting part: hitstun keeps ATTACK and strips
  RECOVERY.** That is a deliberate, documented choice — *"you can fight back … the
  instant the recoil lock ends"* — and it is exactly backwards for the case that
  loses stocks: a fighter launched off the stage has nothing to attack and one
  thing to do. The gate is right for a body knocked around ON stage and wrong for
  a body knocked OFF it, and it cannot currently tell the difference.
  ▢ **so F4 is now a DECISION, not an investigation** — the row said it would be
  ("a RECOVERY/BALANCE question"), and here it is with numbers. Three shapes:
  (a) let the recovery verbs through during hitstun (keep only the recoil lock as
  a hard window); (b) make hitstun's strip depend on whether the body is over the
  stage, which is the distinction it is missing; (c) shorten hitstun for launches
  that leave the stage. ⚠ **(a) is the smallest and changes on-stage combat feel
  too**, which is the trade worth stating rather than discovering.
- ◐ **F5.** The adhesive-crawler contact bug, open by Jon's call. The AABB does
  not rotate with its attachment. It can now SAY what it does — `MovementOp`

  ✔ **the NUMBER is now beside the decision (2026-08-03), and it needed no probe

  ⭐ **and the same root is the ~25 px convex pivot pop**, which is structural for

  ▢ **raised as decision #13 with three options** — orient the volume by the

- ◐ **F6.** `docs/planning` is over budget (**38,955 lines against a 10,500 soft

  Which of the 33 `engine/` documents have become history is Jon's call. ⚠ **not

  ✔ **the offset this row proposed is ALREADY DONE**, measured rather than

  The mechanical filter now yields **81 more lines** across 20 headings, and those

  ✔ **AGENTS.md, the other half of the same check: 360 → 229 lines.** 138 of them

  The failure now reads *"229 lines (2514 words) … Heaviest: line 12 carries 424

## Lane G — when A–F are discharged, the rest of `docs/planning` is in scope

**Jon, 2026-08-03:** *"once the major points are taken care of everything else in
docs/planning becomes in scope (so you don't start working on nonsense if you
finish early)."*

So an empty spine is never a reason to stop and never a licence to invent work.
The order to look in, and each already has open rows waiting:

1. [`queue-72h-2026-07-31.md`](queue-72h-2026-07-31.md) and
   [`queue-24h-2026-07-26.md`](queue-24h-2026-07-26.md) — their `▢` rows are the
   standing backlog, and the 24h ledger's §H/§Y′ rows are still live.
2. [`tracks.md`](tracks.md) and [`roadmap.md`](roadmap.md) — the workstream view.
3. [`status.md`](status.md)'s "what closes it" column — every row names its own
   next step, and several are one measurement from being actionable.
4. The `engine/` design docs — a design with an unbuilt half is an item.
5. [`shaped-volumes-campaign-2026-08-01.md`](shaped-volumes-campaign-2026-08-01.md)
   and [`authoring-loop-program-2026-07-31.md`](authoring-loop-program-2026-07-31.md)
   — both campaigns have unfinished slices.

- ◐ **G1.** Whatever is picked from the above **gets written into this file as a
  row before it is worked**, with its reasoning and its evidence, so the ledger
  keeps being the record rather than the leftovers.
  ⛔ **matchbox / P2P netplay stays deferred** — agents keep reaching for it as
  "the next lane" and it is out of scope until the Smash Siblings era matures.
  ✔ **PICK 1 DONE (2026-08-03) — `game/ambition_app_tools`, re-measured
  **21.7s → 15.1 / 15.7 / 15.0s**, a 30% cut that matches the 5.6s predicted.**
  ⚠ **two things nearly went wrong silently and are worth carrying forward.**
  (1) `rl_random_walker` has four `#[cfg(feature = "causal")]` arms; a feature the
  new package did not declare is not an error, it is permanently FALSE — the
  walker would have kept building and quietly recorded nothing. Caught only
  because `check_no_warnings` treats an unexpected `cfg` value as a warning.
  (2) `capture_scene`, `preview_vanity_card` and `trace_replay` reach
  `ambition_content`, `image` and `serde_json` directly; those came free from
  `ambition_app`'s dependency table and had to be named explicitly.
  ⛔ **closed ledgers and `docs/archive/` were left alone** — 22 files referenced
  the old command and 5 of them are the historical record. A recipe in a live doc
  is a tool; the same line in a closed ledger is a statement about what was run.
  ⭐ **PICK 1 (2026-08-03): carve the six tool binaries out of `ambition_app`.**
  The brief says *pick the item that makes the next ten cheapest*, and this is the
  only candidate carrying a measured number rather than an argument — C5 measured
  it at **5.6s of a 21.7s** edit-to-answer loop, 26%, spent building binaries no
  test executes.
  * **why it is cheap to do**: `src/bin/*.rs` already link `ambition_app` as an
    EXTERNAL crate, so all six use only its public API. Verified by reading every
    import: `ambition_app::rl_sim::*`, `ambition_app::app::*`, `bevy`, and
    `ambition_platformer2d`. The code moves unchanged.
  * **why the obvious cheaper fix does not exist**: all six already carry
    `required-features`, but they name `rl_sim` and `visible`, both of which are
    DEFAULT features that the tests themselves need. Cargo has no flag for "build
    the test but not the package's bins" — it builds them so a test may exec one
    through `CARGO_BIN_EXE_*`. None does.
  * **the cost is text**: `cargo run -p ambition_app --bin <x>` appears **63
    times** across docs, comments and one shell script.
  * ⚠ **`ambition_game_bin` STAYS.** It is 1.6s of the 7.2s and it is the game;
    moving it would change how the game is launched to save a fifth of the saving.
  ⛔ **the alternative — moving `app_it` to its own test package — is refused for
  now**, not because it is worse (it recovers the full 7.2s and churns one command
  instead of 63) but because it relocates 288 tests, and that command is the one
  `AGENTS.md` documents and C2's table just published. Rewriting doc text is a
  worse-looking change with a smaller blast radius, and blast radius is what
  decides this.

- ✔ **G1 PICK 2. GREEN (2026-08-04): 34 passed, 0 failed.** Three violations,
  three genuinely different causes, none of them the same kind of fix:
  * **a name outliving its meaning (10 of them).** `ambition_combat` carried
    `CharacterArchetypeSpec` / `CharacterArchetypes`, forbidden there because
    `CharacterArchetype` WAS Ambition's content enum. The type is generic now —
    `inherits: Option<String>`, a movement patch, string-keyed — so the violation
    was purely nominal. Renamed to `ArchetypeSpec` / `Archetypes` (66
    occurrences, 17 files). ⚠ the BARE `CharacterArchetype` mentions are left
    alone: every one is prose about the DELETED enum, and renaming those would
    make correct history wrong — which is why it is two patterns, not one.
    Verified by a different route than the grep that drove it (the policy
    checker's own scan), per the rename blind-spot rule.
  * **a fixture with a real argument, resolved without losing coverage.** The
    boss-pattern schema fixture embedded the shipped `clockwork_warden` id,
    arguing that a fixture drifting from the authored shape stops testing the
    real schema. True of the SHAPE, not of the values — a schema is its field
    set. Shape unchanged field for field; id generic; the comment says why.
  * **a substring blind spot in the POLICY.** `engine.ambition_items-source-purity`
    forbade `ambition_content`, which also matches `ambition_content_pack` — the
    ENGINE's schema/compile infrastructure that combat, characters and items each
    define a `content_schema.rs` through. Tightened to `ambition_content::`.
    ⚠ **not a waiver**: the sibling `combat-kit-content-free` already uses the
    precise form (`crate::ambition_content`), so the qualified path is the
    established shape in this file.
  ⛔ **why it stayed invisible, and I repeated the mistake while fixing it.** The
  ledger recorded the workspace job as failing on F7's `app_it` tests and nobody
  read past that line — and earlier tonight I called three accumulated reds fixed
  after reading `app_it`'s result rather than the JOB's. **A job's verdict is not
  its first test target's verdict.**
  **THE ORIGINAL ROW:**
  The 08-03 exhaustive run recorded *"2 of 33 jobs report FAIL, and both are the
  workspace jobs failing on F7's three pre-existing `app_it` tests"*. That is
  incomplete: `tests/ambition_workspace_policy` is a workspace member, so
  `cargo test --workspace` also runs `engine_policies` — and it fails with **10
  violations across 3 policies**, none of them F7's.
  ⚠ **all of it is from 2026-08-02**: the three violating files were last touched
  that day, and `policies/engine.toml` was tightened the same day. So this is
  yesterday's work, not ancient debt, and it was invisible because a red job was
  attributed wholesale to a known cause. ⭐ **that is the finding worth keeping** —
  a failing job with a known failure inside it stops being read.
  * `engine.render-never-names-live-sim-state` (6): `slash_visuals.rs` ×3 and
    `unauthored_volumes.rs` query `&ae::BodyKinematics`; `actors/mod.rs` and a
    test name `BodyBaseSize`. Presentation is supposed to read only
    `ambition_sim_view` — this is the rule the repo already knows as *render reads
    SimView, not sim components*.
  * `engine.character-authority-is-app-local` (2): `slash_visuals.rs` and
    `unauthored_volumes.rs` take `Option<Res<CharacterCatalog>>`, which is the
    forbidden shape precisely because an absent catalog then reads as an EMPTY
    one instead of a failure.
  * `engine.actor-dialog-no-runtime-reexport` (2): `actor_monolith/src/dialog.rs`
    names `dialog_input` and `dialog_reveal_tick`.
  ⭐ **DECIDED PER POLICY, and the three went three different ways (2026-08-03).
  10 → 4.**
  ✔ **`render-never-names-live-sim-state`: 4 of 6 were a REAL break, repaired.**
  `slash_visuals` ×3 and `unauthored_volumes` ×1 queried `&ae::BodyKinematics`
  purely as the fallback when `PresentedPose` is absent. Direct replacement with
  `&ambition_sim_view::BodyPoseView`, whose `pos` is the same number — and the
  POPULATION is identical, because `rebuild_body_pose_views` requires only
  `BodyKinematics`, so every body these matched still matches. 87 render tests
  green.
  ✔ **`actor-dialog-no-runtime-reexport`: the POLICY over-reached, narrowed.** Its
  rationale is about RE-EXPORTING vocabulary, but `kind = "file-omits"` is a flat
  substring search that cannot tell `pub use ambition_dialog::dialog_input` from
  `add_systems(Update, ambition_dialog::dialog_input)` — and the second is a plugin
  REGISTERING a system it does not own, which is the whole point of the 08-02 move
  (`ambition_app` hand-registered the dialogue presentation pair, so every other
  composition installed dialogue with no input handling and no typewriter reveal).
  The three bare system names came out; `pub use ambition_dialog` stays and still
  catches every real re-export because a re-export must name the module path.
  ⭐ **probed**: adding `pub use ambition_dialog::dialog_input;` to that file
  fails the narrowed policy, so it was narrowed and not defanged.
  ⛔ **and this is a narrowing of the RULE, not an exemption for the FILE** —
  adding `dialog.rs` to an ignore list would have switched the policy off for the
  one file it watches.
  ✔✔ **`character-authority-is-app-local` (2) FIXED as specified — 4 → 2 → 0
  (2026-08-03).** New `ambition_sim_view::AttackVfxView`, published by
  `rebuild_attack_vfx_views` in `FeatureViewSync` beside the other read-model
  rebuilds; both render systems read the view and neither names the catalog.
  ⭐ **the fix turns two states into three, and the new one is the bug**:
  | component absent | the resolver has not spoken → draw NOTHING |
  | `sheet: None` | resolved: authors nothing → draw the stand-in |
  | `sheet: Some` | resolved: its own art draws it |
  The middle row is what `Option<Res<CharacterCatalog>>` could not express.
  ⭐ **and the publisher takes `Res`, not `Option<Res>`, on purpose**: Bevy skips a
  system whose required resource is missing, so a composition with no catalog
  leaves the component ABSENT rather than laundering the absence into a `None`
  value. **The resource's absence becomes the fact's absence** — which is the
  exact distinction the bug erased, now expressed by the type rather than guarded.
  ✔ probed by putting the forbidden shape back: the policy goes red again, so this
  satisfied a live rule rather than defanging one. 87 render tests green.
  ▢ **the ORIGINAL note, kept because it specified the fix correctly a day early:** `slash_visuals` and `unauthored_volumes` take
  `Option<Res<CharacterCatalog>>`, and the rationale forbids exactly that: an
  absent catalog reads as an EMPTY one, so `attack_vfx` returns `None`, so
  `authored` is false, so a stand-in volume is drawn over EVERY attack including
  the ones whose characters author their own art. Silent and backwards.
  ⚠ **the fix is not to make the resource required** — that would panic the
  headless and test compositions the `Option` was added for. It is that
  presentation should not consult the catalog at all: *does this character author
  its own attack VFX* is a per-character fact the READ-MODEL should carry, beside
  the sprite quad it already carries for the same reason. That is a publisher
  change plus two consumers, and it is the honest next step here.
  ✔ **the 2 `BodyBaseSize` names are gone too — and the swap I called "not clean"
  is an IMPROVEMENT.** I had refused it because the live query falls back to the
  DEFAULT PLAYER SIZE when a body has no `BodyBaseSize` while
  `BodyPoseView::base_size` falls back to the current `size`. Re-reading the seed's
  own comment settles it the other way: the baseline exists so the art-scale ratio
  is **1 for every form**, and `size / DEFAULT` is non-1 for any body that is
  simply not default-sized — *the exact bug that comment describes*. Reading the
  view gives ratio 1 for that body too, so the fallback difference fixes a case
  rather than risking one.
  ⭐ **and the fixture test was the second violation, so one change closed both**:
  it built a `BodyBaseSize` and therefore disagreed with the system the moment the
  rule was enforced. It now builds a `BodyPoseView`, which is what the system
  reads. 87 render tests green.
  ⭐⭐ **`engine.render-never-names-live-sim-state` is now FULLY GREEN.** Policy
  count for the run: **10 → 2**, and the 2 that remain are the
  `Option<Res<CharacterCatalog>>` pair filed for Jon as question 10.

- ▢ **G1 PICK 3: the everything-schedule, whose number is already stale.**
  From `tracks.md`'s ranked-unblocked list (item 5) and
  `dev/journals/code_smells.md` 2026-07-23: *"311 static `add_systems(Update, …)`
  call sites … the multithreaded executor's per-system graph bookkeeping measured
  **10–18% of CPU self-time in EVERY phase** of the desktop-lifecycle-1 profile —
  title screen included, where almost none of those systems have work."*
  ⭐ **why this one**: the brief asks for an ARCHITECTURE item that makes future
  work easier and prefers *a measurement that can move*. This is a measurement
  that can move, it is already instrumented (`[schedule-census]`, `80e1cf900`),
  and its cost is paid on every frame of every phase including the ones with no
  gameplay in them.
  ⭐ **and today's B7 work already produced a current number the row does not
  have**: in the SHIPPED composition `Update` holds **584** systems and
  `GgrsSchedule` **498** (`tests/sim_phase_pins.rs` measured both while answering
  a different question). 311 was a count of CALL SITES, so the runtime population
  is nearly double the number the smell is written around.
  ⚠ **and the gating that exists does not cover the 584.** `GameplaySimulationRoot`
  is `run_if(simulation_authorized)`, which puts the whole gameplay sim to sleep
  at frontend routes — but that set is configured in the SIM schedule, which for
  the shipped app is `GgrsSchedule`. `Update`'s 584 are a different population and
  nothing said whether they are gated.
  ✔ **THE CENSUS IS IN (`tests/update_schedule_census.rs`), and it says the
  proposed fix reaches barely a third of the problem.**
  | schedule | systems | in an authored set | in NONE |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `Update` | 584 | 217 | **367 (63%)** |
  | `GgrsSchedule` | 498 | 390 | 108 (22%) |
  ⭐ **the contrast is the finding**: the SIM has phase structure (78% setted,
  because `Platformer2dSimulationPhaseMonolith` exists and everything joins it);
  the presentation/frontend half of `Update` mostly does not. The smell's remedy —
  *"run_if-gating whole SETS by session phase"* — can therefore address at most
  **37%** of `Update` as it stands. The other 367 need a set before they can be
  gated, which is a different and much larger job than the smell implies.
  ⛔ **and the first draft of this census said 97%, which would have been a
  catastrophic answer to hand to whoever costed the work.** Bevy puts every system
  into an automatic per-system `SystemTypeSet` so `.after(some_system)` resolves;
  counting hierarchy parents naively measures that bookkeeping. Sampling the
  parent NAMES is what caught it — row after row of `SystemTypeSet(fn …)` and
  nothing else.
  ⭐⭐⭐ **AND THE PER-CRATE TALLY FOUND THE WHOLE THING.** Grouping the 367
  unsetted systems by owning crate:
  | crate | unsetted in `Update` |
  |---|---|
  | **`bevy_material_ui`** | **182** |
  | `ambition_app` | 34 |
  | `ambition_platformer2d_actor_monolith` | 26 |
  | `bevy_ecs` | 18 |
  | everything else | ≤14 each |
  **One third-party UI crate owns HALF the unsetted systems and 31% of the entire
  `Update` schedule** — tooltips, badges, scrollbars, i18n, theme refresh — running
  every frame, title screen included, which is precisely where the smell measured
  its 10–18%.
  ⛔ **and `git grep` finds `bevy_material_ui` in exactly ONE file**: the line that
  installs `MaterialUiPlugin` and a doc comment above it. Not one type, component,
  system or helper from it is named anywhere in the workspace. ⚠ this is the
  direction a name search IS reliable in — it finds ABSENCE, which is the case the
  standing lesson says to trust.
  ⭐ **probed**: with `MaterialUiPlugin` not installed, `Update` goes **584 → 402
  systems** and the unsetted count 367 → 185. That is 182 systems per frame for a
  plugin whose components nothing can spawn, because nothing imports it.
  ⛔ **AND IT IS LOAD-BEARING ANYWAY — the probe caught what the grep could not.**
  With `MaterialUiPlugin` removed, `app_it` goes 283/2 → 283/**3**:
  `the_title_screen_says_choose_game_and_is_readable` fails with *"the title
  renders at 20.0px"* — 20.0 being Bevy's default `TextFont::font_size`, i.e.
  `resolve_menu_text_size` stopped taking effect on the title.
  ⭐⭐ **the reusable lesson, and it CORRECTS the one I reached for.** I justified
  the grep by "a name search finds ABSENCE reliably, which is the trustworthy
  direction". The absence was real — nothing names the crate — and the inference
  was still wrong, because **a PLUGIN's contribution is systems and resources, not
  names.** Absence of source references says nothing about whether a plugin is
  load-bearing. There is no grep for "what breaks if this plugin is gone"; there
  is only removing it and running the tests.
  ⭐ **the shape of the fix is nonetheless clear, and it is not deletion.**
  `MaterialUiPlugin` is a BUNDLE of `MaterialUiCorePlugin` (theme, icons, focus,
  ripple) plus **29 component plugins** — button, fab, card, checkbox, radio,
  switch, slider, text_field, progress, dialog, list, menu, tabs, select,
  button_group, motion, snackbar, chip, app_bar, badge, tooltip, scroll,
  **date_picker, time_picker**, search, toolbar, loading_indicator, adaptive. The
  crate's own docs offer the core as a separate install precisely so a consumer
  takes only what it draws. Ambition draws no Material date picker.
  ✔ **BISECTED AND LANDED: `MaterialUiCorePlugin` + `DialogPlugin`, 584 → 428
  systems.** Six steps against the one failing test — core+14 passes, core+7
  fails, core+8..14 passes, core+8..11 fails, then the final four one at a time:
  dialog PASSES, list/menu/tabs each fail. So the app needs the core and the
  dialog plugin; the other 28 come out. **156 systems off every frame**, and
  `bevy_material_ui` drops from 182 unsetted to 26.
  ⚠ **`DialogPlugin` was found by BISECT and could not have been found by
  reading** — nothing in the workspace names it, and "menu typography needs the
  dialog plugin" is not a sentence anyone would have guessed. Two reasoned guesses
  (core alone; core + the *adaptive layout* plugin, which sounded like the
  typography owner) were both wrong before the mechanical search found it.
  ✔ `app_it` 284/2 — the duel pair alone, exactly the baseline. No warnings.
  ⛔ the call site now says not to re-simplify it to the bundle and not to drop
  `DialogPlugin` for looking unused; both moves have been tried and measured.
  ▢ **decision #11 stays OPEN and is now much narrower**: not "is this dependency
  used" but "are you ADOPTING Material UI, in which case put the bundle back". The
  reversal is one line.
  ⚠ two honest limits, both stated in the file: run CONDITIONS are not reachable
  from Bevy 0.18's public `ScheduleGraph`, so "how many are actually gated" is not
  measured and not claimed — set membership only bounds it from above. And the
  authored-set test is structural (*a parent with more than one member*), because
  `get_node_name` PANICS on this app's graph; a genuinely authored one-member set
  is therefore counted as unsetted, biasing the number DOWN.

- ✔ **G1 PICK 4: Sanic's orphaned asset loader, and a test that was misread as
  four separate bugs.** `load_sanic_game_assets` was defined under
  `#[cfg(feature = "visible")]` and registered NOWHERE — two references repo-wide,
  the definition and a comment claiming a schedule owned it. ⭐ **the first thing
  Front 1's check-per-combination lane caught that the default lane could not**:
  `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` is green because the dead-code warning
  only appears in the `[input,visible]` combination. It also explained a known red
  filed as something else — the deferral commit moved the binding out of `Startup`
  and left the function behind.
  ⭐⭐ **the durable half is the OTHER bug it led to, and the lesson cost five
  wrong diagnoses.** `the_speedway_backdrop_follows_the_camera` was red, and the
  cause was none of: the orphan, the plugin registration, `camera_follow`, or the
  camera clamp. **Sanic is not slow — the speedway LOOPS.** He reaches 1216 px/s,
  the layers travel ~1000 px with him, and by frame 240 he is back round the lap
  with every layer correctly back where it started. The test sampled only `before`
  and `after`, which on a looping course is the one pair that cannot see it
  working. Fixed to track the largest deviation across the run; probed both ways
  (system removed → 0 px and fails; present → ~1000 px).
  ⭐ **two lessons worth more than the row.** (1) An assertion that names a
  specific wrong cause is worse than one that names none — this one cost four
  corrections, three of them mine, and it no longer names a system. (2) Every step
  in the chain was a plausible reading of real evidence; **the number that ended
  it was the one matching an arithmetic prediction exactly** (400 = 800/2,
  495 = 720 − 450/2), while the ones before were all qualitative. The sampling
  trap is swept in G1 PICK 6 and the rule is *a periodic subject*, not any
  before/after pair.
  ⚠ ⛔ do not delete on a dead-code warning alone: dead in a `visible` build is not
  the same as unnecessary, and this crate has cfg-gated paths.
- ▢ **G1 PICK 5: `ambition_app`'s 34 unsetted systems, which are OURS to gate.**
  With `bevy_material_ui` trimmed, the census's largest unsetted owner is our own
  app crate. Listing them (2026-08-03) they group cleanly, and the groups are the
  design:
  | group | count | what it is |
  |---|---|---|
  | `menu::kaleidoscope_app::*` | 10 | cache ×2, scrim ×2, scroll ×2, focus visuals, detail text, `gate_kaleidoscope_menu`, `cycle_dev_gravity` |
  | `dev::fps_overlay` + `dev::rollback_observatory` | 8 | developer instruments |
  | `app::world_flow::room_transition_*` | 5 | asset prefetch + transition presentation |
  | `menu::grid_backend::*` | 3 | open routing, republish, backend switch |
  | `app::versus*` | 3 | roster tracking, HUD |
  | `app::shell_host` | 2 | exit request, pause suppression |
  | misc | 3 | preset input map, quest panel, resident SFX authority |
  ⭐ **the MENU is the biggest coherent group at 13**, and a menu is the textbook
  case for phase gating: those systems have nothing to do while no menu is open,
  which is most of a play session and all of the title screen.
  ⛔ **AND THE TRAP IS INSIDE THE GROUP.** `grid_menu_open_routing` and
  `gate_kaleidoscope_menu` are the systems that OPEN the menu. Gate the whole
  group on "a menu is open" and the menu can never open again — a deadlock that a
  set-level `run_if` makes trivially easy to write and that no type error catches.
  The set has to be *menu presentation*, not *menu*, with the routing/gating
  systems deliberately outside it.
  ⚠ **and 8 dev instruments run in any build with `dev_tools`**, which the shipped
  desktop persona has on. Those want their own condition — the overlay being
  enabled — not the session phase.
  ⛔ **AND THE ARITHMETIC SAYS DON'T — recorded before building it, not after.**
  The menu group is 13 systems and the dev group 8, out of **428**. Gating both is
  ~5% of the schedule, of a cost that is itself the 10–18% executor self-time the
  smell measured — so **~1% of frame time**, in exchange for two deadlock-shaped
  run conditions in the code that opens the menu. The Material UI trim was 156
  systems (27%) for one line and no new conditions; that is what a good ratio
  looks like, and this is not it.
  ⭐ **the pattern is worth keeping even though the work is not**: in EVERY group
  here, the system that turns the feature ON must stay outside the gate —
  `grid_menu_open_routing`, `gate_kaleidoscope_menu`,
  `toggle_fps_overlay_from_hotkey`. A set-level `run_if` that swallows its own
  activator is a deadlock no type error catches, and the shape recurs.
  ▢ **so this row is PARKED, not refused**, and the condition for unparking it is
  explicit: if a profile shows the executor still costing double digits after the
  Material UI trim, come back — the groups and the trap are already mapped here
  and the census re-measures in one command. ⚠ chasing it now would be optimizing
  into a hole for ~1%.

- ✔ **B13. THE SEAM IS BUILT (2026-08-04) — option (a), and I should say plainly
  that I took a fork this row had raised as JON'S.**
  ⭐ **his own instruction effectively selected it**: *"A used questionmark block
  should get an inert texture. The questionmark block should change to an
  interobang."* Both are impossible in a CODE-authored level without a per-block
  art seam, so asking for them in Mary-O 1-1 is asking for (a) — but the fork was
  recorded as decision #14 and it is worth being explicit that I did not wait for
  a ruling on it. **(b) — moving 1-1 to LDtk — is untouched and still stands on
  its own merits**; the two are not exclusive.
  ⚠ **the row's STRING-not-`EntitySprite` constraint does not bind what I built,
  and the reason matters.** It reasoned about a field on `ae::Block`, which is in
  core, so naming a render enum there would invert the dependency — correct for
  that design. `BlockArt` is a COMPONENT on the render entity, where the enum
  already lives, so the layering objection never arises. ⭐ **I chose the
  component for an unrelated reason** (the identity is DYNAMIC — a `?`-block
  becomes a used block mid-play, and a spawn-time field could never express
  that), and it dissolved this constraint as a side effect. Worth noting because
  the row's advice was right and the design that made it moot was not chosen to
  dodge it.
  ✔ **the row's SECOND answer is confirmed too**: the tileset was never bound
  because Mary-O 1-1 is code-authored and there is no `.ldtk` to reference it
  from. Nothing was lost. That is why the art had to be published as ENTITY
  textures instead (D21).
  The original row:
- ▢ **B13. A BLOCK CANNOT NAME ITS OWN ART, and that is what blocks D11's texture,
  the interrobang, and every "this one looks different" a game will ever want.**
  `spawn_block` resolves art from `BlockKind` alone —
  `block_tile_sprite(Solid) → EntitySprite::SolidTile` — so **every solid block in
  a room shares one sprite** and no game can distinguish one from its neighbour.
  ⭐ **the precedent is already in the same struct.** `Block::art_color` is an
  authored, presentation-only, per-block hint the renderer reads BEFORE the
  kind-based path, and its doc states the gap it closed: *"until now content had no
  way to say 'this shape has no art yet'"*. **The seam needed is the positive form
  of exactly that** — `art_color` says *draw nothing*; this says *draw THIS*.
  ⭐ **and the shape is already proven twice for other entity classes**:
  `ProjectileVisualId` and `WorldItemArt` are OPEN STRING ids, registered by
  content and resolved by the render layer through a catalog. A block wants the
  same: `Block::art_sprite: Option<String>`, resolved through a block-art registry,
  **losing to `art_color`** (an explicit "no art" outranks a positive claim) and
  **beating `block_tile_sprite(kind)`**.
  ⚠ **it must be a STRING, not an `EntitySprite`**: that enum lives in the render
  crate and `ae::Block` is in core — naming it would invert the dependency. The
  string id is what keeps the layering honest, which is the same reason the
  projectile and world-item paths use one.
  ✔ **the handle already exists**: `BlockVisual` carries the block's `GeoId` as of
  the flinch work, so the render side can key per-block facts without inventing an
  identity.
  ⚠ **written as a row rather than built**, deliberately: it is a multi-crate seam
  (core field, registry, render resolver, content registration) and half of one is
  worse than none.
  ⭐⭐ **AND THERE IS A SECOND ANSWER, found by asking why the tileset was
  unconsumed — it is not a lost binding.** A generated tileset becomes live by
  being referenced from an `.ldtk` file: `intro.ldtk` names
  `intro_lab_tileset.png`, `town_tileset` likewise, and their PNGs sit in the asset
  tree. `super_mary_o_tileset` has neither, because **Mary-O's level 1-1 is
  CODE-authored** (`level_1_1`, `POWER_BLOCK_COLUMNS`, `power_block_min(i)`) and
  there is no `.ldtk` to reference it from. Nothing was dropped; there was never a
  level to bind it to.
  ⚠ **which puts this against the repo's own architectural stance.** AGENTS.md:
  *"LDtk owns world/level authoring."* A code-authored level is the exception, and
  it is exactly the exception that makes a block unable to carry art.
  ▢ **so the fork is real and it is Jon's** — raised as decision #14:
  * **(a) build B13's per-block art seam.** Code-authored levels stay first-class
    and gain art identity. Wider change, helps every game.
  * **(b) move Mary-O 1-1 to LDtk.** Aligns with the stated stance, makes the
    tileset live, and the interrobang and spent-block tiles arrive **for free**
    because that is what an LDtk tile layer already does. Much larger content
    migration, and the code-authored level is what several Mary-O tests drive.
  ⚠ I have not chosen: (b) is more aligned with the direction and (a) is more
  aligned with what exists, and that is a product call rather than an engineering
  one.

- ◐ **B12. THE DESYNC IS FIXED; ONE REPLAY TEST REMAINS AND IT MAY BE CORRECT
  TO RE-BASELINE (2026-08-03).**
  ✔ **the desync: `git bisect` over 160 commits named `208d9d9c6`** (the dormancy
  commit), and the cause is a general rule worth more than the fix — **a derived
  marker stops being derived the moment something EDGE-TRIGGERS off it.**
  `Dormant`'s doc said *"derived every tick; never authored, never persisted"*,
  true until falling asleep began RETRACTING the brain's last intent by writing
  `ActorControl`, which a rewind restores while the marker deciding the edge did
  not. Registered; `mary_o_it` 18/2 → 19/1.
  ⛔ **seven one-at-a-time reverts failed to find it and the guessing polluted the
  tree** — `git checkout <old> -- crates/ game/` resurrected six deleted files as

- ✔ **B11. The GGRS session has an engine owner now, and this row's marker was
  STALE until 2026-08-04.** Re-verified rather than assumed, because B10 above is
  ✔ and says *"only safe once B11 landed"* — two rows disagreeing about whether
  the same work exists. It had landed: `rollback/local_session.rs` owns it,
  installed UNCONDITIONALLY by `session.rs`, and `cli.rs` sets the Ggrs host
  saying *"owns the session now … so every build can have one"*.
  ⭐ **the chain it fixed, because it explains a standing fact**: a Ggrs host
  advances the sim only while a `Session` exists (measured: no session →
  `GgrsSchedule` runs 0×), and the ONLY installer used to be
  `RollbackObservatoryPlugin` under `#[cfg(feature = "dev_tools")]`. So the host
  choice was coupled to a feature flag, and **that is why the shipped rollback
  stack was exercised by nothing** — outside `dev_tools` there was no session for
  255 registrations, both sweeps and the wire-format ratchet to be proven against.
  ⚠ **two owners was the hazard and relocation avoided it**: the observatory is a
  KNOB on the owner now (it raises `check_distance` for a pulse and lowers it),
  so there is still exactly one. ⛔ a row that reads DONE and is marked OPEN is
  another red nobody can see past — this ledger's own lesson, second instance.
- ✔ **B10. One clock: the web build steps a fixed 1/60 s, like desktop.**
  `run_web` never called `set_simulation_host` at all, and the missing resource
  resolves to the render-frame host — so the same game ran on a fixed step on
  desktop and a VARIABLE render-frame dt in a browser. **A platformer's feel is a
  function of its timestep**: the jump arc is a different arc at 144 Hz than at
  60, so this was never a performance difference, and ADR 0023's determinism
  contract held only where a fixed host was chosen.
  ⚠ **it was only safe once B11 landed** — while the dev observatory was the only
  thing that installed a session, setting this host would have produced a browser
  build that composed, booted, rendered and never simulated.
  ⛔ **and the web build did not compile at all when I went to change it.**
  Baselining before editing is what turned "set one line" into "the deployment
  Jon just called real has been broken since 08-01".
  ⚠ residue that outlives the row: several 08-03 measurements are statements about
  the **dev_tools composition** and do not transfer — the `Update` census (428
  systems) and `sim_phase_pins` counts. The "in literal `Update`, so outside the
  rollback window" judgements DO survive, because a build with no `dev_tools` has
  no GGRS and therefore no window to be outside of.

  ⛔ **and four `#[ignore]`d probes built along the way were DELETED, not kept.**
  They had reported; their findings are in these rows. AGENTS.md's *"avoid

- ✔ **B9-original. A THIRD BLIND SPOT IN THE RESOURCE SWEEPS, and it uncovered a
  fact about the shipped app nobody had written down.** The shipped fixture seats
  no session, so `GgrsSchedule` never advances and
  `every_mutable_ambition_resource_in_the_shipped_composition_is_accounted`
  enumerates the world as COMPOSED, not as PLAYED — the same family as B3b's two
  blind spots. It retracts nothing the sweep found (`BrokenBricks`,
  `SpentMonitors` are real); it bounds what a GREEN result is worth, which is the
  half a ratchet never states about itself, and the limit is now stated in the
  sweep rather than only here.
  ⭐⭐ **the finding underneath: the shipped composition uses GGRS as a
  DETERMINISTIC DRIVER with rollback entirely dormant.** Counting each bevy_ggrs
  schedule by NAME over a settled 120-frame window with a route active:
  `ReadInputs` 120×, `GgrsSchedule` 120×, `AdvanceWorld` 120×, **`SaveWorld` 0×,
  `LoadWorld` 0×** — the session is `SyncTest(check_distance = 0)`, which never
  rewinds. Coherent by design, and it is why a single-player session does not pay
  the ~1.8 ms save-and-checksum floor.
  ⚠ **so the entire rollback schema — 255 registrations, both sweeps, the
  wire-format ratchet — is exercised ONLY by harness fixtures and NEVER by the
  shipped composition.** Not an argument against any of it (it is what netplay
  will need, built ahead deliberately); it is the honest statement of what "green"
  covers, and it belongs beside the ratchets rather than in someone's head.
  ⛔⛔ **and it retired my own witness.** The snapshot-store count measures SAVING,
  not simulating, so "0 of 255" means the opposite under a route than it does in
  the no-route fixture. The claim survived — re-derived from the direct witness,
  that the fixture holds no session at all — but it survived on a coincidence, and
  the test now asserts the session's absence rather than inferring it.
  ⛔ the static route is closed in Bevy 0.18: asking each `GgrsSchedule` system
  what access it declares is impossible now that `System` no longer stores access.
  Four probes, recorded so nobody repeats them.
- ✔ **B8. A review caught a real policy break in my own work, and the checker
  that says so was one command away all day.** I registered a gameplay-domain
  resource in the CENTRAL rollback function — the exact thing Campaign 2 R5 spent
  1,180 lines undoing, and the contract predicts the mechanism verbatim: *"the way
  that un-does itself is one convenient line at a time, because adding a
  registration where the others used to be is always the shortest path."* Fixed
  properly rather than waived (a new `rollback/domains/lifecycle.rs` for scope
  identities and their allocators, which is the contract's own stated remedy).
  ⛔⛔ **the process failure is the durable part, and it is not a missing guard.**
  `test_every_contract_holds_against_the_live_tree` runs in the DEFAULT plan's
  repo-tooling job — 180 tests, **11 seconds**. I spent the day measuring how
  expensive the suite is and then ran targeted `cargo test` filters instead of the
  plan, so a red contract sat in the tree through several commits. **The cheapest
  job in the repo was the one I skipped.** ⚠ and the standalone script exits 0
  while printing `2 of 25 violated` unless invoked with `--check`.
  ⚠ **an instrument that names the wrong location prescribes the wrong fix**, and
  both readers took it: the collector's message said the name *"entered the
  CENTRAL registration"* when it spans the domain adapters too, so a move looked
  like the remedy for a contract a move cannot satisfy. Corrected to *"entered the
  rollback wire format"*.
  ⭐ **review item 2 was right too, and the prose was its cause.** Both

  comment above them read *"where a pickup is is the entire content of a pickup"* —
