  ⭐ **the question was worth asking once**: three instances made a pattern, and
  the pattern had two more members sitting in plain sight.

- ⛔ **G1 PICK 24b — history of the closed PICK 24, not open work.** See the note
  on 19b. Original text:** The pattern
  now has three instances, which makes "what else is in `scripts/` that nothing
  runs?" a question worth answering ONCE rather than discovering a fourth time.
  ⚠ compare `ls scripts/check_*.py` against `run_tests.py --list` and against
  `scripts/tests/`, and note that some are invoked BY a pytest wrapper rather
  than directly — absence from the plan is not proof nothing runs them.

- ✅ **G1 PICK 25: THE EXHAUSTIVE SWEEP'S ONE RED WAS A REAL REGRESSION**
  (`b88071ff0`, worktree agent). `external consumer: outlander` failed because
  `7446a76d7` made `maintain_local_session` install a session the moment a
  gameplay world exists — and `PlatformerApp::rollback(n)` explicitly does NOT
  start one, because a session rebases frame zero onto a world that must be
  CONSTRUCTED first. With a session already live, the caller's activate/settle
  ticks ADVANCED the simulation: ~5 ticks of falling the fixed-tick host never
  did, and the gate opened on **179 against 180**.
  ⭐ **the foreign-session rule could not save it** — it defers to a session
  somebody else started and cannot fire before that session EXISTS.
  `LocalSessionPolicy::autostart` covers exactly that window; a shipped app keeps
  autostarting because it has no other installer.
  ⭐ **and only the OUTSIDE fixture could see it.** Every in-workspace job was
  green, which is the entire argument for keeping the external consumers.
  ⚠ **a worktree agent's ABSENCE claims are scoped to its branch point** — this
  one reported `ambition_relativity2d` "is not in this tree", true where it stood
  and false on main. Verify absence claims against HEAD before acting on them.

- ✅ **G1 PICK 26: THE SILENT INPUT SEAM NOW SPEAKS** (`3d5c7dc6f`). The
  mechanism is exact rather than guessed: `publish_ggrs_input` OVERWRITES
  `ControlFrame` from the session's confirmed inputs every simulated frame, and
  those come from `PendingLocalInput` — so `ControlFrame` non-neutral while
  `PendingLocalInput` is neutral means somebody else wrote it and that write is
  about to be discarded. A CPU-only session, a driven harness and an ordinary
  player are all silent. Sustained 45 frames, said once.
  ⭐ **it publishes `InputSeamMisuse` rather than only logging**, and that came
  out of the test being wrong first: a log-only diagnostic forces a test to
  re-derive the predicate and assert on THAT, which passes just as happily when
  the system is never registered. Publishing the finding makes the SYSTEM the
  subject and lets a consumer ask without scraping stderr.
  ⚠ runs in `Update`, outside the rollback schedule, because its counter is a
  `Local`, a `Local` does not rewind, and a diagnostic that miscounts under
  resimulation would be reporting on itself.

- ✅ **G1 PICK 27: `ambition_content` DECLARES WHETHER ITS ACTORS SLEEP**
  (`80f880a8c`, worktree agent). 240+ staged actors surveyed by capability, and
  the stances are per-actor with reasons at the call site: `Never` for bosses
  (the phase machine IS the fight — and the brain tick that honours `Dormant`
  excludes bosses entirely, so a radius there would READ as enforced and not be),
  encounter mobs, driven bodies, the duel pair and the placed cast; the Hall's
  144 get `Never` **because it is a stress test** and a wake radius would delete
  the load it exists to apply. `AwakeNearObservers { 2560 }` for roaming
  hostiles.
  ⭐ **the radius derivation is better than mine was**: Ambition's observer top
  speed is not its RUN speed — the protagonist blinks, ~1056 px/s, about 4×
  `MAX_RUN_SPEED` — so 2.4s of lead is 2560, where deriving from the run speed
  would have given 648 and enemies snapping into motion in view. ⚠ and it stated
  that the largest enemy-bearing room is SMALLER than the radius, so it barely
  fires, rather than tuning it down to look busy: the radius is a fact about the
  observer, not about room size.

- ✅ **G1 PICK 28: AN OBSERVER IS A BODY SOMEBODY IS DRIVING** (`5f38e9675`).
  Found by PICK 27 and correctly NOT patched around content-side. `markers.rs`
  already states the rule — *"the controlled body is whichever entity carries
  `Brain::Player(…)` — during possession that is a DIFFERENT entity. Input,
  abilities, camera, portal viewer, and the melee lifecycle derive from"* it —
  and dormancy asked for `PlayerEntity`, so it was not on the list. Possess an
  actor, walk it from the parked avatar, and the body you are DRIVING falls
  asleep with its control frame retracted.
  ⚠ **three test fixtures were encoding the old definition**, spawning an
  observer as the marker ALONE — exactly the shape that cannot tell a parked
  avatar from a driven one.

- ⛔ **G1 PICK 26b — history of the closed PICK 26, not open work.** See the note
  on 19b. Original text:** From
  the roadmap's Task 8 status, which records it as *"the same class of defect one
  level up"* and queues it as Phase-6 leak #4 / D2b(b) — a row id that appears
  NOWHERE else in `docs/planning`, so the queue entry it points at does not
  exist. A fixed-tick host consumes the `ControlFrame` resource; a GGRS host
  consumes `PendingLocalInput`, because the frame it simulates is the one the
  session confirmed. Writing the wrong one is ignored without a word: the walk
  runs, the body never moves, nothing says why. Every in-repo caller happens to
  be on the right side, so no test can notice — and PICK 25 above is the same
  fixture catching the same class, which is evidence this is worth doing.
  ⚠ **the fix is probably NOT to unify them** (the GGRS frame must be the
  confirmed one) but to make the silent case LOUD, the way
  `draw_unclaimed_feature_views` does for an undrawn feature.

- ✅ **G1 PICK 30: THE LAYER-2 SILENCE** (`999622c9e`). `load_entity_sprites`
  dropped an unresolvable sprite with a bare `continue`; the feature then drew a
  colour fallback and the only way to learn which one went missing was to notice
  the picture. It reports a TALLY now — matching `character_sprites`' existing
  shape, because a headless fixture misses every sprite and forty warnings would
  train everyone to filter the channel meant to carry this.
  ⚠ **the probe NARROWED the claim, and that is the keeper.** I wrote that it
  catches a sprite that "did not resolve", then renamed an id to
  `chest_open_PROBE_MISSING` to watch it fire and nothing was reported: the
  catalog synthesizes a path from whatever id it is handed, so a bogus name still
  resolves and the asset server complains later, about a load, in its own words.
  What this branch catches is the catalog REFUSING an id. Verified silent on the
  real demo; **not** verified firing, and the comment says so.

- ◐ **G1 PICK 31: THE `#[ignore]` CENSUS — 13 total, 3 ROT, 0 unknown.** Ten are
  honest COST (audit listings and diagnostics that print and assert nothing).
  ⭐ **the structural finding beats the list** (`069882b78`): `--heavy` runs
  `--include-ignored`, and one of those diagnostics ended in an unconditional
  `panic!` — so **the only suite mode that executes ignored tests could never be
  green**, its output was noise, and that is the mechanism by which the rot
  survived. Fixed to print-and-pass like its three siblings.
  ⛔ **and removing the by-design panic exposed a REAL red under it**: that test
  now fails its own fixture assertion, *"the calibration lab must author a
  breakable named `calibration_brick` … Present: []"*, while the brick IS
  authored (`intro.ldtk`, `combat_calibration_lab`, a `BreakablePlatform` of that
  exact name). The three-way join over `FeatureId + FeatureName +
  BreakableFeature` yields nothing. ▢ **which of the three is missing is not
  chased** — its own row below.
  ▢ **the three ROT ignores, ranked:**
  1. ✅ **`dive_drill_lunges_through_the_targets` — FIXED AND UN-IGNORED**
     (`5a5c5cf1e`), and the stated reason was measurably false. It said the dive
     *"clips a 4-HP target by one point instead of killing it"*; one
     `--nocapture` run printed `target HP [20, 4] -> [20, 0]` — the target DIED.
     The defect was in the test: `enemy_hps` queried every `BodyHealth`
     INCLUDING THE PLAYER'S, so `after_alive == 0` demanded the player be dead
     while the assertion two lines above demands `resets == 0`. It could not
     pass. ⚠ the diagnosis was written in the same commit as the refactor it
     blamed, which is how a self-contradictory assertion became a "re-tune
     later" for 39 days. ⚠ **and the census's own hypothesis was wrong too** —
     it predicted the dive corridor had stopped applying damage; the printout the
     test already emitted settled it in one line.
     ⛔ **the original entry, kept:** in no ledger row at all,
     ignored 39 days ago INSIDE the refactor that plausibly broke it
     (`cc8e3c08f1`, which merged the player and actor combat components and then
     needed a `Without<PlayerEntity>` filter on `apply_feature_hit_events` —
     damage routing is exactly what moved). ⭐ **its own printout is the
     discriminator**: HP dropped but not to zero ⇒ the stated re-tune story;
     HP untouched ⇒ the dive corridor stopped applying damage, a code defect.
  2. `a_scripted_run_walks_…_and_finishes` (PICK 12) — half its cause is
     demonstrably fixed (the `power_block_0` lookup is gone), the stale route
     half stands.
  3. `she_plays_level_one_from_spawn_to_the_pole_and_it_replays` (PICK 12).
  ⚠ **do NOT un-ignore 2 and 3** — routes rot with the level; delete them when
  the fixture course's route lands.

- ✅ **G1 PICK 32: THE POPULATION SWEEP RUNS** (`aea87e84e`). Calibrate the
  INTACT world, then despawn, then walk with the ids already held — every
  consumer of `OracleTargets` was already an `.any()` or a `.find().map()` and
  tolerated an absent target. It reports all four variants now (intact,
  no_brick, no_switch, no_pickups: all CLEAN), which is a bisection tool the repo
  has had on paper and never once been able to run.
  ⚠ **I almost dropped a fixture guard doing it** — hoisting moved the *"booted
  with no live enemies … the melee-hit observation would be vacuous"* assertion
  out of the walk, which is the exact failure this file is a monument to,
  committed while fixing an instance of it. Restored on the main-oracle path;
  the sweep takes the other entry point and owns the risk, because removing
  populations is its job.
  ⭐ **with the dive drill, `app_it --include-ignored` is 298 passed / 0 failed /
  0 ignored.** The chain closes: a by-design panic made `--heavy` unreadable →
  ignored tests rotted behind it → one was a self-contradictory assertion blamed
  on an innocent refactor for 39 days, another was this sweep.
  ⛔ **the original diagnosis, kept:**
  ⭐ **DIAGNOSED, and it is not a missing component.** The spawn attaches all
  three (`spawn_static.rs:612-626` inserts `FeatureId`, `FeatureName` and
  `BreakableFeature` together), and `calibrate_targets` works fine on a fresh
  harness — `combat_equipment_switch_and_breakable_survive_forced_rollback_identically`
  calls it and passes. By elimination the `Present: []` comes from the
  **`no_brick` variant**, which despawns every `BreakableFeature` on purpose and
  then walks a route whose first act is to ASSERT the brick is present.
  ⛔ **so the two halves are incompatible by construction**: the sweep exists to
  ask *"does the divergence still need this class?"* and its route refuses to run
  without the class. `calibrate_targets`' fixture guard is RIGHT for the main
  oracle — a fixture's health belongs in its own assertion, which this ledger
  records as a three-instance lesson — and structurally wrong for a sweep that
  removes fixtures deliberately.
  ▢ **the shape of the fix**: calibrate on the INTACT world, then despawn, then
  walk with the recorded targets and let the walk tolerate a target that is now
  absent. That is a rework of the diagnostic, not a one-liner, which is why it is
  a row rather than an edit. ⚠ until then `--heavy` stays red on this one test —
  faithfully, which it was not before.

- ▢ **G1 PICK 31b: HOW MANY `#[ignore]`d TESTS ARE HIDING A RED?** Three surfaced
  today by accident — 1-1's two route tests (*"tuned to 1-1's old arrangement"*)
  and `she_plays_level_one_from_spawn_to_the_pole_and_it_replays`, whose ledger
  row was still claiming a workspace red that the ignore had silenced. An
  `#[ignore]` is the one suppression the suite reports as a PASS, so the census
  is worth taking once: every ignored test, its stated reason, and whether the
  reason is COST (legitimately expensive) or ROT (it would fail and nobody wants
  to look). ⚠ the second class is where a defect hides in plain sight.

- ✅ **G1 PICK 32b: THE ONE RED THE HEAVY MODE FOUND.** With `--heavy` readable
  and `app_it`'s ignored set green, `cargo test --workspace -- --include-ignored`
  reported exactly one failure across the whole workspace: the `consumer_source`
  doc example, ` ```ignore `, which does not compile (`cannot find value 'app'`).
  ⭐ it is the same shape as everything else this thread found — a suppression
  whose subject rotted behind it — and the smallest possible instance of it. The
  example now compiles (real `App`, real `AssetApp`, a literal authored root
  instead of the imaginary `my_game::my_asset_root()`) rather than being re-hidden.
  ⚠ **the other 26 ` ```ignore ` blocks in the workspace all passed** under
  `--include-ignored`, so this was rot in one doc, not a convention that rots.

---

## HEADLINE: the smash character select screen (Jon, 2026-08-05)

Spec kept verbatim in [`JONS_OBSERVATIONS_BUGS_AND_ISSUES.md`](JONS_OBSERVATIONS_BUGS_AND_ISSUES.md)
under *"◐ HEADLINE FEATURE, 2026-08-05"* — read it there, not from a paraphrase.

- ◐ **G1 PICK 33: A REAL CHARACTER SELECT SCREEN, IN THE SMASH DEMO.** A grid of
  character portraits across the top 65%, four participant slot cards across the
  bottom 35%, and ONE cursor — driven by arrows, a stick, or the mouse — that
  clicks, picks up and drags. **Nothing in the engine had to support it** (Jon's
  words), and nothing did: `game/ambition_demo_smash/src/select_screen/` is the
  whole feature, in three pieces that each know one thing.
  - ✅ **`layout.rs` is a pure function from the viewport to rectangles**, and
    this is the decision the rest fell out of. The obvious build is a flex tree
    that hit-tests by reading `ComputedNode` back out; that pays twice — a
    freshly spawned node reads a ZERO rect until `PostUpdate`, and the geometry
    then only exists under a renderer, which the demo's own headless app does not
    have. ⭐ deriving the rectangles and POSITIONING the nodes from them means
    **what you clicked and what you saw are the same numbers**, and the whole
    screen is unit-testable: no overlap, everything on screen at 640x360 through
    2340x1080, the 65/35 split checked where it is claimed.
  - ✅ **`cursor.rs` is a pointer over rectangles** with no Bevy in it. Arrows and
    the d-pad SNAP (`along + 2 × across`) because `MenuControlFrame`'s directions
    are just-pressed edges — there is no "held right" to integrate — and snapping
    is better on a pad anyway: every stop is on something clickable and a carried
    token lands on a portrait rather than between two. The mouse writes a
    position directly, and ⚠ only a MOVE counts, or a stationary mouse fights the
    arrows for the cursor every frame.
  - ✅ **`select.rs` still decides**, with Jon's model: a slot is Absent /
    Controller{device} / Cpu, and `cycle_occupant` SKIPS the controller rung when
    no unclaimed source is left — Jon's *"which must have a corresponding
    attached controller"* enforced in the value rather than checked in a widget.
    ⛔ `Controller` naming its DEVICE is the couch bug's cure in one field.
  - ✅ **START is a button now.** The old screen left the instant `ready()` went
    true, which is not how the real one works and made a decided lobby impossible
    to photograph — every attempt captured the match instead.
  - ⭐ **THE ROSTER IS A COMPOSITION FACT, and the engine taught me that by
    refusing.** The first draft declared its own `smash_mary_o`, `smash_sanic`,
    `smash_solid_snake` and `smash_super_sanic` on the sheets those characters
    already use, and the assembled catalog rejected all four on display-name
    uniqueness. That rule is right and the draft was wrong: **a crossover stage
    does not need copies of the cast, it needs the cast.** `SmashRoster` now
    resolves at `Startup` — the demo's own four, then every character whose
    catalog row carries the `smash` tag. Mary-O, Solid Snake, Sanic and Super
    Sanic opt in from their OWN demos' rows, one word each.
  - ⭐ **the portraits needed no engine work at all.** 141 characters already ship
    a generated `<stem>_portraits.png`, and `CharacterCatalog::portrait_ref` has
    derived the path from the sheet's name the whole time — with the dialogue box
    as its only consumer. This screen is the second, which is the only real test
    of whether that was a seam.
  - ✅ **the match returns to the select screen when it ends**, which Jon decided
    on 2026-07-29 (*"when the game ends it goes back to the character select
    screen"*), was recorded in `maintainer-decisions.md`, and was never built.
  - ✅ **LOOKED at it** (`capture_scene --route smash_select`, 1280x720), and the
    capture found three things reading did not:
    - **eight fighters wrapped 6 + 2**, because the column count was `min(n, 6)`.
      It balances now (8 → 4+4, 9 → 5+4) and a test says so.
    - **the first row sat under the shell's own Menu/Back buttons.** The title
      strip is 64px rather than 36 for exactly that reason, named at the constant.
    - ⛔ **`mary_o` drew a HOLE.** Her catalog row names
      `mary_o_v2_spritesheet.png`, `portrait_ref` derives
      `mary_o_v2_portraits.png` by convention, **and that file was never
      generated** — so the path resolves, the load fails, and the `ImageNode`
      renders nothing at all. ⭐ **a derived path is not a promise that the art
      exists**, and every layer below was silent about it. Every cell now carries
      its initials underneath, shown only where `get_load_state` reports a
      failure — so a missing portrait reads as missing ART rather than as a
      broken grid. ⚠ **the underlying gap is real and is NOT fixed**: Mary-O has
      no portrait sheet, and nothing in the pipeline notices.
  - ✅ **THE CENSUS WAS WORTH TAKING, and it was six characters and one cause.**
    Every one wore the `mary_o_v2` sheet family, named by FOUR surfaces — the
    Mary-O demo's three forms, `pocket_runner`, `twintrack_traveler`, and
    Ambition's own versus arena. ⛔ **the root cause was upstream of the art**:
    those three targets were in NO `regen_sprites.sh` batch at all, so their
    sheets existed only on machines that had once rendered them by hand. That is
    verbatim the class the script's own comment records for five Hall characters
    *"that previously depended on manually generated local assets and therefore
    disappeared on a fresh clone"*. They are in the tack-on batch now, with their
    portraits listed in `expected_files` beside their sheets — listing only the
    sheet would let the same half-publish pass the postcondition again.
    - ⭐ **the instrument beat the census.** A hand-rolled regex over the catalog
      RON found five and missed `arena_duelist_close`, which is declared in Rust
      inside `ambition_app`. `every_catalog_character_that_derives_a_portrait_has_the_art`
      asks the ASSEMBLED catalog — the same object the UI asks — and is the
      fourth member of `declared_art_resolves.rs`'s existing family, whose own
      header says the failure class is *"found by a player noticing something
      missing, not by a test"*. This one was found by looking at a screenshot.
    - ⚠ `inspect_hall_portraits.py` existed the whole time and could not have
      caught any of it: it reads ONE catalog file and filters to rows carrying a
      `hall_dialogue_id`, so a character outside the Hall — or declared by a
      demo's own Rust fragment — was never in its population. **A checker's
      POPULATION is the part that rots**, not its classification.
  - ✅ **JON'S ROSTER, 2026-08-05 (second pass).** *"I don't want copies of
    different character forms. So remove robot v2, remove fire mary-o, remove
    super sanic. Add alice, bob, oiler, pca, goblin, no-ether… make it easy to
    have the exact number of characters configurable. We may go more than 8."*
    Eleven fighters now, and the count is ONE ordered list — `select::SMASH_ROSTER`
    — filtered to the ids the assembled catalog carries. ⭐ that list REPLACED the
    `smash` tag: a tag is a second authority, letting a character opt in from its
    own crate with the demo having no say in the order or the count, and "easy to
    configure" has to mean a line count. The layout needed no number changed —
    11 balances to 6 + 5 on its own.
  - ⛔ **ALICE AND OILER DREW AS A STRIP OF EIGHT TINY FIGURES**, and only the
    capture said so. Their portrait sheets are **2048x320** — a
    `default`/`speaking`/`focused` clip set — and the screen was loading each PNG
    whole. Nine of eleven sheets are one 256x320 frame, which is exactly why it
    looked right. **A portrait sheet is a SHEET**, and the manifest beside it has
    said so all along; the screen takes the default clip's rect through
    `PortraitSheetRegistry` now, installing that plugin if nobody else has.
    ⚠ **this is the third defect in one feature that only a picture found**, after
    the 6+2 wrap and Mary-O's missing art.
  - ⛔ **`smash_george_booul` had a catalog row and no `CharacterDefinition`.**
    The file's own comment warns about it in so many words — *"REGISTER the
    characters, not only their catalog rows"* — and the tell was NOT a missing
    sprite: it was a stocks fighter that never seated, one layer away from where
    the mistake was.
  - ✅ **`Option<Res<CharacterCatalog>>` is forbidden** by
    `engine.character-authority-is-app-local`, caught by the workspace policy
    suite. Required now, and the rationale fits this screen exactly: an absent
    catalog would draw a grid of nameless plates that reads as missing ART rather
    than as a composition with no cast.
  - ▢ **the touch bezel covers P1's card** on a 1280x720 capture — the joystick
    circle over the first slot, `Select (F)` / `Select (Z)` over the fourth.
    ⚠ **not this screen's bug and possibly not a bug**: the bezel is gated on the
    `touch_controls_visible` SETTING rather than on the route, and its own doc
    says it doubles as an input display for keyboard and pad. But a select
    screen on a phone needs a drag surface, not a gameplay stick, and Jon's spec
    named *"arrows or game stick or mouse"* with no touch at all. So: recorded,
    out of scope, and the first thing to look at if this screen is ever wanted on
    a phone.

- ◐ **G1 PICK 34: THE PORTRAIT TARGET → ART RESOLVER** (queue Y″6). Jon decided
  it 2026-07-29 at HIGH confidence and it was never built: *"the resolver makes
  sense… a character portrait for a dialog box is ubiquitous for platformer 2d
  games, so it makes sense the engine has a mechanism to make it easy, although
  like most things with the engine, it should always be possible to ignore some
  part of it and roll your own."*
  - ⭐ **the select screen is why this is now cheap rather than speculative.**
    Building the grid made portraits a SECOND consumer of
    `CharacterCatalog::portrait_ref`, which had had exactly one (the dialogue
    box) for its whole life, and a seam with one consumer is a private detail
    wearing a seam's clothes.
  - ⚠ **`PortraitTarget` already exists** (`definition.rs`, a `Namespace` beside
    `SheetTarget`), `CharacterBindings.portraits` already exists, and
    `CharacterDefinition.portrait` is carried faithfully through preparation and
    read by NOTHING. What is missing is the middle: no target → art resolver, no
    `with_portrait` builder, and **no caller anywhere supplies a portrait
    vocabulary**, so the validation that does exist has never once run.
  - ⭐ **the target is already baked and then thrown away.** Every one of the 144
    portrait manifests carries `target: "<name>"`, and
    `PortraitSheetRegistry::from_baked_table` indexes by manifest PATH and
    discards it. Half the resolver is recovering a field that is already there.
  - ⛔ **what it must not become**, per the field's own doc: a copy of the
    catalog's concrete paths. Two places declaring the same art is the split this
    campaign exists to remove. The catalog road stays the fall-through, which is
    what "possible to ignore" means here.
  - ⚠ **do NOT auto-supply the vocabulary in `try_register_character`** the way
    the sheet road does. The sheet vocabulary is a pure `OnceLock`; the portrait
    registry is a Bevy resource that may be absent or deliberately replaced, and
    reaching for a baked global inside registration would make one definition
    prepare differently in the smash app than in the host.

- ✅ **G1 PICK 35: TWENTY-SIX CHARACTERS DID NOT REGENERATE.** Jon, told that
  George Booul's art is in no batch: *"Are there other characters that don't
  regen? If there are we need to fix that."* There were **26** — every catalog
  sheet stem `regen_sprites.sh` never names, cross-checked against
  `sprite2d_renderer list`, and **all 26 are registered targets that simply
  nothing ran**. Almost all are one cast: the Hall's mathematician and scientist
  NPCs (Data Lovelace, Marie Curry, Hypatia Prime, Paul Diracula, Yuclid, Georg
  Canter…), plus George Booul and BOTH robot-lineage sheets.
  - ⛔ **generated art is gitignored**, so every one of them existed only on
    machines that had once rendered it. This is the THIRD time this class has
    been found and the first two were also accidents — five Hall characters
    recorded in the script's own comment, then `mary_o_v2`, then these.
  - ✅ **the durable fix is a check, not a longer list.**
    `scripts/tests/test_every_character_regenerates.py` compares the assembled
    cast against what the script publishes, in the 11-second repo-tooling job. A
    hand-maintained allowlist orphans every character added after the last audit,
    and the failure is invisible to whoever added it. ⚠ probed: it reported the
    26, then the 5 unit-test fixtures, then nothing.
  - ⚠ two censuses were WRONG before this one was right: a first pass counted a
    stem named only in a COMMENT as covered (`player_robot_v3` read as fine), and
    a second used a `\b`-style boundary that missed `<stem>_spritesheet`. Both
    over- and under-reported. The check carries both lessons in its own code.

- ✅ **G1 PICK 36: PLAYER ONE COULD ONLY EVER BE THE STAGE'S STARTING
  CHARACTER**, and picking anybody else seated NOBODY.
  - ⚠ **it presented as "George Booul cannot be seated"** and was nothing to do
    with him. He was simply the first fighter on the grid that was not
    `SMASH_CHARACTER_ID`. Any of the twelve would have done it.
  - ⛔ **the mechanism**: seating ADOPTS the primary player's existing body
    rather than spawning a second one — right, and what stopped two Mary-Os
    appearing in the arena — and if seat 0's character disagrees with what that
    body WEARS it returns. That `return` leaves the whole system, so one seat
    disagreeing seats every other participant too, every tick, forever.
  - ⛔ **and it was SILENT.** The roster published, the route reached the stage,
    no `MatchSeatingRefused` was recorded, nothing was logged, and the stage came
    up empty. Three lines below, an unknown brain profile refuses the roster out
    loud with a recorded reason — its own comment says why: *"the per-seat
    `debug_assert!` this replaced was invisible in release."* The loud path was
    built for exactly this class and this arm was left quiet.
  - ✅ **two fixes.** The engine now WARNS with both ids and what to do about it
    (throttled per pair, so a retry loop says it once). The demo re-dresses the
    primary body as seat 0's pick — which is the reconciliation the seating
    comment itself names, *"that is `WornCharacter`'s job"* — because a
    `StartingCharacter` is decided at PREPARATION and a select screen decides
    seat 0 afterwards.
  - ⭐ the stage test now seats **George Booul at slot 0 on purpose**, so the
    exact combination that was impossible is the one it pins.

- ◐ **G1 PICK 37: SEVEN OF TWELVE GRID FIGHTERS CANNOT THROW A PUNCH.** Jon,
  2026-08-05: *"We might need to generate real smash movesets for the characters
  if they are missing them."* He was right, and it is now MEASURED rather than
  assumed — `smash_roster_movesets.rs` reports every grid fighter's resolved
  action set:

```text
  player_robot_v3              ambition::peaceful        melee=NONE
  smash_george_booul           smash::duelist            Swipe  4 dmg  34px
  mary_o                       mary_o::peaceful          melee=NONE
  sanic                        sanic::peaceful           melee=NONE
  npc_pirate_admiral           ambition::pirate_pistol   Swipe  1 dmg  28px
  npc_ninja_shadow_oni_leader  ambition::striker_swipe   Swipe  1 dmg  28px
  npc_alice                    ambition::peaceful        melee=NONE
  npc_bob                      ambition::peaceful        melee=NONE
  npc_oiler                    ambition::peaceful        melee=NONE
  perfect_cellular_automaton   ambition::striker_swipe   Swipe  1 dmg  28px
  goblin                       ambition::striker_swipe   Swipe  1 dmg  28px
  npc_noether                  ambition::peaceful        melee=NONE
```

  - ⛔ **seven have no melee at all** and would arrive on a platform-fighter
    stage unable to hit anybody — including Mary-O, Sanic and the player robot.
    All seven resolve to a `peaceful` preset, because standing in a room and
    talking is what a Hall NPC was authored for.
  - ⚠ **and the five that CAN hit disagree by 4x on damage**, because two are a
    demo's fighter (`smash::duelist`, 4 dmg / 34px) and three are an adventure
    game's enemies (1 dmg / 28px). So this is not "author seven movesets" — it
    is "a platform fighter wants one authored kit per character", which is the
    job Jon named.
  - ⭐ **the ability/moveset split is the seam to keep.** `SmashSelect::roster`
    already levels the ABILITY set so nobody is stronger for coming from another
    demo — an ability is *may this body attack*; a moveset is *what the attack
    IS*, and it comes from the character's own catalog row. Levelling the second
    the way the first is levelled would erase the character; authoring it is the
    work.
  - ▢ the guard asserts the SET of unarmed fighters, so it fires both when the
    grid grows a fighter nobody armed and when one is armed and the list is not
    updated.


- ◐ **G1 PICK 38: A MATCH MAY DECLARE A FIGHTER'S KIT.** The seam PICK 37's
  measurement asks for. Seven of twelve grid fighters have no melee because a
  moveset comes from the character's own catalog row, which was authored for the
  game that character belongs to — Alice's `peaceful` preset is correct in the
  Hall and wrong on a stage.
  - ⭐ **the roster already does this one level up.** `fighter_abilities` is a
    match rule that overrides what a body brought, and its doc says exactly why
    it lives there: *"It is a rule of the MATCH rather than something seating
    decides… the engine does not get an opinion about what a fighter may do."*
    A moveset is the same statement one level finer — per SEAT rather than per
    match, because what distinguishes characters is precisely the thing a
    per-match override would erase.
  - ▢ `MatchParticipant.action_set: Option<ActionSet>`, applied at seating
    beside the ability set. `None` keeps the catalog default, which is every
    existing roster and every scripted encounter.
  - ⚠ **a shared FLOOR is not the goal, it is the first fill.** Giving all twelve
    one kit makes the grid playable and is honestly a levelling; per-character
    kits are the content job Jon named, and the seam is what makes them
    authorable without editing a character's home row.

- ✅ **G1 PICK 39: AN INVISIBLE BLOCK IS AN INVISIBLE FLOOR.** Jon, 2026-08-05:
  *"you should not be able to stand on an invisible block."* A
  `MaryOBlockLook::Hidden` block is drawn transparent and is still
  `BlockKind::Solid`, so it holds her up before she has found it.
  - ⭐ **the engine has HALF this vocabulary already.** `BlockKind::OneWay` is
    *"Landing platform: only solid when the player crosses from above."* A hidden
    block is the MIRROR — solid only when struck from below — and both belong in
    the same predicate. `collision_semantics.rs` is one file with exhaustive
    matches, so a new kind names every site that has to decide rather than
    letting one quietly default.
  - ⛔ **not "make it non-solid".** The bonk is a `ContactKind::Head` contact
    produced by the collision system, so a block with no collision cannot be
    struck at all — removing the floor would remove the reward with it. That is
    the trap this row exists to write down.
  - ✅ `BlockKind::BonkOnly` landed, and the exhaustive matches did the work they
    exist for: **eight** sites named themselves, across collision, blink,
    perception, room labels, art resolution and three renderers. Each one is a
    decision written down rather than a default — perception does not model it as
    terrain (a patrol routed over it would fall), the in-game fill is fully
    TRANSPARENT (a tint would announce the secret) while the developer overlay
    and the geometry diagram both draw it (that is what those views are for).
  - ⚠ **it is pass-through FOREVER, including after it has been struck.** In the
    source material a revealed block becomes a solid platform, and that needs the
    room's geometry to change at runtime — which is rollback-relevant state and a
    bigger question than this. Jon's report is satisfied (you cannot stand on
    what you have not found); the platform half is not, and is the follow-on.

## GPT 5.6 review through `d7d7955` — triage (2026-08-05)

Ten items. Verdicts below; each ACCEPTED one is a row, each REFUSED one says no
without hedging, each DEFERRED one says what it is waiting on.

- ✅ **#6 DONE (2026-08-05), and it was my code.** *"Do not use a quasar as a fallback error
  value."* Correct: `pickup_reward` and `next_rung_toward` both answer
  `quasar_reward()` for `MaryOPickup::Coin`, a branch their own comments admit is
  routed away before it can be reached. **A caller that forgets the filter
  silently spawns the most powerful reward in the game.** The comment even argues
  for it — *"answering the quasar keeps this total rather than inventing a 'no
  reward' state"* — which is the totality argument used to hide an impossibility.
  `Option<PowerReward>` is the honest shape and it deletes the duplicated coin
  routing at the call sites.
- ✅ **#1 DONE (2026-08-05), verified first.** 1-2 really does author a `Brick`
  holding `AlwaysWand`, and the wand really does share the beacon's exclusive
  slot — so fire Mary-O really did come out tall. `without_downgrading` pays
  coins instead; the block still spends, flinches and changes art.
  ⤷ original triage: A wand downgrading fire Mary-O to tall
  is a real regression if it reproduces; the rule (`small < tall < fire`,
  monotonic) belongs in Mary-O, not in the generic equipment engine, and the
  review is right about that placement. ⚠ verify FIRST — this reviewer's previous
  round had confirmed and unconfirmed items mixed.
- ✅ **#5 DONE (2026-08-05).** `MaryOBlockLook::Quasar` is retired. It was the
  split's own counter-example living inside the split: it dressed to
  `BonusBlockTile`, exactly like `Question`, so the only thing it said that
  `Question` did not was what came out — the other field's job. The two blocks in
  1-1 that authored it now say `kind: Question, contents: AlwaysQuasar`, migrated
  through `entity set-field` (⛔ never `json.dumps` a `.ldtk`) with the editor's
  formatting intact and `doctor` green. A level still saying `Quasar` is refused
  at load with the replacement named in the message. ⚠ the migration test used to
  assert a LOOK existed, which would have gone green forever the moment the
  variant was deleted — it now asserts the level still pays a quasar, and I
  probed that by pointing it at the wrong pickup.
- ▢ **#2, #3, #4 DEFERRED, not refused.** Authorable victory destinations,
  cross-room pipe destinations and dropdown-backed enum fields are all the right
  direction and all sizeable authoring-tool work. They belong to the LDtk
  migration's own arc rather than to a review pass. ⚠ #4 in particular is
  `ambition_ldtk_tools` work with a schema-sync contract to respect.
- ▢ **#7 AGREED, and already true.** *"Do not encode the Mary-O progression graph
  as authored data."* It is not encoded as data; `next_rung_toward` is Rust and
  the block authors a semantic contents. Recorded so a later pass does not
  "helpfully" move it.
- ▢ **#8 AGREED as direction.** LDtk as the durable source with the Rust
  coordinate arrays deleted as each family lands. Already the migration's stated
  endpoint.
- ▢ **#9 ALREADY OPEN and correctly described.** The roster/topology/session
  ordering. ⚠ the review's own caution is the important half — *"do not repair
  this by restarting a session after topology changes"* — and it matches what
  this repo already learned about frozen seating.
- ▢ **#10 ALREADY OPEN.** Sanic's ability fallback and the process-global item
  catalog. Both tracked.

## SR-3 causal-pursuit overlay — triage (2026-08-05)

`untracked/ambition-twintrack-sr-causal-pursuit-overlay-2026-08-05-1824.zip`, the
third SR overlay from GPT 5.6. 22 files: a physics core addition, two new
`ambition_relativity2d` read models, a 748-line observatory viewport, and the
demo's pursuit phase. **Taken, with five defects fixed on the way in.**

- ✅ **the physics is the substance and it is good.** `observe_photon_direction`
  (aberration + Doppler + a documented `D^3` beaming proxy), its inverse,
  proper↔coordinate velocity, and `solve_null_intercept_constant_velocity`
  (`|r + vt| = ct`, earliest future root, timelike targets only). Pure functions,
  unit-tested, no Bevy. ⭐ it deliberately avoids `tanh` and says why — the
  overlay respects ADR 0023 rather than needing to be told.
- ✅ **the acceptance test is real.** The scripted participant reads the published
  intercept direction, fires, and the run asserts a hit on the beacon's receiver
  — not just that a number was published.
- ⛔ **it reverted `edad6a5e9` a SECOND time.** The traveler's two `transit_body`
  calls went back to bare `body.pos = ` / `body.vel = ` writes, comments and all,
  and `engine.pose-writes-are-authority-only` caught it. **The lesson is about
  overlays, not about that line**: each one is cut against the tree as it stood
  when the previous one shipped, so every fix it predates has to be re-applied by
  hand. Restored, and the comment now records both reversions.
- ⛔ **B0001 in the shipped host.** `update_observatory_targeting` took two `&mut`
  queries over `Sprite`/`Transform`/`Visibility` with no `Without` — a runtime
  parameter panic, in `ambition_app`, costing four of its tests. Bevy does not
  infer disjointness from a marker component.
- ⛔ **the observatory camera erased the laboratory.** `ClearColorConfig::Custom`
  on an `order: 4` camera clears the WHOLE attachment, not its viewport — the
  scissor rect does not bound a load op. The first capture was a panel floating
  on a black rectangle where the experiment used to be. It now paints its own
  backdrop and clears nothing.
- ⛔ **half the panel had never drawn.** The camera sat at Bevy's default
  `z = 1000` with `default_2d()`'s `near = -1000 .. far = 1000`, so its visible
  range was `0 ..= 2000` — and the sky disk and spacetime-diagram backing are at
  negative z. Camera moved to `z = 0`.
- ⛔ **the panel layout asked the WINDOW how big it was**, so it collapsed
  whenever the game rendered to a texture. Both split systems now read
  `Camera::physical_target_size`, which is also what made a capture possible.
- ⚠ **the beacon's prescribed worldline is its own file + a scoped waiver.**
  `chase_beacon.rs` writes `body.pos` analytically every tick; the entity has no
  `MotionModel` and no contact clusters, so there is nothing for `transit_body`
  to reconcile and it cannot be called at all. The skip path names THAT FILE, not
  the demo — `lib.rs` moves the traveler and stays covered.
- ⭐ **`capture_twintrack` is new, and it found three of the five.** The third
  caller of `ambition_render::capture`, plus the one edge the shared adoption
  cannot know: the observatory camera is neither `MainCamera` nor
  `FrontHudCamera`.
- ✅ **FIXED (2026-08-05), and it was the RENDERER, not TwinTrack.** Four declared
  slots piled into the top-left as unreadable mush. `place_declared_hud` advanced
  its stack by `font_size + gap` — ONE line — whatever the slot was showing, so
  any game publishing a multi-line readout had the next slot drawn through its
  own text. Counting `\n` fixed the explicit breaks and left the WRAPPED ones
  colliding, so placement now reads `ComputedNode` — last frame's real height, in
  logical px — and falls back to the line estimate before the first layout. ⚠ the
  gauge bar had the same one-line assumption and sat through the middle of a tall
  readout; same derivation. Probed by restoring the one-line advance.

  v3 is *supposed* to be chibi, so "make everyone the same height" is the wrong
  rule. What is needed first is a MEASUREMENT: the baked body height per hall
  character, next to the height its target intends. That is the same shape as
  `enemy_quad_matches_its_box` and it can move.

  no longer exists. Harmless — the table matches dialogue node keys, and the raid

  lines as a live character. Worth one look at what `exact` actually matches
  before deleting them; a wrong deletion mutes a scene.
- ✅ **DONE (2026-08-05): the mirror exists** —
  `ambition_app/tests/rendered_identities_are_registered.rs`. It asks the

  population reported four real characters as orphans on the first pass. Ten ids
  are waived in a TABLE rather than a match, so each excuse is falsifiable three
  ways: the id is still declared by a target, it is still unregistered, and no

  that DECLARES a `character_id` nobody registers is art the game can never

- ✅ **DONE (2026-08-05).** The cause was `diagram_position` clamping its x

  characters did not exist. `scripts/tests/test_catalog_rows_are_in_the_characters_map.py`
  asks the question from the file: a row carrying `display_name` and
  `spritesheet` is a character row and belongs in exactly one map. Structure, not

  rather than writing to the tracked catalog.
- ▢ **Three renderer targets declare an id the game does not use.** `sanic.py`
  says `npc_sanic`, `solid_snake.py` says `npc_solid_snake`, `player_robot_v3.py`
  says `player`. All three bodies ARE in the game, registered by their providers
  under different ids — so the metadata lies, and the mirror guard has to waive
  three rows that are not really orphans. ⚠ the fix is in the NESTED renderer
  checkout, which is Jon's tree; a row here rather than an edit there.
- ✅ **DONE (2026-08-05), and the question turned out to be the other way round.**
  All 48 exact keys are redundant *today* — every one resolves to its own cue
  through its own alias — so the column looked like dead weight. It is not: `exact`
  is consulted BEFORE `aliases`, so an exact key silently MASKS a collision. If
  one cue's alias is a substring of another cue's authored name, the authored
  name still resolves (through the exact map) while every undeclared speaker
  shaped like it goes to the wrong voice, and the existing test cannot see it
  because it only asks about keys the exact map already answers.
  `no_exact_key_is_masking_an_alias_that_would_steal_it` asks with the exact map
  REMOVED. Probed with a two-letter alias (`ob`), which stole `robot` and
  `goblin_chieftain` on the spot. ⚠ its message says what NOT to do: a failure
  means two cues share vocabulary, not that somebody should add an exact key —
  adding one is what hides it.
- ✅ **DONE (2026-08-05) — by narrowing the READOUTS, after the obvious answer was
  refuted.** The three wide slots now break into lines that end well left of the
  panel, and a capture shows the corner fully readable. Multi-line is only safe
  because `place_declared_hud` measures a slot's real height as of the same day;
  before that, breaking a line would have had the next slot drawn through it.
  ⤷ the refutation, kept because it is the part worth remembering: The readouts are a full-width overlay and the panel is a
  right-hand viewport, so at 1280x720 the status line's tail disappears behind
  it. This row used to say the fix was probably a reserved `SurroundRegion`;
  switching the demo to `profiles::fixed_four_by_three()` and recapturing changed
  **nothing** — the pillarboxes a 4:3 rect leaves on 16:9 are ~160px wide and
  these readouts are far wider than that, so `select_hud_region`'s `fits` test
  rejects every region and drops them back to the overlay corner, which is
  exactly where they already were. **The width is the problem, not the
  placement**: either TwinTrack publishes narrower readouts (three short slots
  instead of one wide one), or the panel yields horizontal room. Whoever takes
  this should start from `select_hud_region`'s `min_px` test, not from the
  profile.

  replacement rather than a second character, and the question is whether the
  cast row should point AT it. One line from him settles it either way; do not
  guess, because both readings are plausible and only one is reversible cheaply.
- ✅ **DONE (2026-08-05): the warning is where a cleaner will stand.** The boss
  block in `regen_sprites.sh` now says that `gnu_ton_boss_body` / `_hands` are
  assembled by SUFFIX at load while the encounter reads the sibling `giant_gnu_*`
  family by literal — both published, both read — and repeats the rule the
  `super_mary_o` near-miss taught: **a target is not its sheets, and a sheet
  nobody spells is not unused.** ⚠ verified before writing: both file families
  really are present, so the `expected_files` entries resolve and the
  postcondition was never lying. That check was worth making — the row assumed a

- ✅ **DECIDED and DONE (2026-08-05): pinned, not taught.** Parsing spec
  constructors means teaching the scan every new spec SHAPE — a maintenance tail
  for a guard nobody is paid to keep. Instead `COMPUTED_ID_TARGETS = 9` pins how
  many targets carry a `character_id` the scan CANNOT read, and names them when
  it changes. The next family built that way fails the ratchet and somebody looks
  by hand, which is the actual question the snakes needed asked. Probed by
  lowering the count.

- ▢ **The flying-swarm ENEMY SHAPE does not exist yet**, and it is the blocker

  pairs every `hall_dialogue_id: Some(..)` with a `title:` node, in both

### Refilled 2026-08-05, last

- ✅ **SWEPT (2026-08-05), and it found nothing — which is the useful answer.**
  Every `assert!(x.is_empty())` in `crates/` was read: they are unit assertions
  with an ADJACENT POSITIVE check in the same test (`edges.edge("charge")` beside
  `unroutable.is_empty()`; `pending` contains `"new_art"` beside
  `failed.is_empty()`), so a helper that broke to always-empty fails the
  neighbour. That adjacency is what makes them falsifiable, and it is precisely
  what a CENSUS lacks: one predicate, one population, one emptiness claim, and
  nothing else in the test to disagree. `declared_art_resolves` was the only one
  of that shape. ⚠ recorded so nobody re-greps it — a sweep that finds nothing is
  a result, and an unrecorded one gets repeated.
- ✅ **DONE (2026-08-05), and the MEASUREMENT chose the rule.** A hall node is
  written by copying a neighbour and rewriting the lines, so the failure is a
  node that resolves perfectly and opens as somebody else — id paired, art
  resolved, a line found, every check green.
  ⚠ **an exact `display_name == speaker` rule reports 28 of 124 pedestals**, and
  every one is a shortening a writer would defend: *Fretjaw, Cantina Chieftain*
  speaks as `Fretjaw`, *Emmy No-Ether* as `Emmy`, *Pirate Admiral* as `Admiral`.
  Sharing ONE word of three-plus letters passes all 28 and still catches an
  unrelated speaker. Measured both rules before writing either.
  ⭐ the poison plants both cases — a stranger's node AND a legitimately
  shortened one — so the guard cannot be tightened back into noise without
  failing.
- ✅ **DONE (2026-08-05): `capture_scene --fit-room`.** The whole room, edge to
  edge, at any output size. It BYPASSES the camera snapshot rather than feeding
  it a wide focus — that resolver clamps to the room and to camera zones, which
  is right for gameplay and wrong for a portrait of a room.
  ⛔ **the first version framed the hall at a fifth of the image**: it computed
  `scale = max(room / base_view)`, and `scale` multiplies an extent that depends
  on the projection's MODE and the viewport's aspect, so that arithmetic only
  holds when those agree. Asking for a `ScalingMode::AutoMin` of exactly the
  room's size is the same request with nothing to get wrong.
  ⭐ and the picture earns it immediately: Jon's *"dramatically out of scale"* is
  unmissable with the whole cast in one frame, where the report can only rank
  them.
